The methods that add, subtract, or"}, {"line": 19, "text": "rearrange their members in place, and don't return a specific item, never return"}, {"line": 20, "text": "the collection instance itself but ``None``."}, {"line": 21, "text": ""}, {"line": 22, "text": "Some operations are supported by several object types; in particular,"}, {"line": 23, "text": "practically all objects can be compared for equality, tested for truth"}, {"line": 24, "text": "value, and converted to a string (with the :func:`repr` function or the"}, {"line": 25, "text": "slightly different :func:`str` function).  The latter function is implicitly"}, {"line": 26, "text": "used when an object is written by the :func:`print` function."}, {"line": 27, "text": ""}, {"line": 28, "text": ""}, {"line": 29, "text": ".. _truth:"}, {"line": 30, "text": ""}, {"line": 31, "text": "Truth Value Testing"}, {"line": 32, "text": "==================="}, {"line": 33, "text": ""}, {"line": 34, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 35, "text": "   pair: statement; if"}, {"line": 36, "text": "   pair: statement; while"}, {"line": 37, "text": "   pair: truth; value"}, {"line": 38, "text": "   pair: Boolean; operations"}, {"line": 39, "text": "   single: false"}, {"line": 40, "text": ""}, {"line": 41, "text": "Any object can be tested for truth value, for use in an :keyword:`if` or"}, {"line": 42, "text": ":keyword:`while` condition or as operand of the Boolean operations below."}, {"line": 43, "text": ""}, {"line": 44, "text": ".. index:: single: true"}, {"line": 45, "text": ""}, {"line": 46, "text": "By default, an object is considered true unless its class defines either a"}, {"line": 47, "text": ":meth:`~object.__bool__` method that returns ``False`` or a"}, {"line": 48, "text": ":meth:`~object.__len__` method that"}, {"line": 49, "text": "returns zero, when called with the object. [1]_ If one of the methods raises an"}, {"line": 50, "text": "exception when called, the exception is propagated and the object does"}, {"line": 51, "text": "not have a truth value (for example, :data:`NotImplemented`)."}, {"line": 52, "text": "Here are most of the built-in objects considered false:"}, {"line": 53, "text": ""}, {"line": 54, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 55, "text": "   single: None (Built-in object)"}, {"line": 56, "text": "   single: False (Built-in object)"}, {"line": 57, "text": ""}, {"line": 58, "text": "* constants defined to be false: ``None`` and ``False``"}, {"line": 59, "text": ""}, {"line": 60, "text": "* zero of any numeric type: ``0``, ``0.0``, ``0j``, ``Decimal(0)``,"}, {"line": 61, "text": "  ``Fraction(0, 1)``"}, {"line": 62, "text": ""}, {"line": 63, "text": "* empty sequences and collections: ``''``, ``()``, ``[]``, ``{}``, ``set()``,"}, {"line": 64, "text": "  ``range(0)``"}, {"line": 65, "text": ""}, {"line": 66, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 67, "text": "   pair: operator; or"}, {"line": 68, "text": "   pair: operator; and"}, {"line": 69, "text": "   single: False"}, {"line": 70, "text": "   single: True"}, {"line": 71, "text": ""}, {"line": 72, "text": "Operations and built-in functions that have a Boolean result always return ``0``"}, {"line": 73, "text": "or ``False`` for false and ``1`` or ``True`` for true, unless otherwise stated."}, {"line": 74, "text": "(Important exception: the Boolean operations ``or`` and ``and`` always return"}, {"line": 75, "text": "one of their operands.)"}, {"line": 76, "text": ""}, {"line": 77, "text": ""}, {"line": 78, "text": ".. _boolean:"}, {"line": 79, "text": ""}, {"line": 80, "text": "Boolean Operations --- :keyword:`!and`, :keyword:`!or`, :keyword:`!not`"}, {"line": 81, "text": "======================================================================="}, {"line": 82, "text": ""}, {"line": 83, "text": ".. index:: pair: Boolean; operations"}, {"line": 84, "text": ""}, {"line": 85, "text": "These are the Boolean operations, ordered by ascending priority:"}, {"line": 86, "text": ""}, {"line": 87, "text": "+-------------+---------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 88, "text": "| Operation   | Result                          | Notes |"}, {"line": 89, "text": "+=============+=================================+=======+"}, {"line": 90, "text": "| ``x or y``  | if *x* is true, then *x*, else  | \\(1)  |"}, {"line": 91, "text": "|             | *y*                             |       |"}, {"line": 92, "text": "+-------------+---------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 93, "text": "| ``x and y`` | if *x* is false, then *x*, else | \\(2)  |"}, {"line": 94, "text": "|             | *y*                             |       |"}, {"line": 95, "text": "+-------------+---------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 96, "text": "| ``not x``   | if *x* is false, then ``True``, | \\(3)  |"}, {"line": 97, "text": "|             | else ``False``                  |       |"}, {"line": 98, "text": "+-------------+---------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 99, "text": ""}, {"line": 100, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 101, "text": "   pair: operator; and"}, {"line": 102, "text": "   pair: operator; or"}, {"line": 103, "text": "   pair: operator; not"}, {"line": 104, "text": ""}, {"line": 105, "text": "Notes:"}, {"line": 106, "text": ""}, {"line": 107, "text": "(1)"}, {"line": 108, "text": "   This is a short-circuit operator, so it only evaluates the second"}, {"line": 109, "text": "   argument if the first one is false."}, {"line": 110, "text": ""}, {"line": 111, "text": "(2)"}, {"line": 112, "text": "   This is a short-circuit operator, so it only evaluates the second"}, {"line": 113, "text": "   argument if the first one is true."}, {"line": 114, "text": ""}, {"line": 115, "text": "(3)"}, {"line": 116, "text": "   ``not`` has a lower priority than non-Boolean operators, so ``not a == b`` is"}, {"line": 117, "text": "   interpreted as ``not (a == b)``, and ``a == not b`` is a syntax error."}, {"line": 118, "text": ""}, {"line": 119, "text": ""}, {"line": 120, "text": ".. _stdcomparisons:"}, {"line": 121, "text": ""}, {"line": 122, "text": "Comparisons"}, {"line": 123, "text": "==========="}, {"line": 124, "text": ""}, {"line": 125, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 126, "text": "   pair: chaining; comparisons"}, {"line": 127, "text": "   pair: operator; comparison"}, {"line": 128, "text": "   pair: operator; =="}, {"line": 129, "text": "   pair: operator; < (less)"}, {"line": 130, "text": "   pair: operator; <="}, {"line": 131, "text": "   pair: operator; > (greater)"}, {"line": 132, "text": "   pair: operator; >="}, {"line": 133, "text": "   pair: operator; !="}, {"line": 134, "text": "   pair: operator; is"}, {"line": 135, "text": "   pair: operator; is not"}, {"line": 136, "text": ""}, {"line": 137, "text": "There are eight comparison operations in Python.  They all have the same"}, {"line": 138, "text": "priority (which is higher than that of the Boolean operations).  Comparisons can"}, {"line": 139, "text": "be chained arbitrarily; for example, ``x < y <= z`` is equivalent to ``x < y and"}, {"line": 140, "text": "y <= z``, except that *y* is evaluated only once (but in both cases *z* is not"}, {"line": 141, "text": "evaluated at all when ``x < y`` is found to be false)."}, {"line": 142, "text": ""}, {"line": 143, "text": "This table summarizes the comparison operations:"}, {"line": 144, "text": ""}, {"line": 145, "text": "+------------+-------------------------+"}, {"line": 146, "text": "| Operation  | Meaning                 |"}, {"line": 147, "text": "+============+=========================+"}, {"line": 148, "text": "| ``<``      | strictly less than      |"}, {"line": 149, "text": "+------------+-------------------------+"}, {"line": 150, "text": "| ``<=``     | less than or equal      |"}, {"line": 151, "text": "+------------+-------------------------+"}, {"line": 152, "text": "| ``>``      | strictly greater than   |"}, {"line": 153, "text": "+------------+-------------------------+"}, {"line": 154, "text": "| ``>=``     | greater than or equal   |"}, {"line": 155, "text": "+------------+-------------------------+"}, {"line": 156, "text": "| ``==``     | equal                   |"}, {"line": 157, "text": "+------------+-------------------------+"}, {"line": 158, "text": "| ``!=``     | not equal               |"}, {"line": 159, "text": "+------------+-------------------------+"}, {"line": 160, "text": "| ``is``     | object identity         |"}, {"line": 161, "text": "+------------+-------------------------+"}, {"line": 162, "text": "| ``is not`` | negated object identity |"}, {"line": 163, "text": "+------------+-------------------------+"}, {"line": 164, "text": ""}, {"line": 165, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 166, "text": "   pair: object; numeric"}, {"line": 167, "text": "   pair: objects; comparing"}, {"line": 168, "text": ""}, {"line": 169, "text": "Unless stated otherwise, objects of different types never compare equal."}, {"line": 170, "text": "The ``==`` operator is always defined but for some object types (for example,"}, {"line": 171, "text": "class objects) is equivalent to :keyword:`is`. The ``<``, ``<=``, ``>`` and ``>=``"}, {"line": 172, "text": "operators are only defined where they make sense; for example, they raise a"}, {"line": 173, "text": ":exc:`TypeError` exception when one of the arguments is a complex number."}, {"line": 174, "text": ""}, {"line": 175, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 176, "text": "   single: __eq__() (instance method)"}, {"line": 177, "text": "   single: __ne__() (instance method)"}, {"line": 178, "text": "   single: __lt__() (instance method)"}, {"line": 179, "text": "   single: __le__() (instance method)"}, {"line": 180, "text": "   single: __gt__() (instance method)"}, {"line": 181, "text": "   single: __ge__() (instance method)"}, {"line": 182, "text": ""}, {"line": 183, "text": "Non-identical instances of a class normally compare as non-equal unless the"}, {"line": 184, "text": "class defines the :meth:`~object.__eq__` method."}, {"line": 185, "text": ""}, {"line": 186, "text": "Instances of a class cannot be ordered with respect to other instances of the"}, {"line": 187, "text": "same class, or other types of object, unless the class defines enough of the"}, {"line": 188, "text": "methods :meth:`~object.__lt__`, :meth:`~object.__le__`, :meth:`~object.__gt__`, and"}, {"line": 189, "text": ":meth:`~object.__ge__` (in general, :meth:`~object.__lt__` and"}, {"line": 190, "text": ":meth:`~object.__eq__` are sufficient, if you want the conventional meanings of the"}, {"line": 191, "text": "comparison operators)."}, {"line": 192, "text": ""}, {"line": 193, "text": "The behavior of the :keyword:`is` and :keyword:`is not` operators cannot be"}, {"line": 194, "text": "customized; also they can be applied to any two objects and never raise an"}, {"line": 195, "text": "exception."}, {"line": 196, "text": ""}, {"line": 197, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 198, "text": "   pair: operator; in"}, {"line": 199, "text": "   pair: operator; not in"}, {"line": 200, "text": ""}, {"line": 201, "text": "Two more operations with the same syntactic priority, :keyword:`in` and"}, {"line": 202, "text": ":keyword:`not in`, are supported by types that are :term:`iterable` or"}, {"line": 203, "text": "implement the :meth:`~object.__contains__` method."}, {"line": 204, "text": ""}, {"line": 205, "text": ".. _typesnumeric:"}, {"line": 206, "text": ""}, {"line": 207, "text": "Numeric Types --- :class:`int`, :class:`float`, :class:`complex`"}, {"line": 208, "text": "================================================================"}, {"line": 209, "text": ""}, {"line": 210, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 211, "text": "   pair: object; numeric"}, {"line": 212, "text": "   pair: object; Boolean"}, {"line": 213, "text": "   pair: object; integer"}, {"line": 214, "text": "   pair: object; floating-point"}, {"line": 215, "text": "   pair: object; complex number"}, {"line": 216, "text": "   pair: C; language"}, {"line": 217, "text": ""}, {"line": 218, "text": "There are three distinct numeric types: :dfn:`integers`, :dfn:`floating-point"}, {"line": 219, "text": "numbers`, and :dfn:`complex numbers`.  In addition, Booleans are a"}, {"line": 220, "text": "subtype of integers.  Integers have unlimited precision.  Floating-point"}, {"line": 221, "text": "numbers are usually implemented using :c:expr:`double` in C; information"}, {"line": 222, "text": "about the precision and internal representation of floating-point"}, {"line": 223, "text": "numbers for the machine on which your program is running is available"}, {"line": 224, "text": "in :data:`sys.float_info`.  Complex numbers have a real and imaginary"}, {"line": 225, "text": "part, which are each a floating-point number.  To extract these parts"}, {"line": 226, "text": "from a complex number *z*, use ``z.real`` and ``z.imag``. (The standard"}, {"line": 227, "text": "library includes the additional numeric types :mod:`fractions.Fraction`, for"}, {"line": 228, "text": "rationals, and :mod:`decimal.Decimal`, for floating-point numbers with"}, {"line": 229, "text": "user-definable precision.)"}, {"line": 230, "text": ""}, {"line": 231, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 232, "text": "   pair: numeric; literals"}, {"line": 233, "text": "   pair: integer; literals"}, {"line": 234, "text": "   pair: floating-point; literals"}, {"line": 235, "text": "   pair: complex number; literals"}, {"line": 236, "text": "   pair: hexadecimal; literals"}, {"line": 237, "text": "   pair: octal; literals"}, {"line": 238, "text": "   pair: binary; literals"}, {"line": 239, "text": ""}, {"line": 240, "text": "Numbers are created by numeric literals or as the result of built-in functions"}, {"line": 241, "text": "and operators.  Unadorned integer literals (including hex, octal and binary"}, {"line": 242, "text": "numbers) yield integers.  Numeric literals containing a decimal point or an"}, {"line": 243, "text": "exponent sign yield floating-point numbers.  Appending ``'j'`` or ``'J'`` to a"}, {"line": 244, "text": "numeric literal yields an imaginary number (a complex number with a zero real"}, {"line": 245, "text": "part) which you can add to an integer or float to get a complex number with real"}, {"line": 246, "text": "and imaginary parts."}, {"line": 247, "text": ""}, {"line": 248, "text": "The constructors :func:`int`, :func:`float`, and"}, {"line": 249, "text": ":func:`complex` can be used to produce numbers of a specific type."}, {"line": 250, "text": ""}, {"line": 251, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 252, "text": "   single: arithmetic"}, {"line": 253, "text": "   pair: built-in function; int"}, {"line": 254, "text": "   pair: built-in function; float"}, {"line": 255, "text": "   pair: built-in function; complex"}, {"line": 256, "text": "   single: operator; + (plus)"}, {"line": 257, "text": "   single: + (plus); unary operator"}, {"line": 258, "text": "   single: + (plus); binary operator"}, {"line": 259, "text": "   single: operator; - (minus)"}, {"line": 260, "text": "   single: - (minus); unary operator"}, {"line": 261, "text": "   single: - (minus); binary operator"}, {"line": 262, "text": "   pair: operator; * (asterisk)"}, {"line": 263, "text": "   pair: operator; / (slash)"}, {"line": 264, "text": "   pair: operator; //"}, {"line": 265, "text": "   pair: operator; % (percent)"}, {"line": 266, "text": "   pair: operator; **"}, {"line": 267, "text": ""}, {"line": 268, "text": ".. _stdtypes-mixed-arithmetic:"}, {"line": 269, "text": ""}, {"line": 270, "text": "Python fully supports mixed arithmetic: when a binary arithmetic operator has"}, {"line": 271, "text": "operands of different built-in numeric types, the operand with the \"narrower\""}, {"line": 272, "text": "type is widened to that of the other:"}, {"line": 273, "text": ""}, {"line": 274, "text": "* If both arguments are complex numbers, no conversion is performed;"}, {"line": 275, "text": "* if either argument is a complex or a floating-point number, the other is"}, {"line": 276, "text": "  converted to a floating-point number;"}, {"line": 277, "text": "* otherwise, both must be integers and no conversion is necessary."}, {"line": 278, "text": ""}, {"line": 279, "text": "Arithmetic with complex and real operands is defined by the usual mathematical"}, {"line": 280, "text": "formula, for example::"}, {"line": 281, "text": ""}, {"line": 282, "text": "    x + complex(u, v) = complex(x + u, v)"}, {"line": 283, "text": "    x * complex(u, v) = complex(x * u, x * v)"}, {"line": 284, "text": ""}, {"line": 285, "text": "A comparison between numbers of different types behaves as though the exact"}, {"line": 286, "text": "values of those numbers were being compared. [2]_"}, {"line": 287, "text": ""}, {"line": 288, "text": "All numeric types (except complex) support the following operations (for priorities of"}, {"line": 289, "text": "the operations, see :ref:`operator-summary`):"}, {"line": 290, "text": ""}, {"line": 291, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 292, "text": "| Operation           | Result                          | Notes   | Full documentation |"}, {"line": 293, "text": "+=====================+=================================+=========+====================+"}, {"line": 294, "text": "| ``x + y``           | sum of *x* and *y*              |         |                    |"}, {"line": 295, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 296, "text": "| ``x - y``           | difference of *x* and *y*       |         |                    |"}, {"line": 297, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 298, "text": "| ``x * y``           | product of *x* and *y*          |         |                    |"}, {"line": 299, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 300, "text": "| ``x / y``           | quotient of *x* and *y*         |         |                    |"}, {"line": 301, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 302, "text": "| ``x // y``          | floored quotient of *x* and     | \\(1)\\(2)|                    |"}, {"line": 303, "text": "|                     | *y*                             |         |                    |"}, {"line": 304, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 305, "text": "| ``x % y``           | remainder of ``x / y``          | \\(2)    |                    |"}, {"line": 306, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 307, "text": "| ``-x``              | *x* negated                     |         |                    |"}, {"line": 308, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 309, "text": "| ``+x``              | *x* unchanged                   |         |                    |"}, {"line": 310, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 311, "text": "| ``abs(x)``          | absolute value or magnitude of  |         | :func:`abs`        |"}, {"line": 312, "text": "|                     | *x*                             |         |                    |"}, {"line": 313, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 314, "text": "| ``int(x)``          | *x* converted to integer        | \\(3)\\(6)| :func:`int`        |"}, {"line": 315, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 316, "text": "| "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 318, "text": "| ``complex(re, im)`` | a complex number with real part | \\(6)    | :func:`complex`    |"}, {"line": 319, "text": "|                     | *re*, imaginary part *im*.      |         |                    |"}, {"line": 320, "text": "|                     | *im* defaults to zero. conjugate of the complex number |         |                    |"}, {"line": 323, "text": "|                     | *c*                             |         |                    |"}, {"line": 324, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 325, "text": "| ``divmod(x, y)``    | the pair ``(x // y, x % y)``    | \\(2)    | :func:`divmod`     |"}, {"line": 326, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 327, "text": "| ``pow(x, y)``       | *x* to the power *y*            | \\(5)    | :func:`pow`        |"}, {"line": 328, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 329, "text": "| ``x ** y``          | *x* to the power *y*            | \\(5)    |                    |"}, {"line": 330, "text": "+---------------------+---------------------------------+---------+--------------------+"}, {"line": 331, "text": ""}, {"line": 332, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 333, "text": "   triple: operations on; numeric; types"}, {"line": 334, "text": "   single: conjugate() (complex number method)"}, {"line": 335, "text": ""}, {"line": 336, "text": "Notes:"}, {"line": 337, "text": ""}, {"line": 338, "text": "(1)"}, {"line": 339, "text": "   Also referred to as integer division.  For operands of type :class:`int`,"}, {"line": 340, "text": "   the result has type :class:`int`.  For operands of type :class:`float`,"}, {"line": 341, "text": "   the result has type :class:`float`.  In general, the result is a whole"}, {"line": 342, "text": "   integer, though the result's type is not necessarily :class:`int`.  The result is"}, {"line": 343, "text": "   always rounded towards minus infinity: ``1//2`` is ``0``, ``(-1)//2`` is"}, {"line": 344, "text": "   ``-1``, ``1//(-2)`` is ``-1``, and ``(-1)//(-2)`` is ``0``."}, {"line": 345, "text": ""}, {"line": 346, "text": "(2)"}, {"line": 347, "text": "   Not for complex numbers.  Instead convert to floats using :func:`abs` if"}, {"line": 348, "text": "   appropriate."}, {"line": 349, "text": ""}, {"line": 350, "text": "(3)"}, {"line": 351, "text": "   .. index::"}, {"line": 352, "text": "      pair: module; math"}, {"line": 353, "text": "      single: floor() (in module math)"}, {"line": 354, "text": "      single: ceil() (in module math)"}, {"line": 355, "text": "      single: trunc() (in module math)"}, {"line": 356, "text": "      pair: numeric; conversions"}, {"line": 357, "text": ""}, {"line": 358, "text": "   Conversion from :class:`float` to :class:`int` truncates, discarding the"}, {"line": 359, "text": "   fractional part. See functions :func:`math.floor` and :func:`math.ceil` for"}, {"line": 360, "text": "   alternative conversions."}, {"line": 361, "text": ""}, {"line": 362, "text": "(4)"}, {"line": 363, "text": "   float also accepts the strings \"nan\" and \"inf\" with an optional prefix \"+\""}, {"line": 364, "text": "   or \"-\" for Not a Number (NaN) and positive or negative infinity."}, {"line": 365, "text": ""}, {"line": 366, "text": "(5)"}, {"line": 367, "text": "   Python defines ``pow(0, 0)`` and ``0 ** 0`` to be ``1``, as is common for"}, {"line": 368, "text": "   programming languages."}, {"line": 369, "text": ""}, {"line": 370, "text": "(6)"}, {"line": 371, "text": "   The numeric literals accepted include the digits ``0`` to ``9`` or any"}, {"line": 372, "text": "   Unicode equivalent (code points with the ``Nd`` property)."}, {"line": 373, "text": ""}, {"line": 374, "text": "   See `the Unicode Standard "text": "| :func:`round(x[,   | *x* rounded to *n* digits,                  |"}, {"line": 388, "text": "| n]) <round>`       | rounding half to even. If *n* is            |"}, {"line": 389, "text": "|                    | omitted, it defaults to 0.                  |"}, {"line": 390, "text": "+--------------------+---------------------------------------------+"}, {"line": 391, "text": "| :func:`math.floor(\\| the greatest :class:`~numbers.Integral`     |"}, {"line": 392, "text": "| x) <math.floor>`   | <= *x*                                      |"}, {"line": 393, "text": "+--------------------+---------------------------------------------+"}, {"line": 394, "text": "| :func:`math.ceil(x)| the least :class:`~numbers.Integral` >= *x* |"}, {"line": 395, "text": "| <math.ceil>`       |                                             |"}, {"line": 396, "text": "+--------------------+---------------------------------------------+"}, {"line": 397, "text": ""}, {"line": 398, "text": "For additional numeric operations see the :mod:`math` and :mod:`cmath`"}, {"line": 399, "text": "modules."}, {"line": 400, "text": ""}, {"line": 401, "text": ".. XXXJH exceptions: overflow (when? what operations?) zerodivision"}, {"line": 402, "text": ""}, {"line": 403, "text": ""}, {"line": 404, "text": ".. _bitstring-ops:"}, {"line": 405, "text": ""}, {"line": 406, "text": "Bitwise Operations on Integer Types"}, {"line": 407, "text": "-----------------------------------"}, {"line": 408, "text": ""}, {"line": 409, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 410, "text": "   triple: operations on; integer; types"}, {"line": 411, "text": "   pair: bitwise; operations"}, {"line": 412, "text": "   pair: shifting; operations"}, {"line": 413, "text": "   pair: masking; operations"}, {"line": 414, "text": "   pair: operator; | (vertical bar)"}, {"line": 415, "text": "   pair: operator; ^ (caret)"}, {"line": 416, "text": "   pair: operator; & (ampersand)"}, {"line": 417, "text": "   pair: operator; <<"}, {"line": 418, "text": "   pair: operator; >>"}, {"line": 419, "text": "   pair: operator; ~ (tilde)"}, {"line": 420, "text": ""}, {"line": 421, "text": "Bitwise operations only make sense for integers. The result of bitwise"}, {"line": 422, "text": "operations is calculated as though carried out in two's complement with an"}, {"line": 423, "text": "infinite number of sign bits."}, {"line": 424, "text": ""}, {"line": 425, "text": "The priorities of the binary bitwise operations are all lower than the numeric"}, {"line": 426, "text": "operations and higher than the comparisons; the unary operation ``~`` has the"}, {"line": 427, "text": "same priority as the other unary numeric operations (``+`` and ``-``)."}, {"line": 428, "text": ""}, {"line": 429, "text": "This table lists the bitwise operations sorted in ascending priority:"}, {"line": 430, "text": ""}, {"line": 431, "text": "+------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 432, "text": "| Operation  | Result                         | Notes    |"}, {"line": 433, "text": "+============+================================+==========+"}, {"line": 434, "text": "| ``x | y``  | bitwise :dfn:`or` of *x* and   | \\(4)     |"}, {"line": 435, "text": "|            | *y*                            |          |"}, {"line": 436, "text": "+------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 437, "text": "| ``x ^ y``  | bitwise :dfn:`exclusive or` of | \\(4)     |"}, {"line": 438, "text": "|            | *x* and *y*                    |          |"}, {"line": 439, "text": "+------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 440, "text": "| ``x & y``  | bitwise :dfn:`and` of *x* and  | \\(4)     |"}, {"line": 441, "text": "|            | *y*                            |          |"}, {"line": 442, "text": "+------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 443, "text": "| ``x << n`` | *x* shifted left by *n* bits   | (1)(2)   |"}, {"line": 444, "text": "+------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 445, "text": "| ``x >> n`` | *x* shifted right by *n* bits  | (1)(3)   |"}, {"line": 446, "text": "+------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 447, "text": "| ``~x``     | the bits of *x* inverted       |          |"}, {"line": 448, "text": "+------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 449, "text": ""}, {"line": 450, "text": "Notes:"}, {"line": 451, "text": ""}, {"line": 452, "text": "(1)"}, {"line": 453, "text": "   Negative shift counts are illegal and cause a :exc:`ValueError` to be raised."}, {"line": 454, "text": ""}, {"line": 455, "text": "(2)"}, {"line": 456, "text": "   A left shift by *n* bits is equivalent to multiplication by ``pow(2, n)``."}, {"line": 457, "text": ""}, {"line": 458, "text": "(3)"}, {"line": 459, "text": "   A right shift by *n* bits is equivalent to floor division by ``pow(2, n)``."}, {"line": 460, "text": ""}, {"line": 461, "text": "(4)"}, {"line": 462, "text": "   Performing these calculations with at least one extra sign extension bit in"}, {"line": 463, "text": "   a finite two's complement representation (a working bit-width of"}, {"line": 464, "text": "   ``1 + max(x.bit_length(), y.bit_length())`` or more) is sufficient to get the"}, {"line": 465, "text": "   same result as if there were an infinite number of sign bits."}, {"line": 466, "text": ""}, {"line": 467, "text": ""}, {"line": 468, "text": "Additional Methods on Integer Types"}, {"line": 469, "text": "-----------------------------------"}, {"line": 470, "text": ""}, {"line": 471, "text": "The int type implements the :class:`numbers.Integral` :term:`abstract base"}, {"line": 472, "text": "class`. In addition, it provides a few more methods:"}, {"line": 473, "text": ""}, {"line": 474, "text": ".. method:: int.bit_length()"}, {"line": 475, "text": ""}, {"line": 476, "text": "    Return the number of bits necessary to represent an integer in binary,"}, {"line": 477, "text": "    excluding the sign and leading zeros::"}, {"line": 478, "text": ""}, {"line": 479, "text": "        >>> n = -37"}, {"line": 480, "text": "        >>> bin(n)"}, {"line": 481, "text": "        '-0b100101'"}, {"line": 482, "text": "        >>> n.bit_length()"}, {"line": 483, "text": "        6"}, {"line": 484, "text": ""}, {"line": 485, "text": "    More precisely, if ``x`` is nonzero, then ``x.bit_length()`` is the"}, {"line": 486, "text": "    unique positive integer ``k`` such that ``2**(k-1) <= abs(x) < 2**k``."}, {"line": 487, "text": "    Equivalently, when ``abs(x)`` is small enough to have a correctly"}, {"line": 488, "text": "    rounded logarithm, then ``k = 1 + int(log(abs(x), 2))``."}, {"line": 489, "text": "    If ``x`` is zero, then ``x.bit_length()`` returns ``0``."}, {"line": 490, "text": ""}, {"line": 491, "text": "    Equivalent to::"}, {"line": 492, "text": ""}, {"line": 493, "text": "        def bit_length(self):"}, {"line": 494, "text": "            s = bin(self)       # binary representation:  bin(-37) --> '-0b100101'"}, {"line": 495, "text": "            s = s.lstrip('-0b') # remove leading zeros and minus sign"}, {"line": 496, "text": "            return len(s)       # len('100101') --> 6"}, {"line": 497, "text": ""}, {"line": 498, "text": "    .. versionadded:: 3.1"}, {"line": 499, "text": ""}, {"line": 500, "text": ".. method:: int.bit_count()"}, {"line": 501, "text": ""}, {"line": 502, "text": "    Return the number of ones in the binary representation of the absolute"}, {"line": 503, "text": "    value of the integer. This is also known as the population count."}, {"line": 504, "text": "    Example::"}, {"line": 505, "text": ""}, {"line": 506, "text": "        >>> n = 19"}, {"line": 507, "text": "        >>> bin(n)"}, {"line": 508, "text": "        '0b10011'"}, {"line": 509, "text": "        >>> n.bit_count()"}, {"line": 510, "text": "        3"}, {"line": 511, "text": "        >>> (-n).bit_count()"}, {"line": 512, "text": "        3"}, {"line": 513, "text": ""}, {"line": 514, "text": "    Equivalent to::"}, {"line": 515, "text": ""}, {"line": 516, "text": "        def bit_count(self):"}, {"line": 517, "text": "            return bin(self).count(\"1\")"}, {"line": 518, "text": ""}, {"line": 519, "text": "    .. versionadded:: 3.10"}, {"line": 520, "text": ""}, {"line": 521, "text": ".. method:: int.to_bytes(length=1, byteorder='big', *, signed=False)"}, {"line": 522, "text": ""}, {"line": 523, "text": "    Return an array of bytes representing an integer."}, {"line": 524, "text": ""}, {"line": 525, "text": "        >>> (1024).to_bytes(2, byteorder='big')"}, {"line": 526, "text": "        b'\\x04\\x00'"}, {"line": 527, "text": "        >>> (1024).to_bytes(10, byteorder='big')"}, {"line": 528, "text": "        b'\\x00\\x00\\x00\\x00\\x00\\x00\\x00\\x00\\x04\\x00'"}, {"line": 529, "text": "        >>> (-1024).to_bytes(10, byteorder='big', signed=True)"}, {"line": 530, "text": "        b'\\xff\\xff\\xff\\xff\\xff\\xff\\xff\\xff\\xfc\\x00'"}, {"line": 531, "text": "        >>> x = 1000"}, {"line": 532, "text": "        >>> x.to_bytes((x.bit_length() + 7) // 8, byteorder='little')"}, {"line": 533, "text": "        b'\\xe8\\x03'"}, {"line": 534, "text": ""}, {"line": 535, "text": "    The integer is represented using *length* bytes, and defaults to 1.  An"}, {"line": 536, "text": "    :exc:`OverflowError` is raised if the integer is not representable with"}, {"line": 537, "text": "    the given number of bytes."}, {"line": 538, "text": ""}, {"line": 539, "text": "    The *byteorder* argument determines the byte order used to represent the"}, {"line": 540, "text": "    integer, and defaults to ``\"big\"``.  If *byteorder* is"}, {"line": 541, "text": "    ``\"big\"``, the most significant byte is at the beginning of the byte"}, {"line": 542, "text": "    array.  If *byteorder* is ``\"little\"``, the most significant byte is at"}, {"line": 543, "text": "    the end of the byte array."}, {"line": 544, "text": ""}, {"line": 545, "text": "    The *signed* argument determines whether two's complement is used to"}, {"line": 546, "text": "    represent the integer.  If *signed* is ``False`` and a negative integer is"}, {"line": 547, "text": "    given, an :exc:`OverflowError` is raised. The default value for *signed*"}, {"line": 548, "text": "    is ``False``."}, {"line": 549, "text": ""}, {"line": 550, "text": "    The default values can be used to conveniently turn an integer into a"}, {"line": 551, "text": "    single byte object::"}, {"line": 552, "text": ""}, {"line": 553, "text": "        >>> (65).to_bytes()"}, {"line": 554, "text": "        b'A'"}, {"line": 555, "text": ""}, {"line": 556, "text": "    However, when using the default arguments, don't try"}, {"line": 557, "text": "    to convert a value greater than 255 or you'll get an :exc:`OverflowError`."}, {"line": 558, "text": ""}, {"line": 559, "text": "    Equivalent to::"}, {"line": 560, "text": ""}, {"line": 561, "text": "        def to_bytes(n, length=1, byteorder='big', signed=False):"}, {"line": 562, "text": "            if byteorder == 'little':"}, {"line": 563, "text": "                order = range(length)"}, {"line": 564, "text": "            elif byteorder == 'big':"}, {"line": 565, "text": "                order = reversed(range(length))"}, {"line": 566, "text": "            else:"}, {"line": 567, "text": "                raise ValueError(\"byteorder must be either 'little' or 'big'\")"}, {"line": 568, "text": ""}, {"line": 569, "text": "            return bytes((n >> i*8) & 0xff for i in order)"}, {"line": 570, "text": ""}, {"line": 571, "text": "    .. versionadded:: 3.2"}, {"line": 572, "text": "    .. versionchanged:: 3.11"}, {"line": 573, "text": "       Added default argument values for ``length`` and ``byteorder``."}, {"line": 574, "text": ""}, {"line": 575, "text": ".. classmethod:: int.from_bytes(bytes, byteorder='big', *, signed=False)"}, {"line": 576, "text": ""}, {"line": 577, "text": "    Return the integer represented by the given array of bytes."}, {"line": 578, "text": ""}, {"line": 579, "text": "        >>> int.from_bytes(b'\\x00\\x10', byteorder='big')"}, {"line": 580, "text": "        16"}, {"line": 581, "text": "        >>> int.from_bytes(b'\\x00\\x10', byteorder='little')"}, {"line": 582, "text": "        4096"}, {"line": 583, "text": "        >>> int.from_bytes(b'\\xfc\\x00', byteorder='big', signed=True)"}, {"line": 584, "text": "        -1024"}, {"line": 585, "text": "        >>> int.from_bytes(b'\\xfc\\x00', byteorder='big', signed=False)"}, {"line": 586, "text": "        64512"}, {"line": 587, "text": "        >>> int.from_bytes([255, 0, 0], byteorder='big')"}, {"line": 588, "text": "        16711680"}, {"line": 589, "text": ""}, {"line": 590, "text": "    The argument *bytes* must either be a :term:`bytes-like object` or an"}, {"line": 591, "text": "    iterable producing bytes."}, {"line": 592, "text": ""}, {"line": 593, "text": "    The *byteorder* argument determines the byte order used to represent the"}, {"line": 594, "text": "    integer, and defaults to ``\"big\"``.  If *byteorder* is"}, {"line": 595, "text": "    ``\"big\"``, the most significant byte is at the beginning of the byte"}, {"line": 596, "text": "    array.  If *byteorder* is ``\"little\"``, the most significant byte is at"}, {"line": 597, "text": "    the end of the byte array.  To request the native byte order of the host"}, {"line": 598, "text": "    system, use :data:`sys.byteorder` as the byte order value."}, {"line": 599, "text": ""}, {"line": 600, "text": "    The *signed* argument indicates whether two's complement is used to"}, {"line": 601, "text": "    represent the integer."}, {"line": 602, "text": ""}, {"line": 603, "text": "    Equivalent to::"}, {"line": 604, "text": ""}, {"line": 605, "text": "        def from_bytes(bytes, byteorder='big', signed=False):"}, {"line": 606, "text": "            if byteorder == 'little':"}, {"line": 607, "text": "                little_ordered = list(bytes)"}, {"line": 608, "text": "            elif byteorder == 'big':"}, {"line": 609, "text": "                little_ordered = list(reversed(bytes))"}, {"line": 610, "text": "            else:"}, {"line": 611, "text": "                raise ValueError(\"byteorder must be either 'little' or 'big'\")"}, {"line": 612, "text": ""}, {"line": 613, "text": "            n = sum(b << i*8 for i, b in enumerate(little_ordered))"}, {"line": 614, "text": "            if signed and little_ordered and (little_ordered[-1] & 0x80):"}, {"line": 615, "text": "                n -= 1 << 8*len(little_ordered)"}, {"line": 616, "text": ""}, {"line": 617, "text": "            return n"}, {"line": 618, "text": ""}, {"line": 619, "text": "    .. versionadded:: 3.2"}, {"line": 620, "text": "    .. versionchanged:: 3.11"}, {"line": 621, "text": "       Added default argument value for ``byteorder``."}, {"line": 622, "text": ""}, {"line": 623, "text": ".. method:: int.as_integer_ratio()"}, {"line": 624, "text": ""}, {"line": 625, "text": "   Return a pair of integers whose ratio is equal to the original"}, {"line": 626, "text": "   integer and has a positive denominator.  The integer ratio of integers"}, {"line": 627, "text": "   (whole numbers) is always the integer as the numerator and ``1`` as the"}, {"line": 628, "text": "   denominator."}, {"line": 629, "text": ""}, {"line": 630, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.8"}, {"line": 631, "text": ""}, {"line": 632, "text": ".. method:: int.is_integer()"}, {"line": 633, "text": ""}, {"line": 634, "text": "   Returns ``True``. Exists for duck type compatibility with :meth:`float.is_integer`."}, {"line": 635, "text": ""}, {"line": 636, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.12"}, {"line": 637, "text": ""}, {"line": 638, "text": "Additional Methods on Float"}, {"line": 639, "text": "---------------------------"}, {"line": 640, "text": ""}, {"line": 641, "text": "The float type implements the :class:`numbers.Real` :term:`abstract base"}, {"line": 642, "text": "class`. float also has the following additional methods."}, {"line": 643, "text": ""}, {"line": 644, "text": ".. classmethod:: float.from_number(x)"}, {"line": 645, "text": ""}, {"line": 646, "text": "   Class method to return a floating-point number constructed from a number *x*."}, {"line": 647, "text": ""}, {"line": 648, "text": "   If the argument is an integer or a floating-point number, a"}, {"line": 649, "text": "   floating-point number with the same value (within Python's floating-point"}, {"line": 650, "text": "   precision) is returned.  If the argument is outside the range of a Python"}, {"line": 651, "text": "   float, an :exc:`OverflowError` will be raised."}, {"line": 652, "text": ""}, {"line": 653, "text": "   For a general Python object ``x``, ``float.from_number(x)`` delegates to"}, {"line": 654, "text": "   ``x.__float__()``."}, {"line": 655, "text": "   If :meth:`~object.__float__` is not defined then it falls back"}, {"line": 656, "text": "   to :meth:`~object.__index__`."}, {"line": 657, "text": ""}, {"line": 658, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.14"}, {"line": 659, "text": ""}, {"line": 660, "text": ""}, {"line": 661, "text": ".. method:: float.as_integer_ratio()"}, {"line": 662, "text": ""}, {"line": 663, "text": "   Return a pair of integers whose ratio is exactly equal to the"}, {"line": 664, "text": "   original float. The ratio is in lowest terms and has a positive denominator.  Raises"}, {"line": 665, "text": "   :exc:`OverflowError` on infinities and a :exc:`ValueError` on"}, {"line": 666, "text": "   NaNs."}, {"line": 667, "text": ""}, {"line": 668, "text": ".. method:: float.is_integer()"}, {"line": 669, "text": ""}, {"line": 670, "text": "   Return ``True`` if the float instance is finite with integral"}, {"line": 671, "text": "   value, and ``False`` otherwise::"}, {"line": 672, "text": ""}, {"line": 673, "text": "      >>> (-2.0).is_integer()"}, {"line": 674, "text": "      True"}, {"line": 675, "text": "      >>> (3.2).is_integer()"}, {"line": 676, "text": "      False"}, {"line": 677, "text": ""}, {"line": 678, "text": "Two methods support conversion to"}, {"line": 679, "text": "and from hexadecimal strings.  Since Python's floats are stored"}, {"line": 680, "text": "internally as binary numbers, converting a float to or from a"}, {"line": 681, "text": "*decimal* string usually involves a small rounding error.  In"}, {"line": 682, "text": "contrast, hexadecimal strings allow exact representation and"}, {"line": 683, "text": "specification of floating-point numbers.  This can be useful when"}, {"line": 684, "text": "debugging, and in numerical work."}, {"line": 685, "text": ""}, {"line": 686, "text": ""}, {"line": 687, "text": ".. method:: float.hex()"}, {"line": 688, "text": ""}, {"line": 689, "text": "   Return a representation of a floating-point number as a hexadecimal"}, {"line": 690, "text": "   string.  For finite floating-point numbers, this representation"}, {"line": 691, "text": "   will always include a leading ``0x`` and a trailing ``p`` and"}, {"line": 692, "text": "   exponent."}, {"line": 693, "text": ""}, {"line": 694, "text": ""}, {"line": 695, "text": ".. classmethod:: float.fromhex(s)"}, {"line": 696, "text": ""}, {"line": 697, "text": "   Class method to return the float represented by a hexadecimal"}, {"line": 698, "text": "   string *s*.  The string *s* may have leading and trailing"}, {"line": 699, "text": "   whitespace."}, {"line": 700, "text": ""}, {"line": 701, "text": ""}, {"line": 702, "text": "Note that :meth:`float.hex` is an instance method, while"}, {"line": 703, "text": ":meth:`float.fromhex` is a class method."}, {"line": 704, "text": ""}, {"line": 705, "text": "A hexadecimal string takes the form::"}, {"line": 706, "text": ""}, {"line": 707, "text": "   [sign] ['0x'] integer ['.' fraction] ['p' exponent]"}, {"line": 708, "text": ""}, {"line": 709, "text": "where the optional ``sign`` may be either ``+`` or ``-``, ``integer``"}, {"line": 710, "text": "and ``fraction`` are strings of hexadecimal digits, and ``exponent``"}, {"line": 711, "text": "is a decimal integer with an optional leading sign.  Case is not"}, {"line": 712, "text": "significant, and there must be at least one hexadecimal digit in"}, {"line": 713, "text": "either the integer or the fraction.  This syntax is similar to the"}, {"line": 714, "text": "syntax specified in section 6.4.4.2 of the C99 standard, and also to"}, {"line": 715, "text": "the syntax used in Java 1.5 onwards.  In particular, the output of"}, {"line": 716, "text": ":meth:`float.hex` is usable as a hexadecimal floating-point literal in"}, {"line": 717, "text": "C or Java code, and hexadecimal strings produced by C's ``%a`` format"}, {"line": 718, "text": "character or Java's ``Double.toHexString`` are accepted by"}, {"line": 719, "text": ":meth:`float.fromhex`."}, {"line": 720, "text": ""}, {"line": 721, "text": ""}, {"line": 722, "text": "Note that the exponent is written in decimal rather than hexadecimal,"}, {"line": 723, "text": "and that it gives the power of 2 by which to multiply the coefficient."}, {"line": 724, "text": "For example, the hexadecimal string ``0x3.a7p10`` represents the"}, {"line": 725, "text": "floating-point number ``(3 + 10./16 + 7./16**2) * 2.0**10``, or"}, {"line": 726, "text": "``3740.0``::"}, {"line": 727, "text": ""}, {"line": 728, "text": "   >>> float.fromhex('0x3.a7p10')"}, {"line": 729, "text": "   3740.0"}, {"line": 730, "text": ""}, {"line": 731, "text": ""}, {"line": 732, "text": "Applying the reverse conversion to ``3740.0`` gives a different"}, {"line": 733, "text": "hexadecimal string representing the same number::"}, {"line": 734, "text": ""}, {"line": 735, "text": "   >>> float.hex(3740.0)"}, {"line": 736, "text": "   '0x1.d380000000000p+11'"}, {"line": 737, "text": ""}, {"line": 738, "text": ""}, {"line": 739, "text": "Additional Methods on Complex"}, {"line": 740, "text": "-----------------------------"}, {"line": 741, "text": ""}, {"line": 742, "text": "The :class:`!complex` type implements the :class:`numbers.Complex`"}, {"line": 743, "text": ":term:`abstract base class`."}, {"line": 744, "text": ":class:`!complex` also has the following additional methods."}, {"line": 745, "text": ""}, {"line": 746, "text": ".. classmethod:: complex.from_number(x)"}, {"line": 747, "text": ""}, {"line": 748, "text": "   Class method to convert a number to a complex number."}, {"line": 749, "text": ""}, {"line": 750, "text": "   For a general Python object ``x``, ``complex.from_number(x)`` delegates to"}, {"line": 751, "text": "   ``x.__complex__()``.  If :meth:`~object.__complex__` is not defined then it falls back"}, {"line": 752, "text": "   to :meth:`~object.__float__`.  If :meth:`!__float__` is not defined then it falls back"}, {"line": 753, "text": "   to :meth:`~object.__index__`."}, {"line": 754, "text": ""}, {"line": 755, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.14"}, {"line": 756, "text": ""}, {"line": 757, "text": ""}, {"line": 758, "text": ".. _numeric-hash:"}, {"line": 759, "text": ""}, {"line": 760, "text": "Hashing of numeric types"}, {"line": 761, "text": "------------------------"}, {"line": 762, "text": ""}, {"line": 763, "text": "For numbers ``x`` and ``y``, possibly of different types, it's a requirement"}, {"line": 764, "text": "that ``hash(x) == hash(y)`` whenever ``x == y`` (see the :meth:`~object.__hash__`"}, {"line": 765, "text": "method documentation for more details).  For ease of implementation and"}, {"line": 766, "text": "efficiency across a variety of numeric types (including :class:`int`,"}, {"line": 767, "text": ":class:`float`, :class:`decimal.Decimal` and :class:`fractions.Fraction`)"}, {"line": 768, "text": "Python's hash for numeric types is based on a single mathematical function"}, {"line": 769, "text": "that's defined for any rational number, and hence applies to all instances of"}, {"line": 770, "text": ":class:`int` and :class:`fractions.Fraction`, and all finite instances of"}, {"line": 771, "text": ":class:`float` and :class:`decimal.Decimal`.  Essentially, this function is"}, {"line": 772, "text": "given by reduction modulo ``P`` for a fixed prime ``P``.  The value of ``P`` is"}, {"line": 773, "text": "made available to Python as the :attr:`~sys.hash_info.modulus` attribute of"}, {"line": 774, "text": ":data:`sys.hash_info`."}, {"line": 775, "text": ""}, {"line": 776, "text": ".. impl-detail::"}, {"line": 777, "text": ""}, {"line": 778, "text": "   Currently, the prime used is ``P = 2**31 - 1`` on machines with 32-bit C"}, {"line": 779, "text": "   longs and ``P = 2**61 - 1`` on machines with 64-bit C longs."}, {"line": 780, "text": ""}, {"line": 781, "text": "Here are the rules in detail:"}, {"line": 782, "text": ""}, {"line": 783, "text": "- If ``x = m / n`` is a nonnegative rational number and ``n`` is not divisible"}, {"line": 784, "text": "  by ``P``, define ``hash(x)`` as ``m * invmod(n, P) % P``, where ``invmod(n,"}, {"line": 785, "text": "  P)`` gives the inverse of ``n`` modulo ``P``."}, {"line": 786, "text": ""}, {"line": 787, "text": "- If ``x = m / n`` is a nonnegative rational number and ``n`` is"}, {"line": 788, "text": "  divisible by ``P`` (but ``m`` is not) then ``n`` has no inverse"}, {"line": 789, "text": "  modulo ``P`` and the rule above doesn't apply; in this case define"}, {"line": 790, "text": "  ``hash(x)`` to be the constant value ``sys.hash_info.inf``."}, {"line": 791, "text": ""}, {"line": 792, "text": "- If ``x = m / n`` is a negative rational number define ``hash(x)``"}, {"line": 793, "text": "  as ``-hash(-x)``.  If the resulting hash is ``-1``, replace it with"}, {"line": 794, "text": "  ``-2``."}, {"line": 795, "text": ""}, {"line": 796, "text": "- The particular values ``sys.hash_info.inf`` and ``-sys.hash_info.inf``"}, {"line": 797, "text": "  are used as hash values for positive"}, {"line": 798, "text": "  infinity or negative infinity (respectively)."}, {"line": 799, "text": ""}, {"line": 800, "text": "- For a :class:`complex` number ``z``, the hash values of the real"}, {"line": 801, "text": "  and imaginary parts are combined by computing ``hash(z.real) +"}, {"line": 802, "text": "  sys.hash_info.imag * hash(z.imag)``, reduced modulo"}, {"line": 803, "text": "  ``2**sys.hash_info.width`` so that it lies in"}, {"line": 804, "text": "  ``range(-2**(sys.hash_info.width - 1), 2**(sys.hash_info.width -"}, {"line": 805, "text": "  1))``.  Again, if the result is ``-1``, it's replaced with ``-2``."}, {"line": 806, "text": ""}, {"line": 807, "text": ""}, {"line": 808, "text": "To clarify the above rules, here's some example Python code,"}, {"line": 809, "text": "equivalent to the built-in hash, for computing the hash of a rational"}, {"line": 810, "text": "number, :class:`float`, or :class:`complex`::"}, {"line": 811, "text": ""}, {"line": 812, "text": ""}, {"line": 813, "text": "   import sys, math"}, {"line": 814, "text": ""}, {"line": 815, "text": "   def hash_fraction(m, n):"}, {"line": 816, "text": "       \"\"\"Compute the hash of a rational number m / n."}, {"line": 817, "text": ""}, {"line": 818, "text": "       Assumes m and n are integers, with n positive."}, {"line": 819, "text": "       Equivalent to hash(fractions.Fraction(m, n))."}, {"line": 820, "text": ""}, {"line": 821, "text": "       \"\"\""}, {"line": 822, "text": "       P = sys.hash_info.modulus"}, {"line": 823, "text": "       # Remove common factors of P.  (Unnecessary if m and n already coprime.)"}, {"line": 824, "text": "       while m % P == n % P == 0:"}, {"line": 825, "text": "           m, n = m // P, n // P"}, {"line": 826, "text": ""}, {"line": 827, "text": "       if n % P == 0:"}, {"line": 828, "text": "           hash_value = sys.hash_info.inf"}, {"line": 829, "text": "       else:"}, {"line": 830, "text": "           # Fermat's Little Theorem: pow(n, P-1, P) is 1, so"}, {"line": 831, "text": "           # pow(n, P-2, P) gives the inverse of n modulo P."}, {"line": 832, "text": "           hash_value = (abs(m) % P) * pow(n, P - 2, P) % P"}, {"line": 833, "text": "       if m < 0:"}, {"line": 834, "text": "           hash_value = -hash_value"}, {"line": 835, "text": "       if hash_value == -1:"}, {"line": 836, "text": "           hash_value = -2"}, {"line": 837, "text": "       return hash_value"}, {"line": 838, "text": ""}, {"line": 839, "text": "   def hash_float(x):"}, {"line": 840, "text": "       \"\"\"Compute the hash of a float x.\"\"\""}, {"line": 841, "text": ""}, {"line": 842, "text": "       if math.isnan(x):"}, {"line": 843, "text": "           return object.__hash__(x)"}, {"line": 844, "text": "       elif math.isinf(x):"}, {"line": 845, "text": "           return sys.hash_info.inf if x > 0 else -sys.hash_info.inf"}, {"line": 846, "text": "       else:"}, {"line": 847, "text": "           return hash_fraction(*x.as_integer_ratio())"}, {"line": 848, "text": ""}, {"line": 849, "text": "   def hash_complex(z):"}, {"line": 850, "text": "       \"\"\"Compute the hash of a complex number z.\"\"\""}, {"line": 851, "text": ""}, {"line": 852, "text": "       hash_value = hash_float(z.real) + sys.hash_info.imag * hash_float(z.imag)"}, {"line": 853, "text": "       # do a signed reduction modulo 2**sys.hash_info.width"}, {"line": 854, "text": "       M = 2**(sys.hash_info.width - 1)"}, {"line": 855, "text": "       hash_value = (hash_value & (M - 1)) - (hash_value & M)"}, {"line": 856, "text": "       if hash_value == -1:"}, {"line": 857, "text": "           hash_value = -2"}, {"line": 858, "text": "       return hash_value"}, {"line": 859, "text": ""}, {"line": 860, "text": ".. _bltin-boolean-values:"}, {"line": 861, "text": ".. _typebool:"}, {"line": 862, "text": ""}, {"line": 863, "text": "Boolean Type - :class:`bool`"}, {"line": 864, "text": "============================"}, {"line": 865, "text": ""}, {"line": 866, "text": "Booleans represent truth values. The :class:`bool` type has exactly two"}, {"line": 867, "text": "constant instances: ``True`` and ``False``."}, {"line": 868, "text": ""}, {"line": 869, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 870, "text": "   single: False"}, {"line": 871, "text": "   single: True"}, {"line": 872, "text": "   pair: Boolean; values"}, {"line": 873, "text": ""}, {"line": 874, "text": "The built-in function :func:`bool`  converts any value to a boolean, if the"}, {"line": 875, "text": "value can be interpreted as a truth value (see section :ref:`truth` above)."}, {"line": 876, "text": ""}, {"line": 877, "text": "For logical operations, use the :ref:`boolean operators <boolean>` ``and``,"}, {"line": 878, "text": "``or`` and ``not``."}, {"line": 879, "text": "When applying the bitwise operators ``&``, ``|``, ``^`` to two booleans, they"}, {"line": 880, "text": "return a bool equivalent to the logical operations \"and\", \"or\", \"xor\". However,"}, {"line": 881, "text": "the logical operators ``and``, ``or`` and ``!=`` should be preferred"}, {"line": 882, "text": "over ``&``, ``|`` and ``^``."}, {"line": 883, "text": ""}, {"line": 884, "text": ".. deprecated:: 3.12"}, {"line": 885, "text": ""}, {"line": 886, "text": "   The use of the bitwise inversion operator ``~`` is deprecated and will"}, {"line": 887, "text": "   raise an error in Python 3.16."}, {"line": 888, "text": ""}, {"line": 889, "text": ":class:`bool` is a subclass of :class:`int` (see :ref:`typesnumeric`). In"}, {"line": 890, "text": "many numeric contexts, ``False`` and ``True`` behave like the integers 0 and 1, respectively."}, {"line": 891, "text": "However, relying on this is discouraged; explicitly convert using :func:`int`"}, {"line": 892, "text": "instead."}, {"line": 893, "text": ""}, {"line": 894, "text": ".. _typeiter:"}, {"line": 895, "text": ""}, {"line": 896, "text": "Iterator Types"}, {"line": 897, "text": "=============="}, {"line": 898, "text": ""}, {"line": 899, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 900, "text": "   single: iterator protocol"}, {"line": 901, "text": "   single: protocol; iterator"}, {"line": 902, "text": "   single: sequence; iteration"}, {"line": 903, "text": "   single: container; iteration over"}, {"line": 904, "text": ""}, {"line": 905, "text": "Python supports a concept of iteration over containers.  This is implemented"}, {"line": 906, "text": "using two distinct methods; these are used to allow user-defined classes to"}, {"line": 907, "text": "support iteration.  Sequences, described below in more detail, always support"}, {"line": 908, "text": "the iteration methods."}, {"line": 909, "text": ""}, {"line": 910, "text": "One method needs to be defined for container objects to provide :term:`iterable`"}, {"line": 911, "text": "support:"}, {"line": 912, "text": ""}, {"line": 913, "text": ".. XXX duplicated in reference/datamodel!"}, {"line": 914, "text": ""}, {"line": 915, "text": ".. method:: container.__iter__()"}, {"line": 916, "text": ""}, {"line": 917, "text": "   Return an :term:`iterator` object.  The object is required to support the"}, {"line": 918, "text": "   iterator protocol described below.  If a container supports different types"}, {"line": 919, "text": "   of iteration, additional methods can be provided to specifically request"}, {"line": 920, "text": "   iterators for those iteration types.  (An example of an object supporting"}, {"line": 921, "text": "   multiple forms of iteration would be a tree structure which supports both"}, {"line": 922, "text": "   breadth-first and depth-first traversal.)  This method corresponds to the"}, {"line": 923, "text": "   :c:member:`~PyTypeObject.tp_iter` slot of the type structure for Python"}, {"line": 924, "text": "   objects in the Python/C API."}, {"line": 925, "text": ""}, {"line": 926, "text": "The iterator objects themselves are required to support the following two"}, {"line": 927, "text": "methods, which together form the :dfn:`iterator protocol`:"}, {"line": 928, "text": ""}, {"line": 929, "text": ""}, {"line": 930, "text": ".. method:: iterator.__iter__()"}, {"line": 931, "text": ""}, {"line": 932, "text": "   Return the :term:`iterator` object itself.  This is required to allow both"}, {"line": 933, "text": "   containers and iterators to be used with the :keyword:`for` and"}, {"line": 934, "text": "   :keyword:`in` statements.  This method corresponds to the"}, {"line": 935, "text": "   :c:member:`~PyTypeObject.tp_iter` slot of the type structure for Python"}, {"line": 936, "text": "   objects in the Python/C API."}, {"line": 937, "text": ""}, {"line": 938, "text": ""}, {"line": 939, "text": ".. method:: iterator.__next__()"}, {"line": 940, "text": ""}, {"line": 941, "text": "   Return the next item from the :term:`iterator`.  If there are no further"}, {"line": 942, "text": "   items, raise the :exc:`StopIteration` exception.  This method corresponds to"}, {"line": 943, "text": "   the :c:member:`~PyTypeObject.tp_iternext` slot of the type structure for"}, {"line": 944, "text": "   Python objects in the Python/C API."}, {"line": 945, "text": ""}, {"line": 946, "text": "Python defines several iterator objects to support iteration over general and"}, {"line": 947, "text": "specific sequence types, dictionaries, and other more specialized forms.  The"}, {"line": 948, "text": "specific types are not important beyond their implementation of the iterator"}, {"line": 949, "text": "protocol."}, {"line": 950, "text": ""}, {"line": 951, "text": "Once an iterator's :meth:`~iterator.__next__` method raises"}, {"line": 952, "text": ":exc:`StopIteration`, it must continue to do so on subsequent calls."}, {"line": 953, "text": "Implementations that do not obey this property are deemed broken."}, {"line": 954, "text": ""}, {"line": 955, "text": ""}, {"line": 956, "text": ".. _generator-types:"}, {"line": 957, "text": ""}, {"line": 958, "text": "Generator Types"}, {"line": 959, "text": "---------------"}, {"line": 960, "text": ""}, {"line": 961, "text": "Python's :term:`generator`\\s provide a convenient way to implement the iterator"}, {"line": 962, "text": "protocol.  If a container object's :meth:`~object.__iter__` method is implemented as a"}, {"line": 963, "text": "generator, it will automatically return an iterator object (technically, a"}, {"line": 964, "text": "generator object) supplying the :meth:`~iterator.__iter__` and :meth:`~generator.__next__`"}, {"line": 965, "text": "methods."}, {"line": 966, "text": "More information about generators can be found in :ref:`the documentation for"}, {"line": 967, "text": "the yield expression <yieldexpr>`."}, {"line": 968, "text": ""}, {"line": 969, "text": ""}, {"line": 970, "text": ".. _typesseq:"}, {"line": 971, "text": ""}, {"line": 972, "text": "Sequence Types --- :class:`list`, :class:`tuple`, :class:`range`"}, {"line": 973, "text": "================================================================"}, {"line": 974, "text": ""}, {"line": 975, "text": "There are three basic sequence types: lists, tuples, and range objects."}, {"line": 976, "text": "Additional sequence types tailored for processing of"}, {"line": 977, "text": ":ref:`binary data <binaryseq>` and :ref:`text strings <textseq>` are"}, {"line": 978, "text": "described in dedicated sections."}, {"line": 979, "text": ""}, {"line": 980, "text": ""}, {"line": 981, "text": ".. _typesseq-common:"}, {"line": 982, "text": ""}, {"line": 983, "text": "Common Sequence Operations"}, {"line": 984, "text": "--------------------------"}, {"line": 985, "text": ""}, {"line": 986, "text": ".. index:: pair: object; sequence"}, {"line": 987, "text": ""}, {"line": 988, "text": "The operations in the following table are supported by most sequence types,"}, {"line": 989, "text": "both mutable and immutable. The :class:`collections.abc.Sequence` ABC is"}, {"line": 990, "text": "provided to make it easier to correctly implement these operations on"}, {"line": 991, "text": "custom sequence types."}, {"line": 992, "text": ""}, {"line": 993, "text": "This table lists the sequence operations sorted in ascending priority.  In the"}, {"line": 994, "text": "table, *s* and *t* are sequences of the same type, *n*, *i*, *j* and *k* are"}, {"line": 995, "text": "integers and *x* is an arbitrary object that meets any type and value"}, {"line": 996, "text": "restrictions imposed by *s*."}, {"line": 997, "text": ""}, {"line": 998, "text": "The ``in`` and ``not in`` operations have the same priorities as the"}, {"line": 999, "text": "comparison operations. The ``+`` (concatenation) and ``*`` (repetition)"}, {"line": 1000, "text": "operations have the same priority as the corresponding numeric operations. [3]_"}, {"line": 1001, "text": ""}, {"line": 1002, "text": "See :ref:`time-complexity` for the costs of the various sequence"}, {"line": 1003, "text": "operations."}, {"line": 1004, "text": ""}, {"line": 1005, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 1006, "text": "   triple: operations on; sequence; types"}, {"line": 1007, "text": "   pair: built-in function; len"}, {"line": 1008, "text": "   pair: built-in function; min"}, {"line": 1009, "text": "   pair: built-in function; max"}, {"line": 1010, "text": "   pair: concatenation; operation"}, {"line": 1011, "text": "   pair: repetition; operation"}, {"line": 1012, "text": "   pair: subscript; operation"}, {"line": 1013, "text": "   pair: slice; operation"}, {"line": 1014, "text": "   pair: operator; in"}, {"line": 1015, "text": "   pair: operator; not in"}, {"line": 1016, "text": ""}, {"line": 1017, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1018, "text": "| Operation                | Result                         | Notes    |"}, {"line": 1019, "text": "+==========================+================================+==========+"}, {"line": 1020, "text": "| ``x in s``               | ``True`` if an item of *s* is  | \\(1)     |"}, {"line": 1021, "text": "|                          | equal to *x*, else ``False``   |          |"}, {"line": 1022, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1023, "text": "| ``x not in s``           | ``False`` if an item of *s* is | \\(1)     |"}, {"line": 1024, "text": "|                          | equal to *x*, else ``True``    |          |"}, {"line": 1025, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1026, "text": "| ``s + t``                | the concatenation of *s* and   | (6)(7)   |"}, {"line": 1027, "text": "|                          | *t*                            |          |"}, {"line": 1028, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1029, "text": "| ``s * n`` or             | equivalent to adding *s* to    | (2)(7)   |"}, {"line": 1030, "text": "| ``n * s``                | itself *n* times               |          |"}, {"line": 1031, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1032, "text": "| ``s[i]``                 | *i*\\ th item of *s*, origin 0  | (3)(8)   |"}, {"line": 1033, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1034, "text": "| ``s[i:j]``               | slice of *s* from *i* to *j*   | (3)(4)   |"}, {"line": 1035, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1036, "text": "| ``s[i:j:k]``             | slice of *s* from *i* to *j*   | (3)(5)   |"}, {"line": 1037, "text": "|                          | with step *k*                  |          |"}, {"line": 1038, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1039, "text": "| ``len(s)``               | length of *s*                  |          |"}, {"line": 1040, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1041, "text": "| ``min(s)``               | smallest item of *s*           |          |"}, {"line": 1042, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1043, "text": "| ``max(s)``               | largest item of *s*            |          |"}, {"line": 1044, "text": "+--------------------------+--------------------------------+----------+"}, {"line": 1045, "text": ""}, {"line": 1046, "text": "Sequences of the same type also support comparisons.  In particular, tuples"}, {"line": 1047, "text": "and lists are compared lexicographically by comparing corresponding elements."}, {"line": 1048, "text": "This means that to compare equal, every element must compare equal and the"}, {"line": 1049, "text": "two sequences must be of the same type and have the same length.  (For full"}, {"line": 1050, "text": "details see :ref:`comparisons` in the language reference.)"}, {"line": 1051, "text": ""}, {"line": 1052, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 1053, "text": "   single: loop; over mutable sequence"}, {"line": 1054, "text": "   single: mutable sequence; loop over"}, {"line": 1055, "text": ""}, {"line": 1056, "text": "Forward and reversed iterators over mutable sequences access values using an"}, {"line": 1057, "text": "index.  That index will continue to march forward (or backward) even if the"}, {"line": 1058, "text": "underlying sequence is mutated.  The iterator terminates only when an"}, {"line": 1059, "text": ":exc:`IndexError` or a :exc:`StopIteration` is encountered (or when the index"}, {"line": 1060, "text": "drops below zero)."}, {"line": 1061, "text": ""}, {"line": 1062, "text": "Notes:"}, {"line": 1063, "text": ""}, {"line": 1064, "text": "(1)"}, {"line": 1065, "text": "   While the ``in`` and ``not in`` operations are used only for simple"}, {"line": 1066, "text": "   containment testing in the general case, some specialised sequences"}, {"line": 1067, "text": "   (such as :class:`str`, :class:`bytes` and :class:`bytearray`) also use"}, {"line": 1068, "text": "   them for subsequence testing::"}, {"line": 1069, "text": ""}, {"line": 1070, "text": "      >>> \"gg\" in \"eggs\""}, {"line": 1071, "text": "      True"}, {"line": 1072, "text": ""}, {"line": 1073, "text": "(2)"}, {"line": 1074, "text": "   Values of *n* less than ``0`` are treated as ``0`` (which yields an empty"}, {"line": 1075, "text": "   sequence of the same type as *s*).  Note that items in the sequence *s*"}, {"line": 1076, "text": "   are not copied; they are referenced multiple times.  This often haunts"}, {"line": 1077, "text": "   new Python programmers; consider::"}, {"line": 1078, "text": ""}, {"line": 1079, "text": "      >>> lists = [[]] * 3"}, {"line": 1080, "text": "      >>> lists"}, {"line": 1081, "text": "      [[], [], []]"}, {"line": 1082, "text": "      >>> lists[0].append(3)"}, {"line": 1083, "text": "      >>> lists"}, {"line": 1084, "text": "      [[3], [3], [3]]"}, {"line": 1085, "text": ""}, {"line": 1086, "text": "   What has happened is that ``[[]]`` is a one-element list containing an empty"}, {"line": 1087, "text": "   list, so all three elements of ``[[]] * 3`` are references to this single empty"}, {"line": 1088, "text": "   list.  Modifying any of the elements of ``lists`` modifies this single list."}, {"line": 1089, "text": "   You can create a list of different lists this way::"}, {"line": 1090, "text": ""}, {"line": 1091, "text": "      >>> lists = [[] for i in range(3)]"}, {"line": 1092, "text": "      >>> lists[0].append(3)"}, {"line": 1093, "text": "      >>> lists[1].append(5)"}, {"line": 1094, "text": "      >>> lists[2].append(7)"}, {"line": 1095, "text": "      >>> lists"}, {"line": 1096, "text": "      [[3], [5], [7]]"}, {"line": 1097, "text": ""}, {"line": 1098, "text": "   Further explanation is available in the FAQ entry"}, {"line": 1099, "text": "   :ref:`faq-multidimensional-list`."}, {"line": 1100, "text": ""}, {"line": 1101, "text": "(3)"}, {"line": 1102, "text": "   If *i* or *j* is negative, the index is relative to the end of sequence *s*:"}, {"line": 1103, "text": "   ``len(s) + i`` or ``len(s) + j`` is substituted.  But note that ``-0`` is"}, {"line": 1104, "text": "   still ``0``."}, {"line": 1105, "text": ""}, {"line": 1106, "text": "(4)"}, {"line": 1107, "text": "   The slice of *s* from *i* to *j* is defined as the sequence of items with"}, {"line": 1108, "text": "   index *k* such that ``i <= k < j``."}, {"line": 1109, "text": ""}, {"line": 1110, "text": "   * If *i* is omitted or ``None``, use ``0``."}, {"line": 1111, "text": "   * If *j* is omitted or ``None``, use ``len(s)``."}, {"line": 1112, "text": "   * If *i* or *j* is less than ``-len(s)``, use ``0``."}, {"line": 1113, "text": "   * If *i* or *j* is greater than ``len(s)``, use ``len(s)``."}, {"line": 1114, "text": "   * If *i* is greater than or equal to *j*, the slice is empty."}, {"line": 1115, "text": ""}, {"line": 1116, "text": "(5)"}, {"line": 1117, "text": "   The slice of *s* from *i* to *j* with step *k* is defined as the sequence of"}, {"line": 1118, "text": "   items with index  ``x = i + n*k`` such that ``0 <= n < (j-i)/k``.  In other words,"}, {"line": 1119, "text": "   the indices are ``i``, ``i+k``, ``i+2*k``, ``i+3*k`` and so on, stopping when"}, {"line": 1120, "text": "   *j* is reached (but never including *j*).  When *k* is positive,"}, {"line": 1121, "text": "   *i* and *j* are reduced to ``len(s)`` if they are greater."}, {"line": 1122, "text": "   When *k* is negative, *i* and *j* are reduced to ``len(s) - 1`` if"}, {"line": 1123, "text": "   they are greater.  If *i* or *j* are omitted or ``None``, they become"}, {"line": 1124, "text": "   \"end\" values (which end depends on the sign of *k*).  Note, *k* cannot be zero."}, {"line": 1125, "text": "   If *k* is ``None``, it is treated like ``1``."}, {"line": 1126, "text": ""}, {"line": 1127, "text": ".. _typesseq-repeated-concatenation:"}, {"line": 1128, "text": ""}, {"line": 1129, "text": "(6)"}, {"line": 1130, "text": "   Concatenating immutable sequences always results in a new object.  This"}, {"line": 1131, "text": "   means that building up a sequence by repeated concatenation will have a"}, {"line": 1132, "text": "   quadratic runtime cost in the total sequence length.  To get a linear"}, {"line": 1133, "text": "   runtime cost, you must switch to one of the alternatives below:"}, {"line": 1134, "text": ""}, {"line": 1135, "text": "   * if concatenating :class:`str` objects, you can build a list and use"}, {"line": 1136, "text": "     :meth:`str.join` at the end or else write to an :class:`io.StringIO`"}, {"line": 1137, "text": "     instance and retrieve its value when complete"}, {"line": 1138, "text": ""}, {"line": 1139, "text": "   * if concatenating :class:`bytes` objects, you can similarly use"}, {"line": 1140, "text": "     :meth:`bytes.join` or :class:`io.BytesIO`, or you can do in-place"}, {"line": 1141, "text": "     concatenation with a :class:`bytearray` object.  :class:`bytearray`"}, {"line": 1142, "text": "     objects are mutable and have an efficient overallocation mechanism"}, {"line": 1143, "text": ""}, {"line": 1144, "text": "   * if concatenating :class:`tuple` objects, extend a :class:`list` instead"}, {"line": 1145, "text": ""}, {"line": 1146, "text": "   * for other types, investigate the relevant class documentation"}, {"line": 1147, "text": ""}, {"line": 1148, "text": ""}, {"line": 1149, "text": "(7)"}, {"line": 1150, "text": "  Some sequence types (such as :class:`range`) only support item sequences"}, {"line": 1151, "text": "  that follow specific patterns, and hence don't support sequence"}, {"line": 1152, "text": "  concatenation or repetition."}, {"line": 1153, "text": ""}, {"line": 1154, "text": "(8)"}, {"line": 1155, "text": "   An :exc:`IndexError` is raised if *i* is outside the sequence range."}, {"line": 1156, "text": ""}, {"line": 1157, "text": ".. rubric:: Sequence Methods"}, {"line": 1158, "text": ""}, {"line": 1159, "text": "Sequence types also support the following methods:"}, {"line": 1160, "text": ""}, {"line": 1161, "text": ".. method:: list.count(value, /)"}, {"line": 1162, "text": "            range.count(value, /)"}, {"line": 1163, "text": "            tuple.count(value, /)"}, {"line": 1164, "text": "   :no-contents-entry:"}, {"line": 1165, "text": "   :no-index-entry:"}, {"line": 1166, "text": "   :no-typesetting:"}, {"line": 1167, "text": ".. method:: sequence.count(value, /)"}, {"line": 1168, "text": ""}, {"line": 1169, "text": "   Return the total number of occurrences of *value* in *sequence*."}, {"line": 1170, "text": ""}, {"line": 1171, "text": ".. method:: list.index(value[, start[, stop]])"}, {"line": 1172, "text": "            range.index(value[, start[, stop]])"}, {"line": 1173, "text": "            tuple.index(value[, start[, stop]])"}, {"line": 1174, "text": "   :no-contents-entry:"}, {"line": 1175, "text": "   :no-index-entry:"}, {"line": 1176, "text": "   :no-typesetting:"}, {"line": 1177, "text": ".. method:: sequence.index(value[, start[, stop]])"}, {"line": 1178, "text": ""}, {"line": 1179, "text": "   Return the index of the first occurrence of *value* in *sequence*."}, {"line": 1180, "text": ""}, {"line": 1181, "text": "   Raises :exc:`ValueError` if *value* is not found in *sequence*."}, {"line": 1182, "text": ""}, {"line": 1183, "text": "   The *start* or *stop* arguments allow for efficient searching"}, {"line": 1184, "text": "   of subsections of the sequence, beginning at *start* and ending at *stop*."}, {"line": 1185, "text": "   This is roughly equivalent to ``start + sequence[start:stop].index(value)``,"}, {"line": 1186, "text": "   only without copying any data."}, {"line": 1187, "text": ""}, {"line": 1188, "text": "   .. caution::"}, {"line": 1189, "text": "      Not all sequence types support passing the *start* and *stop* arguments."}, {"line": 1190, "text": ""}, {"line": 1191, "text": ""}, {"line": 1192, "text": ".. _typesseq-immutable:"}, {"line": 1193, "text": ""}, {"line": 1194, "text": "Immutable Sequence Types"}, {"line": 1195, "text": "------------------------"}, {"line": 1196, "text": ""}, {"line": 1197, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 1198, "text": "   triple: immutable; sequence; types"}, {"line": 1199, "text": "   pair: object; tuple"}, {"line": 1200, "text": "   pair: built-in function; hash"}, {"line": 1201, "text": ""}, {"line": 1202, "text": "The only operation that immutable sequence types generally implement that is"}, {"line": 1203, "text": "not also implemented by mutable sequence types is support for the :func:`hash`"}, {"line": 1204, "text": "built-in."}, {"line": 1205, "text": ""}, {"line": 1206, "text": "This support allows immutable sequences, such as :class:`tuple` instances, to"}, {"line": 1207, "text": "be used as :class:`dict` keys and stored in :class:`set` and :class:`frozenset`"}, {"line": 1208, "text": "instances."}, {"line": 1209, "text": ""}, {"line": 1210, "text": "Attempting to hash an immutable sequence that contains unhashable values will"}, {"line": 1211, "text": "result in :exc:`TypeError`."}, {"line": 1212, "text": ""}, {"line": 1213, "text": ""}, {"line": 1214, "text": ".. _typesseq-mutable:"}, {"line": 1215, "text": ""}, {"line": 1216, "text": "Mutable Sequence Types"}, {"line": 1217, "text": "----------------------"}, {"line": 1218, "text": ""}, {"line": 1219, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 1220, "text": "   triple: mutable; sequence; types"}, {"line": 1221, "text": "   pair: object; list"}, {"line": 1222, "text": "   pair: object; bytearray"}, {"line": 1223, "text": ""}, {"line": 1224, "text": "The operations in the following table are defined on mutable sequence types."}, {"line": 1225, "text": "The :class:`collections.abc.MutableSequence` ABC is provided to make it"}, {"line": 1226, "text": "easier to correctly implement these operations on custom sequence types."}, {"line": 1227, "text": ""}, {"line": 1228, "text": "In the table *s* is an instance of a mutable sequence type, *t* is any"}, {"line": 1229, "text": "iterable object and *x* is an arbitrary object that meets any type"}, {"line": 1230, "text": "and value restrictions imposed by *s* (for example, :class:`bytearray` only"}, {"line": 1231, "text": "accepts integers that meet the value restriction ``0 <= x <= 255``)."}, {"line": 1232, "text": ""}, {"line": 1233, "text": ""}, {"line": 1234, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 1235, "text": "   triple: operations on; sequence; types"}, {"line": 1236, "text": "   triple: operations on; list; type"}, {"line": 1237, "text": "   pair: subscript; assignment"}, {"line": 1238, "text": "   pair: slice; assignment"}, {"line": 1239, "text": "   pair: statement; del"}, {"line": 1240, "text": ""}, {"line": 1241, "text": "+------------------------------+--------------------------------+---------------------+"}, {"line": 1242, "text": "| Operation                    | Result                         | Notes               |"}, {"line": 1243, "text": "+==============================+================================+=====================+"}, {"line": 1244, "text": "| ``s[i] = x``                 | item *i* of *s* is replaced by |                     |"}, {"line": 1245, "text": "|                              | *x*                            |                     |"}, {"line": 1246, "text": "+------------------------------+--------------------------------+---------------------+"}, {"line": 1247, "text": "| ``del s[i]``                 | removes item *i* of *s*        |                     |"}, {"line": 1248, "text": "+------------------------------+--------------------------------+---------------------+"}, {"line": 1249, "text": "| ``s[i:j] = t``               | slice of *s* from *i* to *j*   |                     |"}, {"line": 1250, "text": "|                              | is replaced by the contents of |                     |"}, {"line": 1251, "text": "|                              | the iterable *t*               |                     |"}, {"line": 1252, "text": "+------------------------------+--------------------------------+---------------------+"}, {"line": 1253,
