If *maxsplit* is given, at most *maxsplit* splits are done (thus,"}, {"line": 2629, "text": "   the list will have at most ``maxsplit+1`` elements).  If *maxsplit* is not"}, {"line": 2630, "text": "   specified or ``-1``, then there is no limit on the number of splits"}, {"line": 2631, "text": "   (all possible splits are made)."}, {"line": 2632, "text": ""}, {"line": 2633, "text": "   If *sep* is given, consecutive delimiters are not grouped together and are"}, {"line": 2634, "text": "   deemed to delimit empty strings (for example, ``'1,,2'.split(',')`` returns"}, {"line": 2635, "text": "   ``['1', '', '2']``).  The *sep* argument may consist of multiple characters"}, {"line": 2636, "text": "   as a single delimiter (to split with multiple delimiters, use"}, {"line": 2637, "text": "   :func:`re.split`). Splitting an empty string with a specified separator"}, {"line": 2638, "text": "   returns ``['']``."}, {"line": 2639, "text": ""}, {"line": 2640, "text": "   For example:"}, {"line": 2641, "text": ""}, {"line": 2642, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 2643, "text": ""}, {"line": 2644, "text": "      >>> '1,2,3'.split(',')"}, {"line": 2645, "text": "      ['1', '2', '3']"}, {"line": 2646, "text": "      >>> '1,2,3'.split(',', maxsplit=1)"}, {"line": 2647, "text": "      ['1', '2,3']"}, {"line": 2648, "text": "      >>> '1,2,,3,'.split(',')"}, {"line": 2649, "text": "      ['1', '2', '', '3', '']"}, {"line": 2650, "text": "      >>> '1<>2<>3<4'.split('<>')"}, {"line": 2651, "text": "      ['1', '2', '3<4']"}, {"line": 2652, "text": ""}, {"line": 2653, "text": "   If *sep* is not specified or is ``None``, a different splitting algorithm is"}, {"line": 2654, "text": "   applied: runs of consecutive :meth:`whitespace <str.isspace>` are regarded"}, {"line": 2655, "text": "   as a single separator,"}, {"line": 2656, "text": "   and the result will contain no empty strings at the start or end if the"}, {"line": 2657, "text": "   string has leading or trailing whitespace.  Consequently, splitting an empty"}, {"line": 2658, "text": "   string or a string consisting of just whitespace with a ``None`` separator"}, {"line": 2659, "text": "   returns ``[]``."}, {"line": 2660, "text": ""}, {"line": 2661, "text": "   For example:"}, {"line": 2662, "text": ""}, {"line": 2663, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 2664, "text": ""}, {"line": 2665, "text": "      >>> '1 2 3'.split()"}, {"line": 2666, "text": "      ['1', '2', '3']"}, {"line": 2667, "text": "      >>> '1 2 3'.split(maxsplit=1)"}, {"line": 2668, "text": "      ['1', '2 3']"}, {"line": 2669, "text": "      >>> '   1   2   3   '.split()"}, {"line": 2670, "text": "      ['1', '2', '3']"}, {"line": 2671, "text": ""}, {"line": 2672, "text": "   If *sep* is not specified or is ``None`` and  *maxsplit* is ``0``, only"}, {"line": 2673, "text": "   leading runs of consecutive whitespace are considered."}, {"line": 2674, "text": ""}, {"line": 2675, "text": "   For example:"}, {"line": 2676, "text": ""}, {"line": 2677, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 2678, "text": ""}, {"line": 2679, "text": "      >>> \"\".split(None, 0)"}, {"line": 2680, "text": "      []"}, {"line": 2681, "text": "      >>> \"   \".split(None, 0)"}, {"line": 2682, "text": "      []"}, {"line": 2683, "text": "      >>> \"   foo   \".split(maxsplit=0)"}, {"line": 2684, "text": "      ['foo   ']"}, {"line": 2685, "text": ""}, {"line": 2686, "text": "   See also :meth:`join` and :meth:`rsplit`."}, {"line": 2687, "text": ""}, {"line": 2688, "text": ""}, {"line": 2689, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 2690, "text": "   single: universal newlines; str.splitlines method"}, {"line": 2691, "text": ""}, {"line": 2692, "text": ".. method:: str.splitlines(keepends=False)"}, {"line": 2693, "text": ""}, {"line": 2694, "text": "   Return a list of the lines in the string, breaking at line boundaries.  Line"}, {"line": 2695, "text": "   breaks are not included in the resulting list unless *keepends* is given and"}, {"line": 2696, "text": "   true."}, {"line": 2697, "text": ""}, {"line": 2698, "text": "   This method splits on the following line boundaries.  In particular, the"}, {"line": 2699, "text": "   boundaries are a superset of :term:`universal newlines`."}, {"line": 2700, "text": ""}, {"line": 2701, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2702, "text": "   | Representation        | Description                 |"}, {"line": 2703, "text": "   +=======================+=============================+"}, {"line": 2704, "text": "   | ``\\n``                | Line Feed                   |"}, {"line": 2705, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2706, "text": "   | ``\\r``                | Carriage Return             |"}, {"line": 2707, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2708, "text": "   | ``\\r\\n``              | Carriage Return + Line Feed |"}, {"line": 2709, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2710, "text": "   | ``\\v`` or ``\\x0b``    | Line Tabulation             |"}, {"line": 2711, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2712, "text": "   | ``\\f`` or ``\\x0c``    | Form Feed                   |"}, {"line": 2713, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2714, "text": "   | ``\\x1c``              | File Separator              |"}, {"line": 2715, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2716, "text": "   | ``\\x1d``              | Group Separator             |"}, {"line": 2717, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2718, "text": "   | ``\\x1e``              | Record Separator            |"}, {"line": 2719, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2720, "text": "   | ``\\x85``              | Next Line (C1 Control Code) |"}, {"line": 2721, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2722, "text": "   | ``\\u2028``            | Line Separator              |"}, {"line": 2723, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2724, "text": "   | ``\\u2029``            | Paragraph Separator         |"}, {"line": 2725, "text": "   +-----------------------+-----------------------------+"}, {"line": 2726, "text": ""}, {"line": 2727, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.2"}, {"line": 2728, "text": ""}, {"line": 2729, "text": "      ``\\v`` and ``\\f`` added to list of line boundaries."}, {"line": 2730, "text": ""}, {"line": 2731, "text": "   For example::"}, {"line": 2732, "text": ""}, {"line": 2733, "text": "      >>> 'ab c\\n\\nde fg\\rkl\\r\\n'.splitlines()"}, {"line": 2734, "text": "      ['ab c', '', 'de fg', 'kl']"}, {"line": 2735, "text": "      >>> 'ab c\\n\\nde fg\\rkl\\r\\n'.splitlines(keepends=True)"}, {"line": 2736, "text": "      ['ab c\\n', '\\n', 'de fg\\r', 'kl\\r\\n']"}, {"line": 2737, "text": ""}, {"line": 2738, "text": "   Unlike :meth:`~str.split` when a delimiter string *sep* is given, this"}, {"line": 2739, "text": "   method returns an empty list for the empty string, and a terminal line"}, {"line": 2740, "text": "   break does not result in an extra line::"}, {"line": 2741, "text": ""}, {"line": 2742, "text": "      >>> \"\".splitlines()"}, {"line": 2743, "text": "      []"}, {"line": 2744, "text": "      >>> \"One line\\n\".splitlines()"}, {"line": 2745, "text": "      ['One line']"}, {"line": 2746, "text": ""}, {"line": 2747, "text": "   For comparison, ``split('\\n')`` gives::"}, {"line": 2748, "text": ""}, {"line": 2749, "text": "      >>> ''.split('\\n')"}, {"line": 2750, "text": "      ['']"}, {"line": 2751, "text": "      >>> 'Two lines\\n'.split('\\n')"}, {"line": 2752, "text": "      ['Two lines', '']"}, {"line": 2753, "text": ""}, {"line": 2754, "text": ""}, {"line": 2755, "text": ".. method:: str.startswith(prefix[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 2756, "text": ""}, {"line": 2757, "text": "   Return ``True`` if string starts with the *prefix*, otherwise return ``False``."}, {"line": 2758, "text": "   *prefix* can also be a tuple of prefixes to look for.  With optional *start*,"}, {"line": 2759, "text": "   test string beginning at that position.  With optional *end*, stop comparing"}, {"line": 2760, "text": "   string at that position."}, {"line": 2761, "text": ""}, {"line": 2762, "text": "   For example:"}, {"line": 2763, "text": ""}, {"line": 2764, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 2765, "text": ""}, {"line": 2766, "text": "      >>> 'Python'.startswith('Py')"}, {"line": 2767, "text": "      True"}, {"line": 2768, "text": "      >>> 'a tuple of prefixes'.startswith(('at', 'a'))"}, {"line": 2769, "text": "      True"}, {"line": 2770, "text": "      >>> 'Python is amazing'.startswith('is', 7)"}, {"line": 2771, "text": "      True"}, {"line": 2772, "text": ""}, {"line": 2773, "text": "   See also :meth:`endswith` and :meth:`removeprefix`."}, {"line": 2774, "text": ""}, {"line": 2775, "text": ""}, {"line": 2776, "text": ".. method:: str.strip(chars=None, /)"}, {"line": 2777, "text": ""}, {"line": 2778, "text": "   Return a copy of the string with the leading and trailing characters removed."}, {"line": 2779, "text": "   The *chars* argument is a string specifying the set of characters to be removed."}, {"line": 2780, "text": "   If omitted or ``None``, the *chars* argument defaults to removing whitespace."}, {"line": 2781, "text": "   The *chars* argument is not a prefix or suffix; rather, all combinations of its"}, {"line": 2782, "text": "   values are stripped."}, {"line": 2783, "text": ""}, {"line": 2784, "text": "   Whitespace characters are defined by :meth:`str.isspace`."}, {"line": 2785, "text": ""}, {"line": 2786, "text": "   For example:"}, {"line": 2787, "text": ""}, {"line": 2788, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 2789, "text": ""}, {"line": 2790, "text": "      >>> '   spacious   '.strip()"}, {"line": 2791, "text": "      'spacious'"}, {"line": 2792, "text": "      >>> 'www.example.com'.strip('cmowz.')"}, {"line": 2793, "text": "      'example'"}, {"line": 2794, "text": ""}, {"line": 2795, "text": "   The outermost leading and trailing *chars* argument values are stripped"}, {"line": 2796, "text": "   from the string. Characters are removed from the leading end until"}, {"line": 2797, "text": "   reaching a string character that is not contained in the set of"}, {"line": 2798, "text": "   characters in *chars*. A similar action takes place on the trailing end."}, {"line": 2799, "text": ""}, {"line": 2800, "text": "   For example:"}, {"line": 2801, "text": ""}, {"line": 2802, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 2803, "text": ""}, {"line": 2804, "text": "      >>> comment_string = '#....... Section 3.2.1 Issue #32 .......'"}, {"line": 2805, "text": "      >>> comment_string.strip('.#! ')"}, {"line": 2806, "text": "      'Section 3.2.1 Issue #32'"}, {"line": 2807, "text": ""}, {"line": 2808, "text": "   See also :meth:`rstrip`."}, {"line": 2809, "text": ""}, {"line": 2810, "text": ""}, {"line": 2811, "text": ".. method:: str.swapcase()"}, {"line": 2812, "text": ""}, {"line": 2813, "text": "   Return a copy of the string with uppercase characters converted to lowercase and"}, {"line": 2814, "text": "   vice versa. For example:"}, {"line": 2815, "text": ""}, {"line": 2816, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 2817, "text": ""}, {"line": 2818, "text": "      >>> 'Hello World'.swapcase()"}, {"line": 2819, "text": "      'hELLO wORLD'"}, {"line": 2820, "text": ""}, {"line": 2821, "text": "   Note that it is not necessarily true that ``s.swapcase().swapcase() == s``."}, {"line": 2822, "text": "   For example:"}, {"line": 2823, "text": ""}, {"line": 2824, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 2825, "text": ""}, {"line": 2826, "text": "      >>> 'straße'.swapcase().swapcase()"}, {"line": 2827, "text": "      'strasse'"}, {"line": 2828, "text": ""}, {"line": 2829, "text": "   See also :meth:`str.lower` and :meth:`str.upper`."}, {"line": 2830, "text": ""}, {"line": 2831, "text": ""}, {"line": 2832, "text": ".. method:: str.title()"}, {"line": 2833, "text": ""}, {"line": 2834, "text": "   Return a titlecased version of the string where words start with an uppercase"}, {"line": 2835, "text": "   character and the remaining characters are lowercase."}, {"line": 2836, "text": ""}, {"line": 2837, "text": "   For example::"}, {"line": 2838, "text": ""}, {"line": 2839, "text": "      >>> 'Hello world'.title()"}, {"line": 2840, "text": "      'Hello World'"}, {"line": 2841, "text": ""}, {"line": 2842, "text": "   The algorithm uses a simple language-independent definition of a word as"}, {"line": 2843, "text": "   groups of consecutive letters.  The definition works in many contexts but"}, {"line": 2844, "text": "   it means that apostrophes in contractions and possessives form word"}, {"line": 2845, "text": "   boundaries, which may not be the desired result::"}, {"line": 2846, "text": ""}, {"line": 2847, "text": "        >>> \"they're bill's friends from the UK\".title()"}, {"line": 2848, "text": "        \"They'Re Bill'S Friends From The Uk\""}, {"line": 2849, "text": ""}, {"line": 2850, "text": "   The :func:`string.capwords` function does not have this problem, as it"}, {"line": 2851, "text": "   splits words on spaces only."}, {"line": 2852, "text": ""}, {"line": 2853, "text": "   Alternatively, a workaround for apostrophes can be constructed using regular"}, {"line": 2854, "text": "   expressions::"}, {"line": 2855, "text": ""}, {"line": 2856, "text": "        >>> import re"}, {"line": 2857, "text": "        >>> def titlecase(s):"}, {"line": 2858, "text": "        ...     return re.sub(r\"[A-Za-z]+('[A-Za-z]+)?\","}, {"line": 2859, "text": "        ...                   lambda mo: mo.group(0).capitalize(),"}, {"line": 2860, "text": "        ...                   s)"}, {"line": 2861, "text": "        ..."}, {"line": 2862, "text": "        >>> titlecase(\"they're bill's friends.\")"}, {"line": 2863, "text": "        \"They're Bill's Friends.\""}, {"line": 2864, "text": ""}, {"line": 2865, "text": "   See also :meth:`istitle`."}, {"line": 2866, "text": ""}, {"line": 2867, "text": ""}, {"line": 2868, "text": ".. method:: str.translate(table, /)"}, {"line": 2869, "text": ""}, {"line": 2870, "text": "   Return a copy of the string in which each character has been mapped through"}, {"line": 2871, "text": "   the given translation table.  The table must be an object that implements"}, {"line": 2872, "text": "   indexing via :meth:`~object.__getitem__`, typically a :term:`mapping` or"}, {"line": 2873, "text": "   :term:`sequence`.  When indexed by a Unicode ordinal (an integer), the"}, {"line": 2874, "text": "   table object can do any of the following: return a Unicode ordinal or a"}, {"line": 2875, "text": "   string, to map the character to one or more other characters; return"}, {"line": 2876, "text": "   ``None``, to delete the character from the return string; or raise a"}, {"line": 2877, "text": "   :exc:`LookupError` exception, to map the character to itself."}, {"line": 2878, "text": ""}, {"line": 2879, "text": "   You can use :meth:`str.maketrans` to create a translation map from"}, {"line": 2880, "text": "   character-to-character mappings in different formats."}, {"line": 2881, "text": ""}, {"line": 2882, "text": "   See also the :mod:`codecs` module for a more flexible approach to custom"}, {"line": 2883, "text": "   character mappings."}, {"line": 2884, "text": ""}, {"line": 2885, "text": ""}, {"line": 2886, "text": ".. method:: str.upper()"}, {"line": 2887, "text": ""}, {"line": 2888, "text": "   Return a copy of the string with all the cased characters [4]_ converted to"}, {"line": 2889, "text": "   uppercase.  Note that ``s.upper().isupper()`` might be ``False`` if ``s``"}, {"line": 2890, "text": "   contains uncased characters or if the Unicode category of the resulting"}, {"line": 2891, "text": "   character(s) is not \"Lu\" (Letter, uppercase), but e.g. \"Lt\" (Letter,"}, {"line": 2892, "text": "   titlecase)."}, {"line": 2893, "text": ""}, {"line": 2894, "text": "   The uppercasing algorithm used is"}, {"line": 2895, "text": "   `described in section 3.13 'Default Case Folding' of the Unicode Standard"}, {"line": 2896, "text": "   <https://www.unicode.org/versions/Unicode16.0.0/core-spec/chapter-3/#G33992>`__."}, {"line": 2897, "text": ""}, {"line": 2898, "text": ""}, {"line": 2899, "text": ".. method:: str.zfill(width, /)"}, {"line": 2900, "text": ""}, {"line": 2901, "text": "   Return a copy of the string left filled with ASCII ``'0'`` digits to"}, {"line": 2902, "text": "   make a string of length *width*. A leading sign prefix (``'+'``/``'-'``)"}, {"line": 2903, "text": "   is handled by inserting the padding *after* the sign character rather"}, {"line": 2904, "text": "   than before. The original string is returned if *width* is less than"}, {"line": 2905, "text": "   or equal to ``len(s)``."}, {"line": 2906, "text": ""}, {"line": 2907, "text": "   For example:"}, {"line": 2908, "text": ""}, {"line": 2909, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 2910, "text": ""}, {"line": 2911, "text": "      >>> \"42\".zfill(5)"}, {"line": 2912, "text": "      '00042'"}, {"line": 2913, "text": "      >>> \"-42\".zfill(5)"}, {"line": 2914, "text": "      '-0042'"}, {"line": 2915, "text": ""}, {"line": 2916, "text": "   See also :meth:`rjust`."}, {"line": 2917, "text": ""}, {"line": 2918, "text": ""}, {"line": 2919, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 2920, "text": "   single: ! formatted string literal"}, {"line": 2921, "text": "   single: formatted string literals"}, {"line": 2922, "text": "   single: ! f-string"}, {"line": 2923, "text": "   single: f-strings"}, {"line": 2924, "text": "   single: fstring"}, {"line": 2925, "text": "   single: interpolated string literal"}, {"line": 2926, "text": "   single: string; formatted literal"}, {"line": 2927, "text": "   single: string; interpolated literal"}, {"line": 2928, "text": "   single: {} (curly brackets); in formatted string literal"}, {"line": 2929, "text": "   single: ! (exclamation mark); in formatted string literal"}, {"line": 2930, "text": "   single: : (colon); in formatted string literal"}, {"line": 2931, "text": "   single: = (equals); for help in debugging using string literals"}, {"line": 2932, "text": ""}, {"line": 2933, "text": ".. _stdtypes-fstrings:"}, {"line": 2934, "text": ""}, {"line": 2935, "text": "Formatted String Literals (f-strings)"}, {"line": 2936, "text": "-------------------------------------"}, {"line": 2937, "text": ""}, {"line": 2938, "text": ".. versionadded:: 3.6"}, {"line": 2939, "text": ".. versionchanged:: 3.7"}, {"line": 2940, "text": "   The :keyword:`await` and :keyword:`async for` can be used in expressions"}, {"line": 2941, "text": "   within f-strings."}, {"line": 2942, "text": ".. versionchanged:: 3.8"}, {"line": 2943, "text": "   Added the debug specifier (``=``)"}, {"line": 2944, "text": ".. versionchanged:: 3.12"}, {"line": 2945, "text": "   Many restrictions on expressions within f-strings have been removed."}, {"line": 2946, "text": "   Notably, nested strings, comments, and backslashes are now permitted."}, {"line": 2947, "text": ""}, {"line": 2948, "text": "An :dfn:`f-string` (formally a :dfn:`formatted string literal`) is"}, {"line": 2949, "text": "a string literal that is prefixed with ``f`` or ``F``."}, {"line": 2950, "text": "This type of string literal allows embedding the results of arbitrary Python"}, {"line": 2951, "text": "expressions within *replacement fields*, which are delimited by curly"}, {"line": 2952, "text": "brackets (``{}``)."}, {"line": 2953, "text": "Each replacement field must contain an expression, optionally followed by:"}, {"line": 2954, "text": ""}, {"line": 2955, "text": "* a *debug specifier* -- an equal sign (``=``);"}, {"line": 2956, "text": "* a *conversion specifier* -- ``!s``, ``!r`` or ``!a``; and/or"}, {"line": 2957, "text": "* a *format specifier* prefixed with a colon (``:``)."}, {"line": 2958, "text": ""}, {"line": 2959, "text": "See the :ref:`Lexical Analysis section on f-strings <f-strings>` for details"}, {"line": 2960, "text": "on the syntax of these fields."}, {"line": 2961, "text": ""}, {"line": 2962, "text": "Debug specifier"}, {"line": 2963, "text": "^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^"}, {"line": 2964, "text": ""}, {"line": 2965, "text": ".. versionadded:: 3.8"}, {"line": 2966, "text": ""}, {"line": 2967, "text": "If a debug specifier -- an equal sign (``=``) -- appears after the replacement"}, {"line": 2968, "text": "field expression, the resulting f-string will contain the expression's source,"}, {"line": 2969, "text": "the equal sign, and the value of the expression."}, {"line": 2970, "text": "This is often useful for debugging::"}, {"line": 2971, "text": ""}, {"line": 2972, "text": "   >>> number = 14.3"}, {"line": 2973, "text": "   >>> f'{number=}'"}, {"line": 2974, "text": "   'number=14.3'"}, {"line": 2975, "text": ""}, {"line": 2976, "text": "Whitespace before, inside and after the expression, as well as whitespace"}, {"line": 2977, "text": "after the equal sign, is significant --- it is retained in the result::"}, {"line": 2978, "text": ""}, {"line": 2979, "text": "   >>> f'{ number  -  4  = }'"}, {"line": 2980, "text": "   ' number  -  4  = 10.3'"}, {"line": 2981, "text": ""}, {"line": 2982, "text": ""}, {"line": 2983, "text": "Conversion specifier"}, {"line": 2984, "text": "^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^"}, {"line": 2985, "text": ""}, {"line": 2986, "text": "By default, the value of a replacement field expression is converted to"}, {"line": 2987, "text": "a string using :func:`str`::"}, {"line": 2988, "text": ""}, {"line": 2989, "text": "   >>> from fractions import Fraction"}, {"line": 2990, "text": "   >>> one_third = Fraction(1, 3)"}, {"line": 2991, "text": "   >>> f'{one_third}'"}, {"line": 2992, "text": "   '1/3'"}, {"line": 2993, "text": ""}, {"line": 2994, "text": "When a debug specifier but no format specifier is used, the default conversion"}, {"line": 2995, "text": "instead uses :func:`repr`::"}, {"line": 2996, "text": ""}, {"line": 2997, "text": "   >>> f'{one_third = }'"}, {"line": 2998, "text": "   'one_third = Fraction(1, 3)'"}, {"line": 2999, "text": ""}, {"line": 3000, "text": "The conversion can be specified explicitly using one of these specifiers:"}, {"line": 3001, "text": ""}, {"line": 3002, "text": "* ``!s`` for :func:`str`"}, {"line": 3003, "text": "* ``!r`` for :func:`repr`"}, {"line": 3004, "text": "* ``!a`` for :func:`ascii`"}, {"line": 3005, "text": ""}, {"line": 3006, "text": "For example::"}, {"line": 3007, "text": ""}, {"line": 3008, "text": "   >>> str(one_third)"}, {"line": 3009, "text": "   '1/3'"}, {"line": 3010, "text": "   >>> repr(one_third)"}, {"line": 3011, "text": "   'Fraction(1, 3)'"}, {"line": 3012, "text": ""}, {"line": 3013, "text": "   >>> f'{one_third!s} is {one_third!r}'"}, {"line": 3014, "text": "   '1/3 is Fraction(1, 3)'"}, {"line": 3015, "text": ""}, {"line": 3016, "text": "   >>> string = \"¡kočka 😸!\""}, {"line": 3017, "text": "   >>> ascii(string)"}, {"line": 3018, "text": "   \"'\\\\xa1ko\\\\u010dka \\\\U0001f638!'\""}, {"line": 3019, "text": ""}, {"line": 3020, "text": "   >>> f'{string = !a}'"}, {"line": 3021, "text": "   \"string = '\\\\xa1ko\\\\u010dka \\\\U0001f638!'\""}, {"line": 3022, "text": ""}, {"line": 3023, "text": ""}, {"line": 3024, "text": "Format specifier"}, {"line": 3025, "text": "^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^"}, {"line": 3026, "text": ""}, {"line": 3027, "text": "After the expression has been evaluated, and possibly converted using an"}, {"line": 3028, "text": "explicit conversion specifier, it is formatted using the :func:`format` function."}, {"line": 3029, "text": "If the replacement field includes a *format specifier* introduced by a colon"}, {"line": 3030, "text": "(``:``), the specifier is passed to :func:`!format` as the second argument."}, {"line": 3031, "text": "The result of :func:`!format` is then used as the final value for the"}, {"line": 3032, "text": "replacement field. For example::"}, {"line": 3033, "text": ""}, {"line": 3034, "text": "   >>> from fractions import Fraction"}, {"line": 3035, "text": "   >>> one_third = Fraction(1, 3)"}, {"line": 3036, "text": "   >>> f'{one_third:.6f}'"}, {"line": 3037, "text": "   '0.333333'"}, {"line": 3038, "text": "   >>> f'{one_third:_^+10}'"}, {"line": 3039, "text": "   '___+1/3___'"}, {"line": 3040, "text": "   >>> f'{one_third!r:_^20}'"}, {"line": 3041, "text": "   '___Fraction(1, 3)___'"}, {"line": 3042, "text": "   >>> f'{one_third = :~>10}~'"}, {"line": 3043, "text": "   'one_third = ~~~~~~~1/3~'"}, {"line": 3044, "text": ""}, {"line": 3045, "text": ".. _stdtypes-tstrings:"}, {"line": 3046, "text": ""}, {"line": 3047, "text": "Template String Literals (t-strings)"}, {"line": 3048, "text": "------------------------------------"}, {"line": 3049, "text": ""}, {"line": 3050, "text": "A :dfn:`t-string` (formally a :dfn:`template string literal`) is"}, {"line": 3051, "text": "a string literal that is prefixed with ``t`` or ``T``."}, {"line": 3052, "text": ""}, {"line": 3053, "text": "These strings follow the same syntax and evaluation rules as"}, {"line": 3054, "text": ":ref:`formatted string literals <stdtypes-fstrings>`,"}, {"line": 3055, "text": "with the following differences:"}, {"line": 3056, "text": ""}, {"line": 3057, "text": "* Rather than evaluating to a ``str`` object, template string literals evaluate"}, {"line": 3058, "text": "  to a :class:`string.templatelib.Template` object."}, {"line": 3059, "text": ""}, {"line": 3060, "text": "* The :func:`format` protocol is not used."}, {"line": 3061, "text": "  Instead, the format specifier and conversions (if any) are passed to"}, {"line": 3062, "text": "  a new :class:`~string.templatelib.Interpolation` object that is created"}, {"line": 3063, "text": "  for each evaluated expression."}, {"line": 3064, "text": "  It is up to code that processes the resulting :class:`~string.templatelib.Template`"}, {"line": 3065, "text": "  object to decide how to handle format specifiers and conversions."}, {"line": 3066, "text": ""}, {"line": 3067, "text": "* Format specifiers containing nested replacement fields are evaluated eagerly,"}, {"line": 3068, "text": "  prior to being passed to the :class:`~string.templatelib.Interpolation` object."}, {"line": 3069, "text": "  For instance, an interpolation of the form ``{amount:.{precision}f}`` will"}, {"line": 3070, "text": "  evaluate the inner expression ``{precision}`` to determine the value of the"}, {"line": 3071, "text": "  ``format_spec`` attribute."}, {"line": 3072, "text": "  If ``precision`` were to be ``2``, the resulting format specifier"}, {"line": 3073, "text": "  would be ``'.2f'``."}, {"line": 3074, "text": ""}, {"line": 3075, "text": "* When the equals sign ``'='`` is provided in an interpolation expression,"}, {"line": 3076, "text": "  the text of the expression is appended to the literal string that precedes"}, {"line": 3077, "text": "  the relevant interpolation."}, {"line": 3078, "text": "  This includes the equals sign and any surrounding whitespace."}, {"line": 3079, "text": "  The :class:`!Interpolation` instance for the expression will be created as"}, {"line": 3080, "text": "  normal, except that :attr:`~string.templatelib.Interpolation.conversion` will"}, {"line": 3081, "text": "  be set to '``r``' (:func:`repr`) by default."}, {"line": 3082, "text": "  If an explicit conversion or format specifier is provided,"}, {"line": 3083, "text": "  this will override the default behaviour."}, {"line": 3084, "text": ""}, {"line": 3085, "text": ""}, {"line": 3086, "text": ".. _old-string-formatting:"}, {"line": 3087, "text": ""}, {"line": 3088, "text": "``printf``-style String Formatting"}, {"line": 3089, "text": "----------------------------------"}, {"line": 3090, "text": ""}, {"line": 3091, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 3092, "text": "   single: formatting, string (%)"}, {"line": 3093, "text": "   single: interpolation, string (%)"}, {"line": 3094, "text": "   single: string; formatting, printf"}, {"line": 3095, "text": "   single: string; interpolation, printf"}, {"line": 3096, "text": "   single: printf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3097, "text": "   single: sprintf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3098, "text": "   single: % (percent); printf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3099, "text": ""}, {"line": 3100, "text": ".. note::"}, {"line": 3101, "text": ""}, {"line": 3102, "text": "   The formatting operations described here exhibit a variety of quirks that"}, {"line": 3103, "text": "   lead to a number of common errors (such as failing to display tuples and"}, {"line": 3104, "text": "   dictionaries correctly)."}, {"line": 3105, "text": ""}, {"line": 3106, "text": "   Using :ref:`formatted string literals <f-strings>`, the :meth:`str.format`"}, {"line": 3107, "text": "   interface, or :class:`string.Template` may help avoid these errors."}, {"line": 3108, "text": "   Each of these alternatives provides their own trade-offs and benefits of"}, {"line": 3109, "text": "   simplicity, flexibility, and/or extensibility."}, {"line": 3110, "text": ""}, {"line": 3111, "text": "String objects have one unique built-in operation: the ``%`` operator (modulo)."}, {"line": 3112, "text": "This is also known as the string *formatting* or *interpolation* operator."}, {"line": 3113, "text": "Given ``format % values`` (where *format* is a string), ``%`` conversion"}, {"line": 3114, "text": "specifications in *format* are replaced with zero or more elements of *values*."}, {"line": 3115, "text": "The effect is similar to using the :c:func:`sprintf` function in the C language."}, {"line": 3116, "text": "For example:"}, {"line": 3117, "text": ""}, {"line": 3118, "text": ".. doctest::"}, {"line": 3119, "text": ""}, {"line": 3120, "text": "   >>> print('%s has %d quote types.' % ('Python', 2))"}, {"line": 3121, "text": "   Python has 2 quote types."}, {"line": 3122, "text": ""}, {"line": 3123, "text": "If *format* requires a single argument, *values* may be a single non-tuple"}, {"line": 3124, "text": "object. [5]_  Otherwise, *values* must be a tuple with exactly the number of"}, {"line": 3125, "text": "items specified by the format string, or a single mapping object (for example, a"}, {"line": 3126, "text": "dictionary)."}, {"line": 3127, "text": ""}, {"line": 3128, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 3129, "text": "   single: () (parentheses); in printf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3130, "text": "   single: * (asterisk); in printf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3131, "text": "   single: . (dot); in printf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3132, "text": ""}, {"line": 3133, "text": "A conversion specifier contains two or more characters and has the following"}, {"line": 3134, "text": "components, which must occur in this order:"}, {"line": 3135, "text": ""}, {"line": 3136, "text": "#. The ``'%'`` character, which marks the start of the specifier."}, {"line": 3137, "text": ""}, {"line": 3138, "text": "#. Mapping key (optional), consisting of a parenthesised sequence of characters"}, {"line": 3139, "text": "   (for example, ``(somename)``)."}, {"line": 3140, "text": ""}, {"line": 3141, "text": "#. Conversion flags (optional), which affect the result of some conversion"}, {"line": 3142, "text": "   types."}, {"line": 3143, "text": ""}, {"line": 3144, "text": "#. Minimum field width (optional).  If specified as an ``'*'`` (asterisk), the"}, {"line": 3145, "text": "   actual width is read from the next element of the tuple in *values*, and the"}, {"line": 3146, "text": "   object to convert comes after the minimum field width and optional precision."}, {"line": 3147, "text": ""}, {"line": 3148, "text": "#. Precision (optional), given as a ``'.'`` (dot) followed by the precision.  If"}, {"line": 3149, "text": "   specified as ``'*'`` (an asterisk), the actual precision is read from the next"}, {"line": 3150, "text": "   element of the tuple in *values*, and the value to convert comes after the"}, {"line": 3151, "text": "   precision."}, {"line": 3152, "text": ""}, {"line": 3153, "text": "#. Length modifier (optional)."}, {"line": 3154, "text": ""}, {"line": 3155, "text": "#. Conversion type."}, {"line": 3156, "text": ""}, {"line": 3157, "text": "When the right argument is a dictionary (or other mapping type), then the"}, {"line": 3158, "text": "formats in the string *must* include a parenthesised mapping key into that"}, {"line": 3159, "text": "dictionary inserted immediately after the ``'%'`` character. The mapping key"}, {"line": 3160, "text": "selects the value to be formatted from the mapping.  For example:"}, {"line": 3161, "text": ""}, {"line": 3162, "text": "   >>> print('%(language)s has %(number)03d quote types.' %"}, {"line": 3163, "text": "   ...       {'language': \"Python\", \"number\": 2})"}, {"line": 3164, "text": "   Python has 002 quote types."}, {"line": 3165, "text": ""}, {"line": 3166, "text": "In this case no ``*`` specifiers may occur in a format (since they require a"}, {"line": 3167, "text": "sequential parameter list)."}, {"line": 3168, "text": ""}, {"line": 3169, "text": "The conversion flag characters are:"}, {"line": 3170, "text": ""}, {"line": 3171, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 3172, "text": "   single: # (hash); in printf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3173, "text": "   single: - (minus); in printf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3174, "text": "   single: + (plus); in printf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3175, "text": "   single: space; in printf-style formatting"}, {"line": 3176, "text": ""}, {"line": 3177, "text": "+---------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+"}, {"line": 3178, "text": "| Flag    | Meaning                                                             |"}, {"line": 3179, "text": "+=========+=====================================================================+"}, {"line": 3180, "text": "| ``'#'`` | The value conversion will use the \"alternate form\" (where defined   |"}, {"line": 3181, "text": "|         | below).                                                             |"}, {"line": 3182, "text": "+---------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+"}, {"line": 3183, "text": "| ``'0'`` | The conversion will be zero padded for numeric values.              |"}, {"line": 3184, "text": "+---------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+"}, {"line": 3185, "text": "| ``'-'`` | The converted value is left adjusted (overrides the ``'0'``         |"}, {"line": 3186, "text": "|         | conversion if both are given).                                      |"}, {"line": 3187, "text": "+---------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+"}, {"line": 3188, "text": "| ``' '`` | (a space) A blank should be left before a positive number (or empty |"}, {"line": 3189, "text": "|         | string) produced by a signed conversion.                            |"}, {"line": 3190, "text": "+---------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+"}, {"line": 3191, "text": "| ``'+'`` | A sign character (``'+'`` or ``'-'``) will precede the conversion   |"}, {"line": 3192, "text": "|         | (overrides a \"space\" flag).                                         |"}, {"line": 3193, "text": "+---------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+"}, {"line": 3194, "text": ""}, {"line": 3195, "text": "A length modifier (``h``, ``l``, or ``L``) may be present, but is ignored as it"}, {"line": 3196, "text": "is not necessary for Python -- so e.g. ``%ld`` is identical to ``%d``."}, {"line": 3197, "text": ""}, {"line": 3198, "text": "The conversion types are:"}, {"line": 3199, "text": ""}, {"line": 3200, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3201, "text": "| Conversion | Meaning                                             | Notes |"}, {"line": 3202, "text": "+============+=====================================================+=======+"}, {"line": 3203, "text": "| ``'d'``    | Signed integer decimal.                             |       |"}, {"line": 3204, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3205, "text": "| ``'i'``    | Signed integer decimal.                             |       |"}, {"line": 3206, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3207, "text": "| ``'o'``    | Signed octal value.                                 | \\(1)  |"}, {"line": 3208, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3209, "text": "| ``'u'``    | Obsolete type -- it is identical to ``'d'``.        | \\(6)  |"}, {"line": 3210, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3211, "text": "| ``'x'``    | Signed hexadecimal (lowercase).                     | \\(2)  |"}, {"line": 3212, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3213, "text": "| ``'X'``    | Signed hexadecimal (uppercase).                     | \\(2)  |"}, {"line": 3214, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3215, "text": "| ``'e'``    | Floating-point exponential format (lowercase).      | \\(3)  |"}, {"line": 3216, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3217, "text": "| ``'E'``    | Floating-point exponential format (uppercase).      | \\(3)  |"}, {"line": 3218, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3219, "text": "| ``'f'``    | Floating-point decimal format.                      | \\(3)  |"}, {"line": 3220, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3221, "text": "| ``'F'``    | Floating-point decimal format.                      | \\(3)  |"}, {"line": 3222, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3223, "text": "| ``'g'``    | Floating-point format. Uses lowercase exponential   | \\(4)  |"}, {"line": 3224, "text": "|            | format if exponent is less than -4 or not less than |       |"}, {"line": 3225, "text": "|            | precision, decimal format otherwise.                |       |"}, {"line": 3226, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3227, "text": "| ``'G'``    | Floating-point format. Uses uppercase exponential   | \\(4)  |"}, {"line": 3228, "text": "|            | format if exponent is less than -4 or not less than |       |"}, {"line": 3229, "text": "|            | precision, decimal format otherwise.                |       |"}, {"line": 3230, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3231, "text": "| ``'c'``    | Single character (accepts integer or single         |       |"}, {"line": 3232, "text": "|            | character string).                                  |       |"}, {"line": 3233, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3234, "text": "| ``'r'``    | String (converts any Python object using            | \\(5)  |"}, {"line": 3235, "text": "|            | :func:`repr`).                                      |       |"}, {"line": 3236, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3237, "text": "| ``'s'``    | String (converts any Python object using            | \\(5)  |"}, {"line": 3238, "text": "|            | :func:`str`).                                       |       |"}, {"line": 3239, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3240, "text": "| ``'a'``    | String (converts any Python object using            | \\(5)  |"}, {"line": 3241, "text": "|            | :func:`ascii`).                                     |       |"}, {"line": 3242, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3243, "text": "| ``'%'``    | No argument is converted, results in a ``'%'``      |       |"}, {"line": 3244, "text": "|            | character in the result.                            |       |"}, {"line": 3245, "text": "+------------+-----------------------------------------------------+-------+"}, {"line": 3246, "text": ""}, {"line": 3247, "text": "For floating-point formats, the result should be correctly rounded to a given"}, {"line": 3248, "text": "precision ``p`` of digits after the decimal point.  The rounding mode matches"}, {"line": 3249, "text": "that of the :func:`round` builtin."}, {"line": 3250, "text": ""}, {"line": 3251, "text": "Notes:"}, {"line": 3252, "text": ""}, {"line": 3253, "text": "(1)"}, {"line": 3254, "text": "   The alternate form causes a leading octal specifier (``'0o'``) to be"}, {"line": 3255, "text": "   inserted before the first digit."}, {"line": 3256, "text": ""}, {"line": 3257, "text": "(2)"}, {"line": 3258, "text": "   The alternate form causes a leading ``'0x'`` or ``'0X'`` (depending on whether"}, {"line": 3259, "text": "   the ``'x'`` or ``'X'`` format was used) to be inserted before the first digit."}, {"line": 3260, "text": ""}, {"line": 3261, "text": "(3)"}, {"line": 3262, "text": "   The alternate form causes the result to always contain a decimal point, even if"}, {"line": 3263, "text": "   no digits follow it."}, {"line": 3264, "text": ""}, {"line": 3265, "text": "   The precision determines the number of digits after the decimal point and"}, {"line": 3266, "text": "   defaults to 6."}, {"line": 3267, "text": ""}, {"line": 3268, "text": "(4)"}, {"line": 3269, "text": "   The alternate form causes the result to always contain a decimal point, and"}, {"line": 3270, "text": "   trailing zeroes are not removed as they would otherwise be."}, {"line": 3271, "text": ""}, {"line": 3272, "text": "   The precision determines the number of significant digits before and after the"}, {"line": 3273, "text": "   decimal point and defaults to 6."}, {"line": 3274, "text": ""}, {"line": 3275, "text": "(5)"}, {"line": 3276, "text": "   If precision is ``N``, the output is truncated to ``N`` characters."}, {"line": 3277, "text": ""}, {"line": 3278, "text": "(6)"}, {"line": 3279, "text": "   See :pep:`237`."}, {"line": 3280, "text": ""}, {"line": 3281, "text": "Since Python strings have an explicit length, ``%s`` conversions do not assume"}, {"line": 3282, "text": "that ``'\\0'`` is the end of the string."}, {"line": 3283, "text": ""}, {"line": 3284, "text": ".. XXX Examples?"}, {"line": 3285, "text": ""}, {"line": 3286, "text": ".. versionchanged:: 3.1"}, {"line": 3287, "text": "   ``%f`` conversions for numbers whose absolute value is over 1e50 are no"}, {"line": 3288, "text": "   longer replaced by ``%g`` conversions."}, {"line": 3289, "text": ""}, {"line": 3290, "text": ""}, {"line": 3291, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 3292, "text": "   single: buffer protocol; binary sequence types"}, {"line": 3293, "text": ""}, {"line": 3294, "text": ".. _binaryseq:"}, {"line": 3295, "text": ""}, {"line": 3296, "text": "Binary Sequence Types --- :class:`bytes`, :class:`bytearray`, :class:`memoryview`"}, {"line": 3297, "text": "================================================================================="}, {"line": 3298, "text": ""}, {"line": 3299, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 3300, "text": "   pair: object; bytes"}, {"line": 3301, "text": "   pair: object; bytearray"}, {"line": 3302, "text": "   pair: object; memoryview"}, {"line": 3303, "text": "   pair: module; array"}, {"line": 3304, "text": ""}, {"line": 3305, "text": "The core built-in types for manipulating binary data are :class:`bytes` and"}, {"line": 3306, "text": ":class:`bytearray`. They are supported by :class:`memoryview` which uses"}, {"line": 3307, "text": "the :ref:`buffer protocol <bufferobjects>` to access the memory of other"}, {"line": 3308, "text": "binary objects without needing to make a copy."}, {"line": 3309, "text": ""}, {"line": 3310, "text": "The :mod:`array` module supports efficient storage of basic data types like"}, {"line": 3311, "text": "32-bit integers and IEEE754 double-precision floating values."}, {"line": 3312, "text": ""}, {"line": 3313, "text": ".. _typebytes:"}, {"line": 3314, "text": ""}, {"line": 3315, "text": "Bytes Objects"}, {"line": 3316, "text": "-------------"}, {"line": 3317, "text": ""}, {"line": 3318, "text": ".. index:: pair: object; bytes"}, {"line": 3319, "text": ""}, {"line": 3320, "text": "Bytes objects are immutable sequences of single bytes. Since many major"}, {"line": 3321, "text": "binary protocols are based on the ASCII text encoding, bytes objects offer"}, {"line": 3322, "text": "several methods that are only valid when working with ASCII compatible"}, {"line": 3323, "text": "data and are closely related to string objects in a variety of other ways."}, {"line": 3324, "text": ""}, {"line": 3325, "text": ".. class:: bytes(source=b'')"}, {"line": 3326, "text": "           bytes(source, encoding, errors='strict')"}, {"line": 3327, "text": ""}, {"line": 3328, "text": "   Firstly, the syntax for bytes literals is largely the same as that for string"}, {"line": 3329, "text": "   literals, except that a ``b`` prefix is added:"}, {"line": 3330, "text": ""}, {"line": 3331, "text": "   * Single quotes: ``b'still allows embedded \"double\" quotes'``"}, {"line": 3332, "text": "   * Double quotes: ``b\"still allows embedded 'single' quotes\"``"}, {"line": 3333, "text": "   * Triple quoted: ``b'''3 single quotes'''``, ``b\"\"\"3 double quotes\"\"\"``"}, {"line": 3334, "text": ""}, {"line": 3335, "text": "   Only ASCII characters are permitted in bytes literals (regardless of the"}, {"line": 3336, "text": "   declared source code encoding). Any binary values over 127 must be entered"}, {"line": 3337, "text": "   into bytes literals using the appropriate escape sequence."}, {"line": 3338, "text": ""}, {"line": 3339, "text": "   As with string literals, bytes literals may also use a ``r`` prefix to disable"}, {"line": 3340, "text": "   processing of escape sequences. See :ref:`strings` for more about the various"}, {"line": 3341, "text": "   forms of bytes literal, including supported escape sequences."}, {"line": 3342, "text": ""}, {"line": 3343, "text": "   While bytes literals and representations are based on ASCII text, bytes"}, {"line": 3344, "text": "   objects actually behave like immutable sequences of integers, with each"}, {"line": 3345, "text": "   value in the sequence restricted such that ``0 <= x < 256`` (attempts to"}, {"line": 3346, "text": "   violate this restriction will trigger :exc:`ValueError`). This is done"}, {"line": 3347, "text": "   deliberately to emphasise that while many binary formats include ASCII based"}, {"line": 3348, "text": "   elements and can be usefully manipulated with some text-oriented algorithms,"}, {"line": 3349, "text": "   this is not generally the case for arbitrary binary data (blindly applying"}, {"line": 3350, "text": "   text processing algorithms to binary data formats that are not ASCII"}, {"line": 3351, "text": "   compatible will usually lead to data corruption)."}, {"line": 3352, "text": ""}, {"line": 3353, "text": "   In addition to the literal forms, bytes objects can be created in a number of"}, {"line": 3354, "text": "   other ways:"}, {"line": 3355, "text": ""}, {"line": 3356, "text": "   * A zero-filled bytes object of a specified length: ``bytes(10)``"}, {"line": 3357, "text": "   * From an iterable of integers: ``bytes(range(20))``"}, {"line": 3358, "text": "   * Copying existing binary data via the buffer protocol:  ``bytes(obj)``"}, {"line": 3359, "text": ""}, {"line": 3360, "text": "   Also see the :ref:`bytes <func-bytes>` built-in."}, {"line": 3361, "text": ""}, {"line": 3362, "text": "   Since 2 hexadecimal digits correspond precisely to a single byte, hexadecimal"}, {"line": 3363, "text": "   numbers are a commonly used format for describing binary data. Accordingly,"}, {"line": 3364, "text": "   the bytes type has an additional class method to read data in that format:"}, {"line": 3365, "text": ""}, {"line": 3366, "text": "   .. classmethod:: fromhex(string, /)"}, {"line": 3367, "text": ""}, {"line": 3368, "text": "      This :class:`bytes` class method returns a bytes object, decoding the"}, {"line": 3369, "text": "      given string object.  The string must contain two hexadecimal digits per"}, {"line": 3370, "text": "      byte, with ASCII whitespace being ignored."}, {"line": 3371, "text": ""}, {"line": 3372, "text": "      >>> bytes.fromhex('2Ef0 F1f2  ')"}, {"line": 3373, "text": "      b'.\\xf0\\xf1\\xf2'"}, {"line": 3374, "text": ""}, {"line": 3375, "text": "      .. versionchanged:: 3.7"}, {"line": 3376, "text": "         :meth:`bytes.fromhex` now skips all ASCII whitespace in the string,"}, {"line": 3377, "text": "         not just spaces."}, {"line": 3378, "text": ""}, {"line": 3379, "text": "      .. versionchanged:: 3.14"}, {"line": 3380, "text": "         :meth:`bytes.fromhex` now accepts ASCII :class:`bytes` and"}, {"line": 3381, "text": "         :term:`bytes-like objects <bytes-like object>` as input."}, {"line": 3382, "text": ""}, {"line": 3383, "text": "   A reverse conversion function exists to transform a bytes object into its"}, {"line": 3384, "text": "   hexadecimal representation."}, {"line": 3385, "text": ""}, {"line": 3386, "text": "   .. method:: hex(*, bytes_per_sep=1)"}, {"line": 3387, "text": "               hex(sep, bytes_per_sep=1)"}, {"line": 3388, "text": ""}, {"line": 3389, "text": "      Return a string object containing two hexadecimal digits for each"}, {"line": 3390, "text": "      byte in the instance."}, {"line": 3391, "text": ""}, {"line": 3392, "text": "      >>> b'\\xf0\\xf1\\xf2'.hex()"}, {"line": 3393, "text": "      'f0f1f2'"}, {"line": 3394, "text": ""}, {"line": 3395, "text": "      If you want to make the hex string easier to read, you can specify a"}, {"line": 3396, "text": "      single character separator *sep* parameter to include in the output."}, {"line": 3397, "text": "      By default, this separator will be included between each byte."}, {"line": 3398, "text": "      A second optional *bytes_per_sep* parameter controls the spacing."}, {"line": 3399, "text": "      Positive values calculate the separator position from the right,"}, {"line": 3400, "text": "      negative values from the left."}, {"line": 3401, "text": ""}, {"line": 3402, "text": "      >>> value = b'\\xf0\\xf1\\xf2'"}, {"line": 3403, "text": "      >>> value.hex('-')"}, {"line": 3404, "text": "      'f0-f1-f2'"}, {"line": 3405, "text": "      >>> value.hex('_', 2)"}, {"line": 3406, "text": "      'f0_f1f2'"}, {"line": 3407, "text": "      >>> b'UUDDLRLRAB'.hex(' ', -4)"}, {"line": 3408, "text": "      '55554444 4c524c52 4142'"}, {"line": 3409, "text": ""}, {"line": 3410, "text": "      .. versionadded:: 3.5"}, {"line": 3411, "text": ""}, {"line": 3412, "text": "      .. versionchanged:: 3.8"}, {"line": 3413, "text": "         :meth:`bytes.hex` now supports optional *sep* and *bytes_per_sep*"}, {"line": 3414, "text": "         parameters to insert separators between bytes in the hex output."}, {"line": 3415, "text": ""}, {"line": 3416, "text": "Since bytes objects are sequences of integers (akin to a tuple), for a bytes"}, {"line": 3417, "text": "object *b*, ``b[0]`` will be an integer, while ``b[0:1]`` will be a bytes"}, {"line": 3418, "text": "object of length 1.  (This contrasts with text strings, where both indexing"}, {"line": 3419, "text": "and slicing will produce a string of length 1)"}, {"line": 3420, "text": ""}, {"line": 3421, "text": "The representation of bytes objects uses the literal format (``b'...'``)"}, {"line": 3422, "text": "since it is often more useful than e.g. ``bytes([46, 46, 46])``.  You can"}, {"line": 3423, "text": "always convert a bytes object into a list of integers using ``list(b)``."}, {"line": 3424, "text": ""}, {"line": 3425, "text": ""}, {"line": 3426, "text": ".. _typebytearray:"}, {"line": 3427, "text": ""}, {"line": 3428, "text": "Bytearray Objects"}, {"line": 3429, "text": "-----------------"}, {"line": 3430, "text": ""}, {"line": 3431, "text": ".. index:: pair: object; bytearray"}, {"line": 3432, "text": ""}, {"line": 3433, "text": ":class:`bytearray` objects are a mutable counterpart to :class:`bytes`"}, {"line": 3434, "text": "objects."}, {"line": 3435, "text": ""}, {"line": 3436, "text": ".. class:: bytearray(source=b'')"}, {"line": 3437, "text": "           bytearray(source, encoding, errors='strict')"}, {"line": 3438, "text": ""}, {"line": 3439, "text": "   There is no dedicated literal syntax for bytearray objects, instead"}, {"line": 3440, "text": "   they are always created by calling the constructor:"}, {"line": 3441, "text": ""}, {"line": 3442, "text": "   * Creating an empty instance: ``bytearray()``"}, {"line": 3443, "text": "   * Creating a zero-filled instance with a given length: ``bytearray(10)``"}, {"line": 3444, "text": "   * From an iterable of integers: ``bytearray(range(20))``"}, {"line": 3445, "text": "   * Copying existing binary data via the buffer protocol:  ``bytearray(b'Hi!')``"}, {"line": 3446, "text": ""}, {"line": 3447, "text": "   As bytearray objects are mutable, they support the"}, {"line": 3448, "text": "   :ref:`mutable <typesseq-mutable>` sequence operations in addition to the"}, {"line": 3449, "text": "   common bytes and bytearray operations described in :ref:`bytes-methods`."}, {"line": 3450, "text": ""}, {"line": 3451, "text": "   Also see the :ref:`bytearray <func-bytearray>` built-in."}, {"line": 3452, "text": ""}, {"line": 3453, "text": "   Since 2 hexadecimal digits correspond precisely to a single byte, hexadecimal"}, {"line": 3454, "text": "   numbers are a commonly used format for describing binary data. Accordingly,"}, {"line": 3455, "text": "   the bytearray type has an additional class method to read data in that format:"}, {"line": 3456, "text": ""}, {"line": 3457, "text": "   .. classmethod:: fromhex(string, /)"}, {"line": 3458, "text": ""}, {"line": 3459, "text": "      This :class:`bytearray` class method returns a bytearray object, decoding"}, {"line": 3460, "text": "      the given string object.  The string must contain two hexadecimal digits"}, {"line": 3461, "text": "      per byte, with ASCII whitespace being ignored."}, {"line": 3462, "text": ""}, {"line": 3463, "text": "      >>> bytearray.fromhex('2Ef0 F1f2  ')"}, {"line": 3464, "text": "      bytearray(b'.\\xf0\\xf1\\xf2')"}, {"line": 3465, "text": ""}, {"line": 3466, "text": "      .. versionchanged:: 3.7"}, {"line": 3467, "text": "         :meth:`bytearray.fromhex` now skips all ASCII whitespace in the string,"}, {"line": 3468, "text": "         not just spaces."}, {"line": 3469, "text": ""}, {"line": 3470, "text": "      .. versionchanged:: 3.14"}, {"line": 3471, "text": "         :meth:`bytearray.fromhex` now accepts ASCII :class:`bytes` and"}, {"line": 3472, "text": "         :term:`bytes-like objects <bytes-like object>` as input."}, {"line": 3473, "text": ""}, {"line": 3474, "text": "   A reverse conversion function exists to transform a bytearray object into its"}, {"line": 3475, "text": "   hexadecimal representation."}, {"line": 3476, "text": ""}, {"line": 3477, "text": "   .. method:: hex(*, bytes_per_sep=1)"}, {"line": 3478, "text": "               hex(sep, bytes_per_sep=1)"}, {"line": 3479, "text": ""}, {"line": 3480, "text": "      Return a string object containing two hexadecimal digits for each"}, {"line": 3481, "text": "      byte in the instance."}, {"line": 3482, "text": ""}, {"line": 3483, "text": "      >>> bytearray(b'\\xf0\\xf1\\xf2').hex()"}, {"line": 3484, "text": "      'f0f1f2'"}, {"line": 3485, "text": ""}, {"line": 3486, "text": "      .. versionadded:: 3.5"}, {"line": 3487, "text": ""}, {"line": 3488, "text": "      .. versionchanged:: 3.8"}, {"line": 3489, "text": "         Similar to :meth:`bytes.hex`, :meth:`bytearray.hex` now supports"}, {"line": 3490, "text": "         optional *sep* and *bytes_per_sep* parameters to insert separators"}, {"line": 3491, "text": "         between bytes in the hex output."}, {"line": 3492, "text": ""}, {"line": 3493, "text": "   .. method:: resize(size, /)"}, {"line": 3494, "text": ""}, {"line": 3495, "text": "      Resize the :class:`bytearray` to contain *size* bytes. *size* must be"}, {"line": 3496, "text": "      greater than or equal to 0."}, {"line": 3497, "text": ""}, {"line": 3498, "text": "      If the :class:`bytearray` needs to shrink, bytes beyond *size* are truncated."}, {"line": 3499, "text": ""}, {"line": 3500, "text": "      If the :class:`bytearray` needs to grow, all new bytes, those beyond *size*,"}, {"line": 3501, "text": "      will be set to null bytes."}, {"line": 3502, "text": ""}, {"line": 3503, "text": ""}, {"line": 3504, "text": "      This is equivalent to:"}, {"line": 3505, "text": ""}, {"line": 3506, "text": "      >>> def resize(ba, size):"}, {"line": 3507, "text": "      ...     if len(ba) > size:"}, {"line": 3508, "text": "      ...         del ba[size:]"}, {"line": 3509, "text": "      ...     else:"}, {"line": 3510, "text": "      ...         ba += b'\\0' * (size - len(ba))"}, {"line": 3511, "text": ""}, {"line": 3512, "text": "      Examples:"}, {"line": 3513, "text": ""}, {"line": 3514, "text": "      >>> shrink = bytearray(b'abc')"}, {"line": 3515, "text": "      >>> shrink.resize(1)"}, {"line": 3516, "text": "      >>> (shrink, len(shrink))"}, {"line": 3517, "text": "      (bytearray(b'a'), 1)"}, {"line": 3518, "text": "      >>> grow = bytearray(b'abc')"}, {"line": 3519, "text": "      >>> grow.resize(5)"}, {"line": 3520, "text": "      >>> (grow, len(grow))"}, {"line": 3521, "text": "      (bytearray(b'abc\\x00\\x00'), 5)"}, {"line": 3522, "text": ""}, {"line": 3523, "text": "      .. versionadded:: 3.14"}, {"line": 3524, "text": ""}, {"line": 3525, "text": "Since bytearray objects are sequences of integers (akin to a list), for a"}, {"line": 3526, "text": "bytearray object *b*, ``b[0]`` will be an integer, while ``b[0:1]`` will be"}, {"line": 3527, "text": "a bytearray object of length 1.  (This contrasts with text strings, where"}, {"line": 3528, "text": "both indexing and slicing will produce a string of length 1)"}, {"line": 3529, "text": ""}, {"line": 3530, "text": "The representation of bytearray objects uses the bytes literal format"}, {"line": 3531, "text": "(``bytearray(b'...')``) since it is often more useful than e.g."}, {"line": 3532, "text": "``bytearray([46, 46, 46])``.  You can always convert a bytearray object into"}, {"line": 3533, "text": "a list of integers using ``list(b)``."}, {"line": 3534, "text": ""}, {"line": 3535, "text": ".. seealso::"}, {"line": 3536, "text": ""}, {"line": 3537, "text": "   For detailed information on thread-safety guarantees for :class:`bytearray`"}, {"line": 3538, "text": "   objects, see :ref:`thread-safety-bytearray`."}, {"line": 3539, "text": ""}, {"line": 3540, "text": ""}, {"line": 3541, "text": ".. _bytes-methods:"}, {"line": 3542, "text": ""}, {"line": 3543, "text": "Bytes and Bytearray Operations"}, {"line": 3544, "text": "------------------------------"}, {"line": 3545, "text": ""}, {"line": 3546, "text": ".. index:: pair: bytes; methods"}, {"line": 3547, "text": "           pair: bytearray; methods"}, {"line": 3548, "text": ""}, {"line": 3549, "text": "Both bytes and bytearray objects support the :ref:`common <typesseq-common>`"}, {"line": 3550, "text": "sequence operations. They interoperate not just with operands of the same"}, {"line": 3551, "text": "type, but with any :term:`bytes-like object`. Due to this flexibility, they can be"}, {"line": 3552, "text": "freely mixed in operations without causing errors. However, the return type"}, {"line": 3553, "text": "of the result may depend on the order of operands."}, {"line": 3554, "text": ""}, {"line": 3555, "text": ".. note::"}, {"line": 3556, "text": ""}, {"line": 3557, "text": "   The methods on bytes and bytearray objects don't accept strings as their"}, {"line": 3558, "text": "   arguments, just as the methods on strings don't accept bytes as their"}, {"line": 3559, "text": "   arguments.  For example, you have to write::"}, {"line": 3560, "text": ""}, {"line": 3561, "text": "      a = \"abc\""}, {"line": 3562, "text": "      b = a.replace(\"a\", \"f\")"}, {"line": 3563, "text": ""}, {"line": 3564, "text": "   and::"}, {"line": 3565, "text": ""}, {"line": 3566, "text": "      a = b\"abc\""}, {"line": 3567, "text": "      b = a.replace(b\"a\", b\"f\")"}, {"line": 3568, "text": ""}, {"line": 3569, "text": "Some bytes and bytearray operations assume the use of ASCII compatible"}, {"line": 3570, "text": "binary formats, and hence should be avoided when working with arbitrary"}, {"line": 3571, "text": "binary data. These restrictions are covered below."}, {"line": 3572, "text": ""}, {"line": 3573, "text": ".. note::"}, {"line": 3574, "text": "   Using these ASCII based operations to manipulate binary data that is not"}, {"line": 3575, "text": "   stored in an ASCII based format may lead to data corruption."}, {"line": 3576, "text": ""}, {"line": 3577, "text": "The following methods on bytes and bytearray objects can be used with"}, {"line": 3578, "text": "arbitrary binary data."}, {"line": 3579, "text": ""}, {"line": 3580, "text": ".. method:: bytes.count(sub[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3581, "text": "            bytearray.count(sub[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3582, "text": ""}, {"line": 3583, "text": "   Return the number of non-overlapping occurrences of subsequence *sub* in"}, {"line": 3584, "text": "   the range [*start*, *end*].  Optional arguments *start* and *end* are"}, {"line": 3585, "text": "   interpreted as in slice notation."}, {"line": 3586, "text": ""}, {"line": 3587, "text": "   The subsequence to search for may be any :term:`bytes-like object` or an"}, {"line": 3588, "text": "   integer in the range 0 to 255."}, {"line": 3589, "text": ""}, {"line": 3590, "text": "   If *sub* is empty, returns the number of empty slices between characters"}, {"line": 3591, "text": "   which is the length of the bytes object plus one."}, {"line": 3592, "text": ""}, {"line": 3593, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.3"}, {"line": 3594, "text": "      Also accept an integer in the range 0 to 255 as the subsequence."}, {"line": 3595, "text": ""}, {"line": 3596, "text": ""}, {"line": 3597, "text": ".. method:: bytes.removeprefix(prefix, /)"}, {"line": 3598, "text": "            bytearray.removeprefix(prefix, /)"}, {"line": 3599, "text": ""}, {"line": 3600, "text": "   If the binary data starts with the *prefix* string, return"}, {"line": 3601, "text": "   ``bytes[len(prefix):]``. Otherwise, return a copy of the original"}, {"line": 3602, "text": "   binary data::"}, {"line": 3603, "text": ""}, {"line": 3604, "text": "      >>> b'TestHook'.removeprefix(b'Test')"}, {"line": 3605, "text": "      b'Hook'"}, {"line": 3606, "text": "      >>> b'BaseTestCase'.removeprefix(b'Test')"}, {"line": 3607, "text": "      b'BaseTestCase'"}, {"line": 3608, "text": ""}, {"line": 3609, "text": "   The *prefix* may be any :term:`bytes-like object`."}, {"line": 3610, "text": ""}, {"line": 3611, "text": "   .. note::"}, {"line": 3612, "text": ""}, {"line": 3613, "text": "      The bytearray version of this method does *not* operate in place -"}, {"line": 3614, "text": "      it always produces a new object, even if no changes were made."}, {"line": 3615, "text": ""}, {"line": 3616, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.9"}, {"line": 3617, "text": ""}, {"line": 3618, "text": ""}, {"line": 3619, "text": ".. method:: bytes.removesuffix(suffix, /)"}, {"line": 3620, "text": "            bytearray.removesuffix(suffix, /)"}, {"line": 3621, "text": ""}, {"line": 3622, "text": "   If the binary data ends with the *suffix* string and that *suffix* is"}, {"line": 3623, "text": "   not empty, return ``bytes[:-len(suffix)]``.  Otherwise, return a copy of"}, {"line": 3624, "text": "   the original binary data::"}, {"line": 3625, "text": ""}, {"line": 3626, "text": "      >>> b'MiscTests'.removesuffix(b'Tests')"}, {"line": 3627, "text": "      b'Misc'"}, {"line": 3628, "text": "      >>> b'TmpDirMixin'.removesuffix(b'Tests')"}, {"line": 3629, "text": "      b'TmpDirMixin'"}, {"line": 3630, "text": ""}, {"line": 3631, "text": "   The *suffix* may be any :term:`bytes-like object`."}, {"line": 3632, "text": ""}, {"line": 3633, "text": "   .. note::"}, {"line": 3634, "text": ""}, {"line": 3635, "text": "      The bytearray version of this method does *not* operate in place -"}, {"line": 3636, "text": "      it always produces a new object, even if no changes were made."}, {"line": 3637, "text": ""}, {"line": 3638, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.9"}, {"line": 3639, "text": ""}, {"line": 3640, "text": ""}, {"line": 3641, "text": ".. method:: bytes.decode(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"strict\")"}, {"line": 3642, "text": "            bytearray.decode(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"strict\")"}, {"line": 3643, "text": ""}, {"line": 3644, "text": "   Return the bytes decoded to a :class:`str`."}, {"line": 3645, "text": ""}, {"line": 3646, "text": "   *encoding* defaults to ``'utf-8'``;"}, {"line": 3647, "text": "   see :ref:`standard-encodings` for possible values."}, {"line": 3648, "text": ""}, {"line": 3649, "text": "   *errors* controls how decoding errors are handled."}, {"line": 3650, "text": "   If ``'strict'`` (the default), a :exc:`UnicodeError` exception is raised."}, {"line": 3651, "text": "   Other possible values are ``'ignore'``, ``'replace'``,"}, {"line": 3652, "text": "   and any other name registered via :func:`codecs.register_error`."}, {"line": 3653, "text": "   See :ref:`error-handlers` for details."}, {"line": 3654, "text": ""}, {"line": 3655, "text": "   For performance reasons, the value of *errors* is not checked for validity"}, {"line": 3656, "text": "   unless a decoding error actually occurs,"}, {"line": 3657, "text": "   :ref:`devmode` is enabled or a :ref:`debug build <debug-build>` is used."}, {"line": 3658, "text": ""}, {"line": 3659, "text": "   .. note::"}, {"line": 3660, "text": ""}, {"line": 3661, "text": "      Passing the *encoding* argument to :class:`str` allows decoding any"}, {"line": 3662, "text": "      :term:`bytes-like object` directly, without needing to make a temporary"}, {"line": 3663, "text": "      :class:`!bytes` or :class:`!bytearray` object."}, {"line": 3664, "text": ""}, {"line": 3665, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.1"}, {"line": 3666, "text": "      Added support for keyword arguments."}, {"line": 3667, "text": ""}, {"line": 3668, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.9"}, {"line": 3669, "text": "      The value of the *errors* argument is now checked in :ref:`devmode` and"}, {"line": 3670, "text": "      in :ref:`debug mode <debug-build>`."}, {"line": 3671, "text": ""}, {"line": 3672, "text": ""}, {"line": 3673, "text": ".. method:: bytes.endswith(suffix[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3674, "text": "            bytearray.endswith(suffix[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3675, "text": ""}, {"line": 3676, "text": "   Return ``True`` if the binary data ends with the specified *suffix*,"}, {"line": 3677, "text": "   otherwise return ``False``.  *suffix* can also be a tuple of suffixes to"}, {"line": 3678, "text": "   look for.  With optional *start*, test beginning at that position.  With"}, {"line": 3679, "text": "   optional *end*, stop comparing at that position."}, {"line": 3680, "text": ""}, {"line": 3681, "text": "   The suffix(es) to search for may be any :term:`bytes-like object`."}, {"line": 3682, "text": ""}, {"line": 3683, "text": ""}, {"line": 3684, "text": ".. method:: bytes.find(sub[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3685, "text": "            bytearray.find(sub[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3686, "text": ""}, {"line": 3687, "text": "   Return the lowest index in the data where the subsequence *sub* is found,"}, {"line": 3688, "text": "   such that *sub* is contained in the slice ``s[start:end]``.  Optional"}, {"line": 3689, "text": "   arguments *start* and *end* are interpreted as in slice notation.  Return"}, {"line": 3690, "text": "   ``-1`` if *sub* is not found."}, {"line": 3691, "text": ""}, {"line": 3692, "text": "   The subsequence to search for may be any :term:`bytes-like object` or an"}, {"line": 3693, "text": "   integer in the range 0 to 255."}, {"line": 3694, "text": ""}, {"line": 3695, "text": "   .. note::"}, {"line": 3696, "text": ""}, {"line": 3697, "text": "      The :meth:`~bytes.find` method should be used only if you need to know the"}, {"line": 3698, "text": "      position of *sub*.  To check if *sub* is a substring or not, use the"}, {"line": 3699, "text": "      :keyword:`in` operator::"}, {"line": 3700, "text": ""}, {"line": 3701, "text": "         >>> b'Py' in b'Python'"}, {"line": 3702, "text": "         True"}, {"line": 3703, "text": ""}, {"line": 3704, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.3"}, {"line": 3705, "text": "      Also accept an integer in the range 0 to 255 as the subsequence."}, {"line": 3706, "text": ""}, {"line": 3707, "text": ""}, {"line": 3708, "text": ".. method:: bytes.index(sub[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3709, "text": "            bytearray.index(sub[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3710, "text": ""}, {"line": 3711, "text": "   Like :meth:`~bytes.find`, but raise :exc:`ValueError` when the"}, {"line": 3712, "text": "   subsequence is not found."}, {"line": 3713, "text": ""}, {"line": 3714, "text": "   The subsequence to search for may be any :term:`bytes-like object` or an"}, {"line": 3715, "text": "   integer in the range 0 to 255."}, {"line": 3716, "text": ""}, {"line": 3717, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.3"}, {"line": 3718, "text": "      Also accept an integer in the range 0 to 255 as the subsequence."}, {"line": 3719, "text": ""}, {"line": 3720, "text": ""}, {"line": 3721, "text": ".. method:: bytes.join(iterable, /)"}, {"line": 3722, "text": "            bytearray.join(iterable, /)"}, {"line": 3723, "text": ""}, {"line": 3724, "text": "   Return a bytes or bytearray object which is the concatenation of the"}, {"line": 3725, "text": "   binary data sequences in *iterable*.  A :exc:`TypeError` will be raised"}, {"line": 3726, "text": "   if there are any values in *iterable* that are not :term:`bytes-like"}, {"line": 3727, "text": "   objects <bytes-like object>`, including :class:`str` objects.  The"}, {"line": 3728, "text": "   separator between elements is the contents of the bytes or"}, {"line": 3729, "text": "   bytearray object providing this method."}, {"line": 3730, "text": ""}, {"line": 3731, "text": ""}, {"line": 3732, "text": ".. staticmethod:: bytes.maketrans(from, to, /)"}, {"line": 3733, "text": "                  bytearray.maketrans(from, to, /)"}, {"line": 3734, "text": ""}, {"line": 3735, "text": "   This static method returns a translation table usable for"}, {"line": 3736, "text": "   :meth:`bytes.translate` that will map each character in *from* into the"}, {"line": 3737, "text": "   character at the same position in *to*; *from* and *to* must both be"}, {"line": 3738, "text": "   :term:`bytes-like objects <bytes-like object>` and have the same length."}, {"line": 3739, "text": ""}, {"line": 3740, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.1"}, {"line": 3741, "text": ""}, {"line": 3742, "text": ""}, {"line": 3743, "text": ".. method:: bytes.partition(sep, /)"}, {"line": 3744, "text": "            bytearray.partition(sep, /)"}, {"line": 3745, "text": ""}, {"line": 3746, "text": "   Split the sequence at the first occurrence of *sep*, and return a 3-tuple"}, {"line": 3747, "text": "   containing the part before the separator, the separator itself or its"}, {"line": 3748, "text": "   bytearray copy, and the part after the separator."}, {"line": 3749, "text": "   If the separator is not found, return a 3-tuple"}, {"line": 3750, "text": "   containing a copy of the original sequence, followed by two empty bytes or"}, {"line": 3751, "text": "   bytearray objects."}, {"line": 3752, "text": ""}, {"line": 3753, "text": "   The separator to search for may be any :term:`bytes-like object`."}, {"line": 3754, "text": ""}, {"line": 3755, "text": ""}, {"line": 3756, "text": ".. method:: bytes.replace(old, new, count=-1, /)"}, {"line": 3757, "text": "            bytearray.replace(old, new, count=-1, /)"}, {"line": 3758, "text": ""}, {"line": 3759, "text": "   Return a copy of the sequence with all occurrences of subsequence *old*"}, {"line": 3760, "text": "   replaced by *new*.  If the optional argument *count* is given, only the"}, {"line": 3761, "text": "   first *count* occurrences are replaced."}, {"line": 3762, "text": ""}, {"line": 3763, "text": "   The subsequence to search for and its replacement may be any"}, {"line": 3764, "text": "   :term:`bytes-like object`."}, {"line": 3765, "text": ""}, {"line": 3766, "text": "   .. note::"}, {"line": 3767, "text": ""}, {"line": 3768, "text": "      The bytearray version of this method does *not* operate in place - it"}, {"line": 3769, "text": "      always produces a new object, even if no changes were made."}, {"line": 3770, "text": ""}, {"line": 3771, "text": ""}, {"line": 3772, "text": ".. method:: bytes.rfind(sub[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3773, "text": "            bytearray.rfind(sub[, start[, end]])"}, {"line": 3774, "text": ""}, {"line": 3775, "text": "   Return the highest index in the sequence where the subsequence *sub* is"}, {"line": 3776, "text": "   found, such that *sub* is contained within ``s[start:end]``. Return"}, {"line": 3778, "text": "   ``-1`` on failure."}, {"line": 3779, "text": ""}, {"line": 3780, "text": "   The subsequence to search for may be any :term:`bytes-like object` or an"}, {"line": 3781, "text": "   integer in the range 0 to 255."}, {"line": 3782, "text": ""}, {"line": 3783, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.3"}, {"line": 3784, "text": "      Also accept an integer in the range subsequence *sub* is not found."}, {"line": 3792, "text": ""}, {"line": 3793, "text": "   The subsequence to search for may be any :term:`bytes-like object` or an"}, {"line": 3794, "text": "   integer in the range 0 to 255."}, {"line": 3795, "text": ""}, {"line": 3796, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.3"}, {"line": 3797, "text": "      Also accept an integer in the range 0 to 255 as the subsequence."}, {"line": 3798, "text": ""}, {"line": 3799, "text": ""}, {"line": 3800, "text": ".. method:: bytes.rpartition(sep, /)"}, {"line": 3801, "text": "            bytearray.rpartition(sep, /)"}, {"line": 3802, "text": ""}, {"line": 3803, "text": "   Split the sequence at the last occurrence of *sep*, and return a 3-tuple"}, {"line": 3804, "text": "   containing the part before the ASCII whitespace characters are"}, {"line": 4166, "text": "   those byte values in the sequence ``b' \\t\\n\\r\\x0b\\f'`` (space, tab, newline,"}, {"line": 4167, "text": "   carriage return, vertical tab, form feed)."}, {"line": 4168, "text": ""}, {"line": 4169, "text": ""}, {"line": 4170, "text": ".. method:: bytes.istitle()"}, {"line": 4171, "text": "            bytearray.istitle()"}, {"line": 4172, "text": ""}, {"line": 4173, "text": "   Return ``True`` if the sequence is ASCII titlecase and the sequence is not"}, {"line": 4174, "text": "   empty, ``False`` otherwise. Uppercase ASCII characters"}, {"line": 4200, "text": "   are those byte values in the sequence ``b'ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ'``."}, {"line": 4201, "text": ""}, {"line": 4202, "text": ""}, {"line": 4203, "text": ".. method:: bytes.lower()"}, {"line": 4204, "text": "            bytearray.lower()"}, {"line": 4205, "text": ""}, {"line": 4206, "text": "   Return a copy of the sequence with all the uppercase ASCII characters"}, {"line": 4207, "text": "   converted to their corresponding lowercase counterpart."}, {"line": 4208, "text": ""}, {"line": 4209, "text": "   For example::"}, {"line": 4210, "text": ""}, {"line": 4211, "text": "      >>> b'Hello World'.lower()"}, {"line": 4212, "text": "      b'hello world'"}, {"line": 4213, "text": ""}, {"line": 4214, "text": "   Lowercase ASCII characters are those byte values in the sequence"}, {"line": 4215, "text": "   ``b'abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz'``. Uppercase ASCII characters"}, {"line": 4216, "text": "   are those byte values in the sequence ``b'ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ'``."}, {"line": 4217, "text": ""}, {"line": 4218, "text": "   .. note::"}, {"line": 4219, "text": ""}, {"line": 4220, "text": "      The bytearray version of this method does *not* operate in place - it"}, {"line": 4221, "text": "      always produces a new object, even if no changes were made."}, {"line": 4222, "text": ""}, {"line": 4223, "text": ""}, {"line": 4224, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 4225, "text": "   single: universal newlines; bytes.splitlines method"}, {"line": 4226, "text": "   single: universal newlines; bytearray.splitlines method"}, {"line": 4227, "text": ""}, {"line": 4228, "text": ".. method:: bytes.splitlines(keepends=False)"}, {"line": 4229, "text": "            bytearray.splitlines(keepends=False)"}, {"line": 4230, "text": ""}, {"line": 4231, "text": "   Return a list of the lines in the binary sequence, breaking at ASCII"}, {"line": 4232, "text": "   line boundaries. This method uses the :term:`universal newlines` approach"}, {"line": 4233, "text": "   to splitting lines. Line breaks are not included in the resulting list"}, {"line": 4234, "text": "   unless *keepends* is given and true."}, {"line": 4235, "text": ""}, {"line": 4236, "text": "   For example::"}, {"line": 4237, "text": ""}, {"line": 4238, "text": "      >>> b'ab c\\n\\nde fg\\rkl\\r\\n'.splitlines()"}, {"line": 4239, "text": "      [b'ab c', b'', b'de fg', b'kl']"}, {"line": 4240, "text": "      >>> b'ab c\\n\\nde fg\\rkl\\r\\n'.splitlines(keepends=True)"}, {"line": 4241, "text": "      [b'ab c\\n', b'\\n', b'de fg\\r', b'kl\\r\\n']"}, {"line": 4242, "text": ""}, {"line": 4243, "text": "   Unlike :meth:`~bytes.split` when a delimiter string *sep* is given, this"}, {"line": 4244, "text": "   method returns an empty list for the empty string, and a terminal line"}, {"line": 4245, "text": "   break does not result in an extra line::"}, {"line": 4246, "text": ""}, {"line": 4247, "text": "      >>> b\"\".split(b'\\n'), b\"Two lines\\n\".split(b'\\n')"}, {"line": 4248, "text": "      ([b''], [b'Two lines', b''])"}, {"line": 4249, "text": "      >>> b\"\".splitlines(), b\"One line\\n\".splitlines()"}, {"line": 4250, "text": "      ([], [b'One line'])"}, {"line": 4251, "text": ""}, {"line": 4252, "text": ""}, {"line": 4253, "text": ".. method:: bytes.swapcase()"}, {"line": 4254, "text": "            bytearray.swapcase()"}, {"line": 4255, "text": ""}, {"line": 4256, "text": "   Return a copy of the sequence with all the lowercase ASCII characters"}, {"line": 4257, "text": "   converted to their corresponding uppercase counterpart and vice-versa."}, {"line": 4258, "text": ""}, {"line": 4259, "text": "   For example::"}, {"line": 4260, "text": ""}, {"line": 4261, "text": "      >>> b'Hello World'.swapcase()"}, {"line": 4262, "text": "      b'hELLO wORLD'"}, {"line": 4263, "text": ""}, {"line": 4264, "text": "   Lowercase ASCII characters are those byte values in the sequence"}, {"line": 4265, "text": "   ``b'abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz'``.
