If not, insert *key*"}, {"line": 5526, "text": "      with a value of *default* and return *default*.  *default* defaults to"}, {"line": 5527, "text": "      ``None``."}, {"line": 5528, "text": ""}, {"line": 5529, "text": "   .. method:: update(**kwargs)"}, {"line": 5530, "text": "               update(mapping, /, **kwargs)"}, {"line": 5531, "text": "               update(iterable, /, **kwargs)"}, {"line": 5532, "text": ""}, {"line": 5533, "text": "      Update the dictionary with the key/value pairs from *mapping* or *iterable* and *kwargs*, overwriting"}, {"line": 5534, "text": "      existing keys.  Return ``None``."}, {"line": 5535, "text": ""}, {"line": 5536, "text": "      :meth:`update` accepts either another object with a ``keys()`` method (in"}, {"line": 5537, "text": "      which case :meth:`~object.__getitem__` is called with every key returned from"}, {"line": 5538, "text": "      the method) or an iterable of key/value pairs (as tuples or other iterables"}, {"line": 5539, "text": "      of length two). If keyword arguments are specified, the dictionary is then"}, {"line": 5540, "text": "      updated with those key/value pairs: ``d.update(red=1, blue=2)``."}, {"line": 5541, "text": ""}, {"line": 5542, "text": "   .. method:: values()"}, {"line": 5543, "text": ""}, {"line": 5544, "text": "      Return a new view of the dictionary's values.  See the"}, {"line": 5545, "text": "      :ref:`documentation of view objects <dict-views>`."}, {"line": 5546, "text": ""}, {"line": 5547, "text": "      An equality comparison between one ``dict.values()`` view and another"}, {"line": 5548, "text": "      will always return ``False``. This also applies when comparing"}, {"line": 5549, "text": "      ``dict.values()`` to itself::"}, {"line": 5550, "text": ""}, {"line": 5551, "text": "         >>> d = {'a': 1}"}, {"line": 5552, "text": "         >>> d.values() == d.values()"}, {"line": 5553, "text": "         False"}, {"line": 5554, "text": ""}, {"line": 5555, "text": "   .. describe:: d | other"}, {"line": 5556, "text": ""}, {"line": 5557, "text": "      Create a new dictionary with the merged keys and values of *d* and"}, {"line": 5558, "text": "      *other*, which must both be dictionaries. The values of *other* take"}, {"line": 5559, "text": "      priority when *d* and *other* share keys."}, {"line": 5560, "text": ""}, {"line": 5561, "text": "      .. versionadded:: 3.9"}, {"line": 5562, "text": ""}, {"line": 5563, "text": "   .. describe:: d |= other"}, {"line": 5564, "text": ""}, {"line": 5565, "text": "      Update the dictionary *d* with keys and values from *other*, which may be"}, {"line": 5566, "text": "      either a :term:`mapping` or an :term:`iterable` of key/value pairs. The"}, {"line": 5567, "text": "      values of *other* take priority when *d* and *other* share keys."}, {"line": 5568, "text": ""}, {"line": 5569, "text": "      .. versionadded:: 3.9"}, {"line": 5570, "text": ""}, {"line": 5571, "text": "   Dictionaries and dictionary views are reversible. ::"}, {"line": 5572, "text": ""}, {"line": 5573, "text": "      >>> d = {\"one\": 1, \"two\": 2, \"three\": 3, \"four\": 4}"}, {"line": 5574, "text": "      >>> d"}, {"line": 5575, "text": "      {'one': 1, 'two': 2, 'three': 3, 'four': 4}"}, {"line": 5576, "text": "      >>> list(reversed(d))"}, {"line": 5577, "text": "      ['four', 'three', 'two', 'one']"}, {"line": 5578, "text": "      >>> list(reversed(d.values()))"}, {"line": 5579, "text": "      [4, 3, 2, 1]"}, {"line": 5580, "text": "      >>> list(reversed(d.items()))"}, {"line": 5581, "text": "      [('four', 4), ('three', 3), ('two', 2), ('one', 1)]"}, {"line": 5582, "text": ""}, {"line": 5583, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.8"}, {"line": 5584, "text": "      Dictionaries are now reversible."}, {"line": 5585, "text": ""}, {"line": 5586, "text": ""}, {"line": 5587, "text": ".. seealso::"}, {"line": 5588, "text": "   :class:`types.MappingProxyType` can be used to create a read-only view"}, {"line": 5589, "text": "   of a :class:`dict`."}, {"line": 5590, "text": ""}, {"line": 5591, "text": ""}, {"line": 5592, "text": ".. seealso::"}, {"line": 5593, "text": ""}, {"line": 5594, "text": "   For detailed information on thread-safety guarantees for :class:`dict`"}, {"line": 5595, "text": "   objects, see :ref:`thread-safety-dict`."}, {"line": 5596, "text": ""}, {"line": 5597, "text": ""}, {"line": 5598, "text": ".. _dict-views:"}, {"line": 5599, "text": ""}, {"line": 5600, "text": "Dictionary view objects"}, {"line": 5601, "text": "-----------------------"}, {"line": 5602, "text": ""}, {"line": 5603, "text": "The objects returned by :meth:`dict.keys`, :meth:`dict.values` and"}, {"line": 5604, "text": ":meth:`dict.items` are *view objects*.  They provide a dynamic view on the"}, {"line": 5605, "text": "dictionary's entries, which means that when the dictionary changes, the view"}, {"line": 5606, "text": "reflects these changes."}, {"line": 5607, "text": ""}, {"line": 5608, "text": "Dictionary views can be iterated over to yield their respective data, and"}, {"line": 5609, "text": "support membership tests:"}, {"line": 5610, "text": ""}, {"line": 5611, "text": ".. describe:: len(dictview)"}, {"line": 5612, "text": ""}, {"line": 5613, "text": "   Return the number of entries in the dictionary."}, {"line": 5614, "text": ""}, {"line": 5615, "text": ".. describe:: iter(dictview)"}, {"line": 5616, "text": ""}, {"line": 5617, "text": "   Return an iterator over the keys, values or items (represented as tuples of"}, {"line": 5618, "text": "   ``(key, value)``) in the dictionary."}, {"line": 5619, "text": ""}, {"line": 5620, "text": "   Keys and values are iterated over in insertion order."}, {"line": 5621, "text": "   This allows the creation of ``(value, key)`` pairs"}, {"line": 5622, "text": "   using :func:`zip`: ``pairs = zip(d.values(), d.keys())``.  Another way to"}, {"line": 5623, "text": "   create the same list is ``pairs = [(v, k) for (k, v) in d.items()]``."}, {"line": 5624, "text": ""}, {"line": 5625, "text": "   Iterating views while adding or deleting entries in the dictionary may raise"}, {"line": 5626, "text": "   a :exc:`RuntimeError` or fail to iterate over all entries."}, {"line": 5627, "text": ""}, {"line": 5628, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.7"}, {"line": 5629, "text": "      Dictionary order is guaranteed to be insertion order."}, {"line": 5630, "text": ""}, {"line": 5631, "text": ".. describe:: x in dictview"}, {"line": 5632, "text": ""}, {"line": 5633, "text": "   Return ``True`` if *x* is in the underlying dictionary's keys, values or"}, {"line": 5634, "text": "   items (in the latter case, *x* should be a ``(key, value)`` tuple)."}, {"line": 5635, "text": ""}, {"line": 5636, "text": ".. describe:: reversed(dictview)"}, {"line": 5637, "text": ""}, {"line": 5638, "text": "   Return a reverse iterator over the keys, values or items of the dictionary."}, {"line": 5639, "text": "   The view will be iterated in reverse order of the insertion."}, {"line": 5640, "text": ""}, {"line": 5641, "text": "   .. versionchanged:: 3.8"}, {"line": 5642, "text": "      Dictionary views are now reversible."}, {"line": 5643, "text": ""}, {"line": 5644, "text": ".. describe:: dictview.mapping"}, {"line": 5645, "text": ""}, {"line": 5646, "text": "   Return a :class:`types.MappingProxyType` that wraps the original"}, {"line": 5647, "text": "   dictionary to which the view refers."}, {"line": 5648, "text": ""}, {"line": 5649, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.10"}, {"line": 5650, "text": ""}, {"line": 5651, "text": "Keys views are set-like since their entries are unique and :term:`hashable`."}, {"line": 5652, "text": "Items views also have set-like operations since the (key, value) pairs"}, {"line": 5653, "text": "are unique and the keys are hashable."}, {"line": 5654, "text": "If all values in an items view are hashable as well,"}, {"line": 5655, "text": "then the items view can interoperate with other sets."}, {"line": 5656, "text": "(Values views are not treated as set-like"}, {"line": 5657, "text": "since the entries are generally not unique.)  For set-like views, all of the"}, {"line": 5658, "text": "operations defined for the abstract base class :class:`collections.abc.Set` are"}, {"line": 5659, "text": "available (for example, ``==``, ``<``, or ``^``).  While using set operators,"}, {"line": 5660, "text": "set-like views accept any iterable as the other operand,"}, {"line": 5661, "text": "unlike sets which only accept sets as the input."}, {"line": 5662, "text": ""}, {"line": 5663, "text": "An example of dictionary view usage::"}, {"line": 5664, "text": ""}, {"line": 5665, "text": "   >>> dishes = {'eggs': 2, 'sausage': 1, 'bacon': 1, 'spam': 500}"}, {"line": 5666, "text": "   >>> keys = dishes.keys()"}, {"line": 5667, "text": "   >>> values = dishes.values()"}, {"line": 5668, "text": ""}, {"line": 5669, "text": "   >>> # iteration"}, {"line": 5670, "text": "   >>> n = 0"}, {"line": 5671, "text": "   >>> for val in values:"}, {"line": 5672, "text": "   ...     n += val"}, {"line": 5673, "text": "   ..."}, {"line": 5674, "text": "   >>> print(n)"}, {"line": 5675, "text": "   504"}, {"line": 5676, "text": ""}, {"line": 5677, "text": "   >>> # keys and values are iterated over in the same order (insertion order)"}, {"line": 5678, "text": "   >>> list(keys)"}, {"line": 5679, "text": "   ['eggs', 'sausage', 'bacon', 'spam']"}, {"line": 5680, "text": "   >>> list(values)"}, {"line": 5681, "text": "   [2, 1, 1, 500]"}, {"line": 5682, "text": ""}, {"line": 5683, "text": "   >>> # view objects are dynamic and reflect dict changes"}, {"line": 5684, "text": "   >>> del dishes['eggs']"}, {"line": 5685, "text": "   >>> del dishes['sausage']"}, {"line": 5686, "text": "   >>> list(keys)"}, {"line": 5687, "text": "   ['bacon', 'spam']"}, {"line": 5688, "text": ""}, {"line": 5689, "text": "   >>> # set operations"}, {"line": 5690, "text": "   >>> keys & {'eggs', 'bacon', 'salad'}"}, {"line": 5691, "text": "   {'bacon'}"}, {"line": 5692, "text": "   >>> keys ^ {'sausage', 'juice'} == {'juice', 'sausage', 'bacon', 'spam'}"}, {"line": 5693, "text": "   True"}, {"line": 5694, "text": "   >>> keys | ['juice', 'juice', 'juice'] == {'bacon', 'spam', 'juice'}"}, {"line": 5695, "text": "   True"}, {"line": 5696, "text": ""}, {"line": 5697, "text": "   >>> # get back a read-only proxy for the original dictionary"}, {"line": 5698, "text": "   >>> values.mapping"}, {"line": 5699, "text": "   mappingproxy({'bacon': 1, 'spam': 500})"}, {"line": 5700, "text": "   >>> values.mapping['spam']"}, {"line": 5701, "text": "   500"}, {"line": 5702, "text": ""}, {"line": 5703, "text": ""}, {"line": 5704, "text": ".. _typecontextmanager:"}, {"line": 5705, "text": ""}, {"line": 5706, "text": "Context Manager Types"}, {"line": 5707, "text": "====================="}, {"line": 5708, "text": ""}, {"line": 5709, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 5710, "text": "   single: context manager"}, {"line": 5711, "text": "   single: context management protocol"}, {"line": 5712, "text": "   single: protocol; context management"}, {"line": 5713, "text": ""}, {"line": 5714, "text": "Python's :keyword:`with` statement supports the concept of a runtime context"}, {"line": 5715, "text": "defined by a context manager.  This is implemented using a pair of methods"}, {"line": 5716, "text": "that allow user-defined classes to define a runtime context that is entered"}, {"line": 5717, "text": "before the statement body is executed and exited when the statement ends:"}, {"line": 5718, "text": ""}, {"line": 5719, "text": ""}, {"line": 5720, "text": ".. method:: contextmanager.__enter__()"}, {"line": 5721, "text": ""}, {"line": 5722, "text": "   Enter the runtime context and return either this object or another object"}, {"line": 5723, "text": "   related to the runtime context. The value returned by this method is bound to"}, {"line": 5724, "text": "   the identifier in the :keyword:`!as` clause of :keyword:`with` statements using"}, {"line": 5725, "text": "   this context manager."}, {"line": 5726, "text": ""}, {"line": 5727, "text": "   An example of a context manager that returns itself is a :term:`file object`."}, {"line": 5728, "text": "   File objects return themselves from __enter__() to allow :func:`open` to be"}, {"line": 5729, "text": "   used as the context expression in a :keyword:`with` statement."}, {"line": 5730, "text": ""}, {"line": 5731, "text": "   An example of a context manager that returns a related object is the one"}, {"line": 5732, "text": "   returned by :func:`decimal.localcontext`. These managers set the active"}, {"line": 5733, "text": "   decimal context to a copy of the original decimal context and then return the"}, {"line": 5734, "text": "   copy. This allows changes to be made to the current decimal context in the body"}, {"line": 5735, "text": "   of the :keyword:`with` statement without affecting code outside the"}, {"line": 5736, "text": "   :keyword:`!with` statement."}, {"line": 5737, "text": ""}, {"line": 5738, "text": ""}, {"line": 5739, "text": ".. method:: contextmanager.__exit__(exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb)"}, {"line": 5740, "text": ""}, {"line": 5741, "text": "   Exit the runtime context and return a Boolean flag indicating if any exception"}, {"line": 5742, "text": "   that occurred should be suppressed. If an exception occurred while executing the"}, {"line": 5743, "text": "   body of the :keyword:`with` statement, the arguments contain the exception type,"}, {"line": 5744, "text": "   value and traceback information. Otherwise, all three arguments are ``None``."}, {"line": 5745, "text": ""}, {"line": 5746, "text": "   Returning a true value from this method will cause the :keyword:`with` statement"}, {"line": 5747, "text": "   to suppress the exception and continue execution with the statement immediately"}, {"line": 5748, "text": "   following the :keyword:`!with` statement. Otherwise the exception continues"}, {"line": 5749, "text": "   propagating after this method has finished executing."}, {"line": 5750, "text": ""}, {"line": 5751, "text": "   If this method raises an exception while handling an earlier exception from the"}, {"line": 5752, "text": "   :keyword:`with` block, the new exception is raised, and the original exception"}, {"line": 5753, "text": "   is stored in its :attr:`~BaseException.__context__` attribute."}, {"line": 5754, "text": ""}, {"line": 5755, "text": "   The exception passed in should never be reraised explicitly - instead, this"}, {"line": 5756, "text": "   method should return a false value to indicate that the method completed"}, {"line": 5757, "text": "   successfully and does not want to suppress the raised exception. This allows"}, {"line": 5758, "text": "   context management code to easily detect whether or not an :meth:`~object.__exit__`"}, {"line": 5759, "text": "   method has actually failed."}, {"line": 5760, "text": ""}, {"line": 5761, "text": "Python defines several context managers to support easy thread synchronisation,"}, {"line": 5762, "text": "prompt closure of files or other objects, and simpler manipulation of the active"}, {"line": 5763, "text": "decimal arithmetic context. The specific types are not treated specially beyond"}, {"line": 5764, "text": "their implementation of the context management protocol. See the"}, {"line": 5765, "text": ":mod:`contextlib` module for some examples."}, {"line": 5766, "text": ""}, {"line": 5767, "text": "Python's :term:`generator`\\s and the :class:`contextlib.contextmanager` decorator"}, {"line": 5768, "text": "provide a convenient way to implement these protocols.  If a generator function is"}, {"line": 5769, "text": "decorated with the :class:`contextlib.contextmanager` decorator, it will return a"}, {"line": 5770, "text": "context manager implementing the necessary :meth:`~contextmanager.__enter__` and"}, {"line": 5771, "text": ":meth:`~contextmanager.__exit__` methods, rather than the iterator produced by an"}, {"line": 5772, "text": "undecorated generator function."}, {"line": 5773, "text": ""}, {"line": 5774, "text": "Note that there is no specific slot for any of these methods in the type"}, {"line": 5775, "text": "structure for Python objects in the Python/C API. Extension types wanting to"}, {"line": 5776, "text": "define these methods must provide them as a normal Python accessible method."}, {"line": 5777, "text": "Compared to the overhead of setting up the runtime context, the overhead of a"}, {"line": 5778, "text": "single class dictionary lookup is negligible."}, {"line": 5779, "text": ""}, {"line": 5780, "text": ""}, {"line": 5781, "text": "Type Annotation Types --- :ref:`Generic Alias <types-genericalias>`, :ref:`Union <types-union>`"}, {"line": 5782, "text": "==============================================================================================="}, {"line": 5783, "text": ""}, {"line": 5784, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 5785, "text": "   single: annotation; type annotation; type hint"}, {"line": 5786, "text": ""}, {"line": 5787, "text": "The core built-in types for :term:`type annotations <annotation>` are"}, {"line": 5788, "text": ":ref:`Generic Alias <types-genericalias>` and :ref:`Union <types-union>`."}, {"line": 5789, "text": ""}, {"line": 5790, "text": ""}, {"line": 5791, "text": ".. _types-genericalias:"}, {"line": 5792, "text": ""}, {"line": 5793, "text": "Generic Alias Type"}, {"line": 5794, "text": "------------------"}, {"line": 5795, "text": ""}, {"line": 5796, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 5797, "text": "   pair: object; GenericAlias"}, {"line": 5798, "text": "   pair: Generic; Alias"}, {"line": 5799, "text": ""}, {"line": 5800, "text": "``GenericAlias`` objects are generally created by"}, {"line": 5801, "text": ":ref:`subscripting <subscriptions>` a class. They are most often used with"}, {"line": 5802, "text": ":ref:`container classes <sequence-types>`, such as :class:`list` or"}, {"line": 5803, "text": ":class:`dict`. For example, ``list[int]`` is a ``GenericAlias`` object created"}, {"line": 5804, "text": "by subscripting the ``list`` class with the argument :class:`int`."}, {"line": 5805, "text": "``GenericAlias`` objects are intended primarily for use with"}, {"line": 5806, "text": ":term:`type annotations <annotation>`."}, {"line": 5807, "text": ""}, {"line": 5808, "text": ".. note::"}, {"line": 5809, "text": ""}, {"line": 5810, "text": "   It is generally only possible to subscript a class if the class implements"}, {"line": 5811, "text": "   the special method :meth:`~object.__class_getitem__`."}, {"line": 5812, "text": ""}, {"line": 5813, "text": "A ``GenericAlias`` object acts as a proxy for a :term:`generic type`,"}, {"line": 5814, "text": "implementing *parameterized generics*."}, {"line": 5815, "text": ""}, {"line": 5816, "text": "For a container class, the"}, {"line": 5817, "text": "argument(s) supplied to a :ref:`subscription <subscriptions>` of the class may"}, {"line": 5818, "text": "indicate the type(s) of the elements an object contains. For example,"}, {"line": 5819, "text": "``set[bytes]`` can be used in type annotations to signify a :class:`set` in"}, {"line": 5820, "text": "which all the elements are of type :class:`bytes`."}, {"line": 5821, "text": ""}, {"line": 5822, "text": "For a class which defines :meth:`~object.__class_getitem__` but is not a"}, {"line": 5823, "text": "container, the argument(s) supplied to a subscription of the class will often"}, {"line": 5824, "text": "indicate the return type(s) of one or more methods defined on an object. For"}, {"line": 5825, "text": "example, :mod:`regular expressions <re>` can be used on both the :class:`str` data"}, {"line": 5826, "text": "type and the :class:`bytes` data type:"}, {"line": 5827, "text": ""}, {"line": 5828, "text": "* If ``x = re.search('foo', 'foo')``, ``x`` will be a"}, {"line": 5829, "text": "  :ref:`re.Match <match-objects>` object where the return values of"}, {"line": 5830, "text": "  ``x.group(0)`` and ``x[0]`` will both be of type :class:`str`. We can"}, {"line": 5831, "text": "  represent this kind of object in type annotations with the ``GenericAlias``"}, {"line": 5832, "text": "  ``re.Match[str]``."}, {"line": 5833, "text": ""}, {"line": 5834, "text": "* If ``y = re.search(b'bar', b'bar')``, (note the ``b`` for :class:`bytes`),"}, {"line": 5835, "text": "  ``y`` will also be an instance of ``re.Match``, but the return"}, {"line": 5836, "text": "  values of ``y.group(0)`` and ``y[0]`` will both be of type"}, {"line": 5837, "text": "  :class:`bytes`. In type annotations, we would represent this"}, {"line": 5838, "text": "  variety of :ref:`re.Match <match-objects>` objects with ``re.Match[bytes]``."}, {"line": 5839, "text": ""}, {"line": 5840, "text": "``GenericAlias`` objects are instances of the class"}, {"line": 5841, "text": ":class:`types.GenericAlias`, which can also be used to create ``GenericAlias``"}, {"line": 5842, "text": "objects directly. Specializations of user-defined :ref:`generic classes <generic-classes>`"}, {"line": 5843, "text": "may not be instances of :class:`types.GenericAlias`, but they provide similar functionality."}, {"line": 5844, "text": ""}, {"line": 5845, "text": ".. describe:: T[X, Y, ...]"}, {"line": 5846, "text": ""}, {"line": 5847, "text": "   Creates a ``GenericAlias`` representing a type ``T`` parameterized by types"}, {"line": 5848, "text": "   *X*, *Y*, and more depending on the ``T`` used."}, {"line": 5849, "text": "   For example, a function expecting a :class:`list` containing"}, {"line": 5850, "text": "   :class:`float` elements::"}, {"line": 5851, "text": ""}, {"line": 5852, "text": "      def average(values: list[float]) -> float:"}, {"line": 5853, "text": "          return sum(values) / len(values)"}, {"line": 5854, "text": ""}, {"line": 5855, "text": "   Another example for :term:`mapping` objects, using a :class:`dict`, which"}, {"line": 5856, "text": "   is a generic type expecting two type parameters representing the key type"}, {"line": 5857, "text": "   and the value type.  In this example, the function expects a ``dict`` with"}, {"line": 5858, "text": "   keys of type :class:`str` and values of type :class:`int`::"}, {"line": 5859, "text": ""}, {"line": 5860, "text": "      def send_post_request(url: str, body: dict[str, int]) -> None:"}, {"line": 5861, "text": "          ..."}, {"line": 5862, "text": ""}, {"line": 5863, "text": "The builtin functions :func:`isinstance` and :func:`issubclass` do not accept"}, {"line": 5864, "text": "``GenericAlias`` types for their second argument::"}, {"line": 5865, "text": ""}, {"line": 5866, "text": "   >>> isinstance([1, 2], list[str])"}, {"line": 5867, "text": "   Traceback (most recent call last):"}, {"line": 5868, "text": "     File \"<stdin>\", line 1, in <module>"}, {"line": 5869, "text": "   TypeError: isinstance() argument 2 cannot be a parameterized generic"}, {"line": 5870, "text": ""}, {"line": 5871, "text": "The Python runtime does not enforce :term:`type annotations <annotation>`."}, {"line": 5872, "text": "This extends to generic types and their type parameters. When creating"}, {"line": 5873, "text": "a container object from a ``GenericAlias``, the elements in the container are not checked"}, {"line": 5874, "text": "against their type. For example, the following code is discouraged, but will"}, {"line": 5875, "text": "run without errors::"}, {"line": 5876, "text": ""}, {"line": 5877, "text": "   >>> t = list[str]"}, {"line": 5878, "text": "   >>> t([1, 2, 3])"}, {"line": 5879, "text": "   [1, 2, 3]"}, {"line": 5880, "text": ""}, {"line": 5881, "text": "Furthermore, parameterized generics erase type parameters during object"}, {"line": 5882, "text": "creation::"}, {"line": 5883, "text": ""}, {"line": 5884, "text": "   >>> t = list[str]"}, {"line": 5885, "text": "   >>> type(t)"}, {"line": 5886, "text": "   <class 'types.GenericAlias'>"}, {"line": 5887, "text": ""}, {"line": 5888, "text": "   >>> l = t()"}, {"line": 5889, "text": "   >>> type(l)"}, {"line": 5890, "text": "   <class 'list'>"}, {"line": 5891, "text": ""}, {"line": 5892, "text": ""}, {"line": 5893, "text": "Instances of ``GenericAlias`` are not classes at runtime, even though they behave like classes (they can be instantiated and subclassed)::"}, {"line": 5894, "text": ""}, {"line": 5895, "text": "   >>> import inspect"}, {"line": 5896, "text": "   >>> inspect.isclass(list[int])"}, {"line": 5897, "text": "   False"}, {"line": 5898, "text": ""}, {"line": 5899, "text": "This is true for :ref:`user-defined generics <user-defined-generics>` also."}, {"line": 5900, "text": ""}, {"line": 5901, "text": "Calling :func:`repr` or :func:`str` on a generic shows the parameterized type::"}, {"line": 5902, "text": ""}, {"line": 5903, "text": "   >>> repr(list[int])"}, {"line": 5904, "text": "   'list[int]'"}, {"line": 5905, "text": ""}, {"line": 5906, "text": "   >>> str(list[int])"}, {"line": 5907, "text": "   'list[int]'"}, {"line": 5908, "text": ""}, {"line": 5909, "text": "The :meth:`~object.__getitem__` method of generic containers will raise an"}, {"line": 5910, "text": "exception to disallow mistakes like ``dict[str][str]``::"}, {"line": 5911, "text": ""}, {"line": 5912, "text": "   >>> dict[str][str]"}, {"line": 5913, "text": "   Traceback (most recent call last):"}, {"line": 5914, "text": "     ..."}, {"line": 5915, "text": "   TypeError: dict[str] is not a generic class"}, {"line": 5916, "text": ""}, {"line": 5917, "text": "However, such expressions are valid when :ref:`type variables <generics>` are"}, {"line": 5918, "text": "used.  The index must have as many elements as there are type variable items"}, {"line": 5919, "text": "in the ``GenericAlias`` object's :attr:`~genericalias.__args__`. ::"}, {"line": 5920, "text": ""}, {"line": 5921, "text": "   >>> from typing import TypeVar"}, {"line": 5922, "text": "   >>> Y = TypeVar('Y')"}, {"line": 5923, "text": "   >>> dict[str, Y][int]"}, {"line": 5924, "text": "   dict[str, int]"}, {"line": 5925, "text": ""}, {"line": 5926, "text": ""}, {"line": 5927, "text": "Standard Generic Classes"}, {"line": 5928, "text": "^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^"}, {"line": 5929, "text": ""}, {"line": 5930, "text": "The following standard library classes support parameterized generics. This"}, {"line": 5931, "text": "list is non-exhaustive."}, {"line": 5932, "text": ""}, {"line": 5933, "text": "* :class:`tuple`"}, {"line": 5934, "text": "* :class:`list`"}, {"line": 5935, "text": "* :class:`asyncio.Future`"}, {"line": 5940, "text": "* :class:`asyncio.Task`"}, {"line": 5941, "text": "* :class:`collections.deque`"}, {"line": 5942, "text": "* :class:`collections.defaultdict`"}, {"line": 5943, "text": "* :class:`collections.OrderedDict`"}, {"line": 5944, "text": "* :class:`collections.Counter`"}, {"line": 5945, "text": "* :class:`collections.ChainMap`"}, {"line": 5946, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Awaitable`"}, {"line": 5947, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Coroutine`"}, {"line": 5948, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.AsyncIterable`"}, {"line": 5949, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.AsyncIterator`"}, {"line": 5950, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.AsyncGenerator`"}, {"line": 5951, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Iterable`"}, {"line": 5952, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Iterator`"}, {"line": 5953, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Generator`"}, {"line": 5954, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Reversible`"}, {"line": 5955, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Container`"}, {"line": 5956, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Collection`"}, {"line": 5957, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Callable`"}, {"line": 5958, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Set`"}, {"line": 5959, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.MutableSet`"}, {"line": 5960, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.Mapping`"}, {"line": 5961, "text": "* "* :class:`collections.abc.ByteString`"}, {"line": 5965, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.MappingView`"}, {"line": 5966, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.KeysView`"}, {"line": 5967, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.ItemsView`"}, {"line": 5968, "text": "* :class:`collections.abc.ValuesView`"}, {"line": 5969, "text": "* :class:`contextlib.AbstractContextManager`"}, {"line": 5970, "text": "* :class:`contextlib.AbstractAsyncContextManager`"}, {"line": 5971, "text": "* :class:`dataclasses.Field`"}, {"line": 5972, "text": "* :class:`functools.cached_property`"}, {"line": 5973, "text": "* :class:`functools.partialmethod`"}, {"line": 5974, "text": "* :class:`os.PathLike`"}, {"line": 5975, "text": "* :class:`queue.LifoQueue`"}, {"line": 5976, "text": "* :class:`queue.Queue`"}, {"line": 5977, "text": "* :class:`queue.PriorityQueue`"}, {"line": 5978, "text": "* :class:`queue.SimpleQueue`"}, {"line": 5979, "text": "* :ref:`re.Pattern <re-objects>`"}, {"line": 5980, "text": "* :ref:`re.Match <match-objects>`"}, {"line": 5981, "text": "* :class:`shelve.BsdDbShelf`"}, {"line": 5982, "text": "* :class:`shelve.DbfilenameShelf`"}, {"line": 5983, "text": "* :class:`shelve.Shelf`"}, {"line": 5984, "text": "* :class:`types.MappingProxyType`"}, {"line": 5985, "text": "* :class:`weakref.WeakKeyDictionary`"}, {"line": 5986, "text": "* :class:`weakref.WeakMethod`"}, {"line": 5987, "text": "* :class:`weakref.WeakSet`"}, {"line": 5988, "text": "* :class:`weakref.WeakValueDictionary`"}, {"line": 5989, "text": ""}, {"line": 5990, "text": ""}, {"line": 5991, "text": ""}, {"line": 5992, "text": "Special Attributes of ``GenericAlias`` objects"}, {"line": 5993, "text": "^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^"}, {"line": 5994, "text": ""}, {"line": 5995, "text": "All parameterized generics implement special read-only attributes."}, {"line": 5996, "text": ""}, {"line": 5997, "text": ".. attribute:: genericalias.__origin__"}, {"line": 5998, "text": ""}, {"line": 5999, "text": "   This attribute points at the non-parameterized generic class::"}, {"line": 6000, "text": ""}, {"line": 6001, "text": "      >>> list[int].__origin__"}, {"line": 6002, "text": "      <class 'list'>"}, {"line": 6003, "text": ""}, {"line": 6004, "text": ""}, {"line": 6005, "text": ".. attribute:: genericalias.__args__"}, {"line": 6006, "text": ""}, {"line": 6007, "text": "   This attribute is a :class:`tuple` (possibly of length 1) of generic"}, {"line": 6008, "text": "   types passed to the original :meth:`~object.__class_getitem__` of the"}, {"line": 6009, "text": "   generic class::"}, {"line": 6010, "text": ""}, {"line": 6011, "text": "      >>> dict[str, list[int]].__args__"}, {"line": 6012, "text": "      (<class 'str'>, list[int])"}, {"line": 6013, "text": ""}, {"line": 6014, "text": ""}, {"line": 6015, "text": ".. attribute:: genericalias.__parameters__"}, {"line": 6016, "text": ""}, {"line": 6017, "text": "   This attribute is a lazily computed tuple (possibly empty) of unique type"}, {"line": 6018, "text": "   variables found in ``__args__``::"}, {"line": 6019, "text": ""}, {"line": 6020, "text": "      >>> from typing import TypeVar"}, {"line": 6021, "text": ""}, {"line": 6022, "text": "      >>> T = TypeVar('T')"}, {"line": 6023, "text": "      >>> list[T].__parameters__"}, {"line": 6024, "text": "      (~T,)"}, {"line": 6025, "text": ""}, {"line": 6026, "text": ""}, {"line": 6027, "text": "   .. note::"}, {"line": 6028, "text": "      A ``GenericAlias`` object with :class:`typing.ParamSpec` parameters may not"}, {"line": 6029, "text": "      have correct ``__parameters__`` after substitution because"}, {"line": 6030, "text": "      :class:`typing.ParamSpec` is intended primarily for static type checking."}, {"line": 6031, "text": ""}, {"line": 6032, "text": ""}, {"line": 6033, "text": ".. attribute:: genericalias.__unpacked__"}, {"line": 6034, "text": ""}, {"line": 6035, "text": "   A boolean that is true if the alias has been unpacked using the"}, {"line": 6036, "text": "   ``*`` operator (see :data:`~typing.TypeVarTuple`)."}, {"line": 6037, "text": ""}, {"line": 6038, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.11"}, {"line": 6039, "text": ""}, {"line": 6040, "text": ""}, {"line": 6041, "text": ".. seealso::"}, {"line": 6042, "text": ""}, {"line": 6043, "text": "   :pep:`484` - Type Hints"}, {"line": 6044, "text": "      Introducing Python's framework for type annotations."}, {"line": 6045, "text": ""}, {"line": 6046, "text": "   :pep:`585` - Type Hinting Generics In Standard Collections"}, {"line": 6047, "text": "      Introducing the ability to natively parameterize standard-library"}, {"line": 6048, "text": "      classes, provided they implement the special class method"}, {"line": 6049, "text": "      :meth:`~object.__class_getitem__`."}, {"line": 6050, "text": ""}, {"line": 6051, "text": "   :ref:`Generics`, :ref:`user-defined generics <user-defined-generics>` and :class:`typing.Generic`"}, {"line": 6052, "text": "      Documentation on how to implement generic classes that can be"}, {"line": 6053, "text": "      parameterized at runtime and understood by static type-checkers."}, {"line": 6054, "text": ""}, {"line": 6055, "text": ".. versionadded:: 3.9"}, {"line": 6056, "text": ""}, {"line": 6057, "text": ""}, {"line": 6058, "text": ".. _types-union:"}, {"line": 6059, "text": ""}, {"line": 6060, "text": "Union Type"}, {"line": 6061, "text": "----------"}, {"line": 6062, "text": ""}, {"line": 6063, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 6064, "text": "   pair: object; Union"}, {"line": 6065, "text": "   pair: union; type"}, {"line": 6066, "text": ""}, {"line": 6067, "text": "A union object holds the value of the ``|`` (bitwise or) operation on"}, {"line": 6068, "text": "multiple :ref:`type objects <bltin-type-objects>`.  These types are intended"}, {"line": 6069, "text": "primarily for :term:`type annotations <annotation>`. The union type expression"}, {"line": 6070, "text": "enables cleaner type hinting syntax compared to subscripting :class:`typing.Union`."}, {"line": 6071, "text": ""}, {"line": 6072, "text": ".. describe:: X | Y | ..."}, {"line": 6073, "text": ""}, {"line": 6074, "text": "   Defines a union object which holds types *X*, *Y*, and so forth. ``X | Y``"}, {"line": 6075, "text": "   means either X or Y.  It is equivalent to ``typing.Union[X, Y]``."}, {"line": 6076, "text": "   For example, the following function expects an argument of type"}, {"line": 6077, "text": "   :class:`int` or :class:`float`::"}, {"line": 6078, "text": ""}, {"line": 6079, "text": "      def square(number: int | float) -> int | float:"}, {"line": 6080, "text": "          return number ** 2"}, {"line": 6081, "text": ""}, {"line": 6082, "text": "   .. note::"}, {"line": 6083, "text": ""}, {"line": 6084, "text": "      The ``|`` operator cannot be used at runtime to define unions where one or"}, {"line": 6085, "text": "      more members is a forward reference. For example, ``int | \"Foo\"``, where"}, {"line": 6086, "text": "      ``\"Foo\"`` is a reference to a class not yet defined, will fail at"}, {"line": 6087, "text": "      runtime. For unions which include forward references, present the"}, {"line": 6088, "text": "      whole expression as a string, e.g. ``\"int | Foo\"``."}, {"line": 6089, "text": ""}, {"line": 6090, "text": ".. describe:: union_object == other"}, {"line": 6091, "text": ""}, {"line": 6092, "text": "   Union objects can be tested for equality with other union objects.  Details:"}, {"line": 6093, "text": ""}, {"line": 6094, "text": "   * Unions of unions are flattened::"}, {"line": 6095, "text": ""}, {"line": 6096, "text": "       (int | str) | float == int | str | float"}, {"line": 6097, "text": ""}, {"line": 6098, "text": "   * Redundant types are removed::"}, {"line": 6099, "text": ""}, {"line": 6100, "text": "       int | str | int == int | str"}, {"line": 6101, "text": ""}, {"line": 6102, "text": "   * When comparing unions, the order is ignored::"}, {"line": 6103, "text": ""}, {"line": 6104, "text": "      int | str == str | int"}, {"line": 6105, "text": ""}, {"line": 6106, "text": "   * It creates instances of :class:`typing.Union`::"}, {"line": 6107, "text": ""}, {"line": 6108, "text": "      int | str == typing.Union[int, str]"}, {"line": 6109, "text": "      type(int | str) is typing.Union"}, {"line": 6110, "text": ""}, {"line": 6111, "text": "   * Optional types can be spelled as a union with ``None``::"}, {"line": 6112, "text": ""}, {"line": 6113, "text": "      str | None == typing.Optional[str]"}, {"line": 6114, "text": ""}, {"line": 6115, "text": ".. describe:: isinstance(obj, union_object)"}, {"line": 6116, "text": ".. describe:: issubclass(obj, union_object)"}, {"line": 6117, "text": ""}, {"line": 6118, "text": "   Calls to :func:`isinstance` and :func:`issubclass` are also supported with a"}, {"line": 6119, "text": "   union object::"}, {"line": 6120, "text": ""}, {"line": 6121, "text": "      >>> isinstance(\"\", int | str)"}, {"line": 6122, "text": "      True"}, {"line": 6123, "text": ""}, {"line": 6124, "text": "   However, :ref:`parameterized generics <types-genericalias>` in"}, {"line": 6125, "text": "   union objects cannot be checked::"}, {"line": 6126, "text": ""}, {"line": 6127, "text": "      >>> isinstance(1, int | list[int])  # short-circuit evaluation"}, {"line": 6128, "text": "      True"}, {"line": 6129, "text": "      >>> isinstance([1], int | list[int])"}, {"line": 6130, "text": "      Traceback (most recent call last):"}, {"line": 6131, "text": "        ..."}, {"line": 6132, "text": "      TypeError: isinstance() argument 2 cannot be a parameterized generic"}, {"line": 6133, "text": ""}, {"line": 6134, "text": "The user-exposed type for the union object can be accessed from"}, {"line": 6135, "text": ":class:`typing.Union` and used for :func:`isinstance` checks::"}, {"line": 6136, "text": ""}, {"line": 6137, "text": "   >>> import typing"}, {"line": 6138, "text": "   >>> isinstance(int | str, typing.Union)"}, {"line": 6139, "text": "   True"}, {"line": 6140, "text": "   >>> typing.Union()"}, {"line": 6141, "text": "   Traceback (most recent call last):"}, {"line": 6142, "text": "     File \"<stdin>\", line 1, in <module>"}, {"line": 6143, "text": "   TypeError: cannot create 'typing.Union' instances"}, {"line": 6144, "text": ""}, {"line": 6145, "text": ".. note::"}, {"line": 6146, "text": "   The :meth:`!__or__` method for type objects was added to support the syntax"}, {"line": 6147, "text": "   ``X | Y``.  If a metaclass implements :meth:`!__or__`, the Union may"}, {"line": 6148, "text": "   override it:"}, {"line": 6149, "text": ""}, {"line": 6150, "text": "   .. doctest::"}, {"line": 6151, "text": ""}, {"line": 6152, "text": "      >>> class M(type):"}, {"line": 6153, "text": "      ...     def __or__(self, other):"}, {"line": 6154, "text": "      ...         return \"Hello\""}, {"line": 6155, "text": "      ..."}, {"line": 6156, "text": "      >>> class C(metaclass=M):"}, {"line": 6157, "text": "      ...     pass"}, {"line": 6158, "text": "      ..."}, {"line": 6159, "text": "      >>> C | int"}, {"line": 6160, "text": "      'Hello'"}, {"line": 6161, "text": "      >>> int | C"}, {"line": 6162, "text": "      int | C"}, {"line": 6163, "text": ""}, {"line": 6164, "text": ".. seealso::"}, {"line": 6165, "text": ""}, {"line": 6166, "text": "   :pep:`604` -- PEP proposing the ``X | Y`` syntax and the Union type."}, {"line": 6167, "text": ""}, {"line": 6168, "text": ".. versionadded:: 3.10"}, {"line": 6169, "text": ""}, {"line": 6170, "text": ".. versionchanged:: 3.14"}, {"line": 6171, "text": ""}, {"line": 6172, "text": "   Union objects are now instances of :class:`typing.Union`. Previously, they were instances"}, {"line": 6173, "text": "   of :class:`types.UnionType`, which remains an alias for :class:`typing.Union`."}, {"line": 6174, "text": ""}, {"line": 6175, "text": ""}, {"line": 6176, "text": ".. _typesother:"}, {"line": 6177, "text": ""}, {"line": 6178, "text": "Other Built-in Types"}, {"line": 6179, "text": "===================="}, {"line": 6180, "text": ""}, {"line": 6181, "text": "The interpreter supports several other kinds of objects. Most of these support"}, {"line": 6182, "text": "only one or two operations."}, {"line": 6183, "text": ""}, {"line": 6184, "text": ""}, {"line": 6185, "text": ".. _typesmodules:"}, {"line": 6186, "text": ""}, {"line": 6187, "text": "Modules"}, {"line": 6188, "text": "-------"}, {"line": 6189, "text": ""}, {"line": 6190, "text": "The only special operation on a module is attribute access: ``m.name``, where"}, {"line": 6191, "text": "*m* is a module and *name* accesses a name defined in *m*'s symbol table."}, {"line": 6192, "text": "Module attributes can be assigned to.  (Note that the :keyword:`import`"}, {"line": 6193, "text": "statement is not, strictly speaking, an operation on a module object; ``import"}, {"line": 6194, "text": "foo`` does not require a module object named *foo* to exist, rather it requires"}, {"line": 6195, "text": "an (external) *definition* for a module named *foo* somewhere.)"}, {"line": 6196, "text": ""}, {"line": 6197, "text": "A special attribute of every module is :attr:`~object.__dict__`. This is the"}, {"line": 6198, "text": "dictionary containing the module's symbol table. Modifying this dictionary will"}, {"line": 6199, "text": "actually change the module's symbol table, but direct assignment to the"}, {"line": 6200, "text": ":attr:`~object.__dict__` attribute is not possible (you can write"}, {"line": 6201, "text": "``m.__dict__['a'] = 1``, which defines ``m.a`` to be ``1``, but you can't write"}, {"line": 6202, "text": "``m.__dict__ = {}``).  Modifying :attr:`~object.__dict__` directly is"}, {"line": 6203, "text": "not recommended."}, {"line": 6204, "text": ""}, {"line": 6205, "text": "Modules built into the interpreter are written like this: ``<module 'sys'"}, {"line": 6206, "text": "(built-in)>``.  If loaded from a file, they are written as ``<module 'os' from"}, {"line": 6207, "text": "'/usr/local/lib/pythonX.Y/os.pyc'>``."}, {"line": 6208, "text": ""}, {"line": 6209, "text": ""}, {"line": 6210, "text": ".. _typesobjects:"}, {"line": 6211, "text": ""}, {"line": 6212, "text": "Classes and Class Instances"}, {"line": 6213, "text": "---------------------------"}, {"line": 6214, "text": ""}, {"line": 6215, "text": "See :ref:`objects` and :ref:`class` for these."}, {"line": 6216, "text": ""}, {"line": 6217, "text": ""}, {"line": 6218, "text": ".. _typesfunctions:"}, {"line": 6219, "text": ""}, {"line": 6220, "text": "Functions"}, {"line": 6221, "text": "---------"}, {"line": 6222, "text": ""}, {"line": 6223, "text": "Function objects are created by function definitions.  The only operation on a"}, {"line": 6224, "text": "function object is to call it: ``func(argument-list)``."}, {"line": 6225, "text": ""}, {"line": 6226, "text": "There are really two flavors of function objects: built-in functions and"}, {"line": 6227, "text": "user-defined functions.  Both support the same operation (to call the function),"}, {"line": 6228, "text": "but the implementation is different, hence the different object types."}, {"line": 6229, "text": ""}, {"line": 6230, "text": "See :ref:`function` for more information."}, {"line": 6231, "text": ""}, {"line": 6232, "text": ""}, {"line": 6233, "text": ".. _typesmethods:"}, {"line": 6234, "text": ""}, {"line": 6235, "text": "Methods"}, {"line": 6236, "text": "-------"}, {"line": 6237, "text": ""}, {"line": 6238, "text": ".. index:: pair: object; method"}, {"line": 6239, "text": ""}, {"line": 6240, "text": "Methods are functions that are called using the attribute notation."}, {"line": 6241, "text": "There are two flavors: :ref:`built-in methods <builtin-methods>`"}, {"line": 6242, "text": "(such as :meth:`~list.append` on lists)"}, {"line": 6243, "text": "and :ref:`class instance methods <instance-methods>`."}, {"line": 6244, "text": "Built-in methods are described with the types that support them."}, {"line": 6245, "text": ""}, {"line": 6246, "text": "If you access a method (a function defined in a class namespace) through an"}, {"line": 6247, "text": "instance, you get a special object: a :dfn:`bound method` (also called"}, {"line": 6248, "text": ":ref:`instance method <instance-methods>`) object. When called, it will add"}, {"line": 6249, "text": "the ``self`` argument"}, {"line": 6250, "text": "to the argument list.  Bound methods have two special read-only attributes:"}, {"line": 6251, "text": ":attr:`m.__self__ <method.__self__>` is the object on which the method"}, {"line": 6252, "text": "operates, and :attr:`m.__func__ <method.__func__>` is"}, {"line": 6253, "text": "the function implementing the method.  Calling ``m(arg-1, arg-2, ..., arg-n)``"}, {"line": 6254, "text": "is completely equivalent to calling ``m.__func__(m.__self__, arg-1, arg-2, ...,"}, {"line": 6255, "text": "arg-n)``."}, {"line": 6256, "text": ""}, {"line": 6257, "text": "Like :ref:`function objects <user-defined-funcs>`, bound method objects support"}, {"line": 6258, "text": "getting arbitrary"}, {"line": 6259, "text": "attributes.  However, since method attributes are actually stored on the"}, {"line": 6260, "text": "underlying function object (:attr:`method.__func__`), setting method attributes on"}, {"line": 6261, "text": "bound methods is disallowed.  Attempting to set an attribute on a method"}, {"line": 6262, "text": "results in an :exc:`AttributeError` being raised.  In order to set a method"}, {"line": 6263, "text": "attribute, you need to explicitly set it on the underlying function object:"}, {"line": 6264, "text": ""}, {"line": 6265, "text": ".. doctest::"}, {"line": 6266, "text": ""}, {"line": 6267, "text": "   >>> class C:"}, {"line": 6268, "text": "   ...     def method(self):"}, {"line": 6269, "text": "   ...         pass"}, {"line": 6270, "text": "   ..."}, {"line": 6271, "text": "   >>> c = C()"}, {"line": 6272, "text": "   >>> c.method.whoami = 'my name is method'  # can't set on the method"}, {"line": 6273, "text": "   Traceback (most recent call last):"}, {"line": 6274, "text": "     File \"<stdin>\", line 1, in <module>"}, {"line": 6275, "text": "   AttributeError: 'method' object has no attribute 'whoami'"}, {"line": 6276, "text": "   >>> c.method.__func__.whoami = 'my name is method'"}, {"line": 6277, "text": "   >>> c.method.whoami"}, {"line": 6278, "text": "   'my name is method'"}, {"line": 6279, "text": ""}, {"line": 6280, "text": "See :ref:`instance-methods` for more information."}, {"line": 6281, "text": ""}, {"line": 6282, "text": ""}, {"line": 6283, "text": ".. index:: object; code, code object"}, {"line": 6284, "text": ""}, {"line": 6285, "text": ".. _bltin-code-objects:"}, {"line": 6286, "text": ""}, {"line": 6287, "text": "Code Objects"}, {"line": 6288, "text": "------------"}, {"line": 6289, "text": ""}, {"line": 6290, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 6291, "text": "   pair: built-in function; compile"}, {"line": 6292, "text": "   single: __code__ (function object attribute)"}, {"line": 6293, "text": ""}, {"line": 6294, "text": "Code objects are used by the implementation to represent \"pseudo-compiled\""}, {"line": 6295, "text": "executable Python code such as a function body. They differ from function"}, {"line": 6296, "text": "objects because they don't contain a reference to their global execution"}, {"line": 6297, "text": "environment.  Code objects are returned by the built-in :func:`compile` function"}, {"line": 6298, "text": "and can be extracted from function objects through their"}, {"line": 6299, "text": ":attr:`~function.__code__` attribute. See also the :mod:`code` module."}, {"line": 6300, "text": ""}, {"line": 6301, "text": "Accessing :attr:`~function.__code__` raises an :ref:`auditing event <auditing>`"}, {"line": 6302, "text": "``object.__getattr__`` with arguments ``obj`` and ``\"__code__\"``."}, {"line": 6303, "text": ""}, {"line": 6304, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 6305, "text": "   pair: built-in function; exec"}, {"line": 6306, "text": "   pair: built-in function; eval"}, {"line": 6307, "text": ""}, {"line": 6308, "text": "A code object can be executed or evaluated by passing it (instead of a source"}, {"line": 6309, "text": "string) to the :func:`exec` or :func:`eval`  built-in functions."}, {"line": 6310, "text": ""}, {"line": 6311, "text": "See :ref:`types` for more information."}, {"line": 6312, "text": ""}, {"line": 6313, "text": ""}, {"line": 6314, "text": ".. _bltin-type-objects:"}, {"line": 6315, "text": ""}, {"line": 6316, "text": "Type Objects"}, {"line": 6317, "text": "------------"}, {"line": 6318, "text": ""}, {"line": 6319, "text": ".. index::"}, {"line": 6320, "text": "   pair: built-in function; type"}, {"line": 6321, "text": "   pair: module; types"}, {"line": 6322, "text": ""}, {"line": 6323, "text": "Type objects represent the various object types.  An object's type is accessed"}, {"line": 6324, "text": "by the built-in function :func:`type`.  There are no special operations on"}, {"line": 6325, "text": "types.  The standard module :mod:`types` defines names for all standard built-in"}, {"line": 6326, "text": "types."}, {"line": 6327, "text": ""}, {"line": 6328, "text": "Types are written like this: ``<class 'int'>``."}, {"line": 6329, "text": ""}, {"line": 6330, "text": ""}, {"line": 6331, "text": ".. _bltin-null-object:"}, {"line": 6332, "text": ""}, {"line": 6333, "text": "The Null Object"}, {"line": 6334, "text": "---------------"}, {"line": 6335, "text": ""}, {"line": 6336, "text": "This object is returned by functions that don't explicitly return a value.  It"}, {"line": 6337, "text": "supports no special operations.  There is exactly one null object, named"}, {"line": 6338, "text": "``None`` (a built-in name).  ``type(None)()`` produces the same singleton."}, {"line": 6339, "text": ""}, {"line": 6340, "text": "It is written as ``None``."}, {"line": 6341, "text": ""}, {"line": 6342, "text": ""}, {"line": 6343, "text": ".. index:: single: ...; ellipsis literal"}, {"line": 6344, "text": ".. _bltin-ellipsis-object:"}, {"line": 6345, "text": ""}, {"line": 6346, "text": "The Ellipsis Object"}, {"line": 6347, "text": "-------------------"}, {"line": 6348, "text": ""}, {"line": 6349, "text": "This object is commonly used to indicate that something is omitted."}, {"line": 6350, "text": "It supports no special operations.  There is exactly one ellipsis object, named"}, {"line": 6351, "text": ":const:`Ellipsis` (a built-in name).  ``type(Ellipsis)()`` produces the"}, {"line": 6352, "text": ":const:`Ellipsis` singleton."}, {"line": 6353, "text": ""}, {"line": 6354, "text": "It is written as ``Ellipsis`` or ``...``."}, {"line": 6355, "text": ""}, {"line": 6356, "text": "In typical use, ``...`` as the ``Ellipsis`` object appears in a few different"}, {"line": 6357, "text": "places, for instance:"}, {"line": 6358, "text": ""}, {"line": 6359, "text": "- In type annotations, such as :ref:`callable arguments <annotating-callables>`"}, {"line": 6360, "text": "  or :ref:`tuple elements <annotating-tuples>`."}, {"line": 6361, "text": ""}, {"line": 6362, "text": "- As the body of a function instead of a :ref:`pass statement <tut-pass>`."}, {"line": 6363, "text": ""}, {"line": 6364, "text": "- In third-party libraries, such as `Numpy's slicing and striding"}, {"line": 6365, "text": "  <https://numpy.org/doc/stable/user/basics.indexing.html#slicing-and-striding>`_."}, {"line": 6366, "text": ""}, {"line": 6367, "text": "Python also uses three dots in ways that are not ``Ellipsis`` objects, for instance:"}, {"line": 6368, "text": ""}, {"line": 6369, "text": "- Doctest's :const:`ELLIPSIS <doctest.ELLIPSIS>`, as a pattern for missing content."}, {"line": 6370, "text": ""}, {"line": 6371, "text": "- The default Python prompt of the :term:`interactive` shell when partial input is incomplete."}, {"line": 6372, "text": ""}, {"line": 6373, "text": "Lastly, the Python documentation often uses three dots in conventional English"}, {"line": 6374, "text": "usage to mean omitted content, even in code examples that also use them as the"}, {"line": 6375, "text": "``Ellipsis``."}, {"line": 6376, "text": ""}, {"line": 6377, "text": ""}, {"line": 6378, "text": ".. _bltin-notimplemented-object:"}, {"line": 6379, "text": ""}, {"line": 6380, "text": "The NotImplemented Object"}, {"line": 6381, "text": "-------------------------"}, {"line": 6382, "text": ""}, {"line": 6383, "text": "This object is returned from comparisons and binary operations when they are"}, {"line": 6384, "text": "asked to operate on types they don't support. See :ref:`comparisons` for more"}, {"line": 6385, "text": "information.  There is exactly one :data:`NotImplemented` object."}, {"line": 6386, "text": ":code:`type(NotImplemented)()` produces the singleton instance."}, {"line": 6387, "text": ""}, {"line": 6388, "text": "It is written as :code:`NotImplemented`."}, {"line": 6389, "text": ""}, {"line": 6390, "text": ""}, {"line": 6391, "text": ".. _typesinternal:"}, {"line": 6392, "text": ""}, {"line": 6393, "text": "Internal Objects"}, {"line": 6394, "text": "----------------"}, {"line": 6395, "text": ""}, {"line": 6396, "text": "See :ref:`types` for this information.  It describes"}, {"line": 6397, "text": ":ref:`stack frame objects <frame-objects>`,"}, {"line": 6398, "text": ":ref:`traceback objects <traceback-objects>`, and slice objects."}, {"line": 6399, "text": ""}, {"line": 6400, "text": ""}, {"line": 6401, "text": ".. _specialattrs:"}, {"line": 6402, "text": ""}, {"line": 6403, "text": "Special Attributes"}, {"line": 6404, "text": "=================="}, {"line": 6405, "text": ""}, {"line": 6406, "text": "The implementation adds a few special read-only attributes to several object"}, {"line": 6407, "text": "types, where they are relevant.  Some of these are not reported by the"}, {"line": 6408, "text": ":func:`dir` built-in function."}, {"line": 6409, "text": ""}, {"line": 6410, "text": ""}, {"line": 6411, "text": ".. attribute:: definition.__name__"}, {"line": 6412, "text": ""}, {"line": 6413, "text": "   The name of the class, function, method, descriptor, or"}, {"line": 6414, "text": "   generator instance."}, {"line": 6415, "text": ""}, {"line": 6416, "text": ""}, {"line": 6417, "text": ".. attribute:: definition.__qualname__"}, {"line": 6418, "text": ""}, {"line": 6419, "text": "   The :term:`qualified name` of the class, function, method, descriptor,"}, {"line": 6420, "text": "   or generator instance."}, {"line": 6421, "text": ""}, {"line": 6422, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.3"}, {"line": 6423, "text": ""}, {"line": 6424, "text": ""}, {"line": 6425, "text": ".. attribute:: definition.__module__"}, {"line": 6426, "text": ""}, {"line": 6427, "text": "   The name of the module in which a class or function was defined."}, {"line": 6428, "text": ""}, {"line": 6429, "text": ""}, {"line": 6430, "text": ".. attribute:: definition.__doc__"}, {"line": 6431, "text": ""}, {"line": 6432, "text": "   The documentation string of a class or function, or ``None`` if undefined."}, {"line": 6433, "text": ""}, {"line": 6434, "text": ""}, {"line": 6435, "text": ".. attribute:: definition.__type_params__"}, {"line": 6436, "text": ""}, {"line": 6437, "text": "   The :ref:`type parameters <type-params>` of generic classes, functions,"}, {"line": 6438, "text": "   and :ref:`type aliases <type-aliases>`. For classes and functions that"}, {"line": 6439, "text": "   are not generic, this will be an empty tuple."}, {"line": 6440, "text": ""}, {"line": 6441, "text": "   .. versionadded:: 3.12"}, {"line": 6442, "text": ""}, {"line": 6443, "text": ""}, {"line": 6444, "text": ".. _int_max_str_digits:"}, {"line": 6445, "text": ""}, {"line": 6446, "text": "Integer string conversion length limitation"}, {"line": 6447, "text": "==========================================="}, {"line": 6448, "text": ""}, {"line": 6449, "text": "CPython has a global limit for converting between :class:`int` and :class:`str`"}, {"line": 6450, "text": "to mitigate denial of service attacks. This limit *only* applies to decimal or"}, {"line": 6451, "text": "other non-power-of-two number bases. Hexadecimal, octal, and binary conversions"}, {"line": 6452, "text": "are unlimited. The limit can be configured."}, {"line": 6453, "text": ""}, {"line": 6454, "text": "The :class:`int` type in CPython is an arbitrary length number stored in binary"}, {"line": 6455, "text": "form (commonly known as a \"bignum\"). There exists no algorithm that can convert"}, {"line": 6456, "text": "a string to a binary integer or a binary integer to a string in linear time,"}, {"line": 6457, "text": "*unless* the base is a power of 2. Even the best known algorithms for base 10"}, {"line": 6458, "text": "have sub-quadratic complexity. Converting a large value such as ``int('1' *"}, {"line": 6459, "text": "500_000)`` can take over a second on a fast CPU."}, {"line": 6460, "text": ""}, {"line": 6461, "text": "Limiting conversion size offers a practical way to avoid :cve:`2020-10735`."}, {"line": 6462, "text": ""}, {"line": 6463, "text": "The limit is applied to the number of digit characters in the input or output"}, {"line": 6464, "text": "string when a non-linear conversion algorithm would be involved.  Underscores"}, {"line": 6465, "text": "and the sign are not counted towards the limit."}, {"line": 6466, "text": ""}, {"line": 6467, "text": "When an operation would exceed the limit, a :exc:`ValueError` is raised:"}, {"line": 6468, "text": ""}, {"line": 6469, "text": ".. doctest::"}, {"line": 6470, "text": ""}, {"line": 6471, "text": "   >>> import sys"}, {"line": 6472, "text": "   >>> sys.set_int_max_str_digits(4300)  # Illustrative, this is the default."}, {"line": 6473, "text": "   >>> _ = int('2' * 5432)"}, {"line": 6474, "text": "   Traceback (most recent call last):"}, {"line": 6475, "text": "   ..."}, {"line": 6476, "text": "   ValueError: Exceeds the limit (4300 digits) for integer string conversion: value has 5432 digits; use sys.set_int_max_str_digits() to increase the limit"}, {"line": 6477, "text": "   >>> i = int('2' * 4300)"}, {"line": 6478, "text": "   >>> len(str(i))"}, {"line": 6479, "text": "   4300"}, {"line": 6480, "text": "   >>> i_squared = i*i"}, {"line": 6481, "text": "   >>> len(str(i_squared))"}, {"line": 6482, "text": "   Traceback (most recent call last):"}, {"line": 6483, "text": "   ..."}, {"line": 6484, "text": "   ValueError: Exceeds the limit (4300 digits) for integer string conversion; use sys.set_int_max_str_digits() to increase the limit"}, {"line": 6485, "text": "   >>> len(hex(i_squared))"}, {"line": 6486, "text": "   7144"}, {"line": 6487, "text": "   >>> assert int(hex(i_squared), base=16) == i*i  # Hexadecimal is unlimited."}, {"line": 6488, "text": ""}, {"line": 6489, "text": "The default limit is 4300 digits as provided in"}, {"line": 6490, "text": ":data:`sys.int_info.default_max_str_digits <sys.int_info>`."}, {"line": 6491, "text": "The lowest limit that can be configured is 640 digits as provided in"}, {"line": 6492, "text": ":data:`sys.int_info.str_digits_check_threshold <sys.int_info>`."}, {"line": 6493, "text": ""}, {"line": 6494, "text": "Verification:"}, {"line": 6495, "text": ""}, {"line": 6496, "text": ".. doctest::"}, {"line": 6497, "text": ""}, {"line": 6498, "text": "   >>> import sys"}, {"line": 6499, "text": "   >>> assert sys.int_info.default_max_str_digits == 4300, sys.int_info"}, {"line": 6500, "text": "   >>> assert sys.int_info.str_digits_check_threshold == 640, sys.int_info"}, {"line": 6501, "text": "   >>> msg = int('578966293710682886880994035146873798396722250538762761564'"}, {"line": 6502, "text": "   ...           '9252925514383915483333812743580549779436104706260696366600'"}, {"line": 6503, "text": "   ...           '571186405732').to_bytes(53, 'big')"}, {"line": 6504, "text": "   ..."}, {"line": 6505, "text": ""}, {"line": 6506, "text": ".. versionadded:: 3.11"}, {"line": 6507, "text": ""}, {"line": 6508, "text": "Affected APIs"}, {"line": 6509, "text": "-------------"}, {"line": 6510, "text": ""}, {"line": 6511, "text": "The limitation only applies to potentially slow conversions between :class:`int`"}, {"line": 6512, "text": "and :class:`str` or :class:`bytes`:"}, {"line": 6513, "text": ""}, {"line": 6514, "text": "* ``int(string)`` with default base 10."}, {"line": 6515, "text": "* ``int(string, base)`` for all bases that are not a power of 2."}, {"line": 6516, "text": "* ``str(integer)``."}, {"line": 6517, "text": "* ``repr(integer)``."}, {"line": 6518, "text": "* any other string conversion to base 10, for example ``f\"{integer}\"``,"}, {"line": 6519, "text": "  ``\"{}\".format(integer)``, or ``b\"%d\" % integer``."}, {"line": 6520, "text": ""}, {"line": 6521, "text": "The limitations do not apply to functions with a linear algorithm:"}, {"line": 6522, "text": ""}, {"line": 6523, "text": "* ``int(string, base)`` with base 2, 4, 8, 16, or 32."}, {"line": 6524, "text": "* :func:`int.from_bytes` and :func:`int.to_bytes`."}, {"line": 6525, "text": "* :func:`hex`, :func:`oct`, :func:`bin`."}, {"line": 6526, "text": "* :ref:`formatspec` for hex, octal, and binary numbers."}, {"line": 6527, "text": "* :class:`str` to :class:`float`."}, {"line": 6528, "text": "* :class:`str` to :class:`decimal.Decimal`."}, {"line": 6529, "text": ""}, {"line": 6530, "text": "Configuring the limit"}, {"line": 6531, "text": "---------------------"}, {"line": 6532, "text": ""}, {"line": 6533, "text": "Before Python starts up you can use an environment variable or an interpreter"}, {"line": 6534, "text": "command line flag to configure the limit:"}, {"line": 6535, "text": ""}, {"line": 6536, "text": "* :envvar:`PYTHONINTMAXSTRDIGITS`, e.g."}, {"line": 6537, "text": "  ``PYTHONINTMAXSTRDIGITS=640 python3`` to set the limit to 640 or"}, {"line": 6538, "text": "  ``PYTHONINTMAXSTRDIGITS=0 python3`` to disable the limitation."}, {"line": 6539, "text": "* :option:`-X int_max_str_digits <-X>`, e.g."}, {"line": 6540, "text": "  ``python3 -X int_max_str_digits=640``"}, {"line": 6541, "text": "* :data:`sys.flags.int_max_str_digits` contains the value of"}, {"line": 6542, "text": "  :envvar:`PYTHONINTMAXSTRDIGITS` or :option:`-X int_max_str_digits <-X>`."}, {"line": 6543, "text": "  If both the env var and the ``-X`` option are set, the ``-X`` option takes"}, {"line": 6544, "text": "  precedence. A value of *-1* indicates that both were unset, thus a value of"}, {"line": 6545, "text": "  :data:`sys.int_info.default_max_str_digits` was used during initialization."}, {"line": 6546, "text": ""}, {"line": 6547, "text": "From code, you can inspect the current limit and set a new one using these"}, {"line": 6548, "text": ":mod:`sys` APIs:"}, {"line": 6549, "text": ""}, {"line": 6550, "text": "* :func:`sys.get_int_max_str_digits` and :func:`sys.set_int_max_str_digits` are"}, {"line": 6551, "text": "  a getter and setter for the interpreter-wide limit. Subinterpreters have"}, {"line": 6552, "text": "  their own limit."}, {"line": 6553, "text": ""}, {"line": 6554, "text": "Information about the default and minimum can be found in :data:`sys.int_info`:"}, {"line": 6555, "text": ""}, {"line": 6556, "text": "* :data:`sys.int_info.default_max_str_digits <sys.int_info>` is the compiled-in"}, {"line": 6557, "text": "  default limit."}, {"line": 6558, "text": "* :data:`sys.int_info.str_digits_check_threshold <sys.int_info>` is the lowest"}, {"line": 6559, "text": "  accepted value for the limit (other than 0 which disables it)."}, {"line": 6560, "text": ""}, {"line": 6561, "text": ".. versionadded:: 3.11"}, {"line": 6562, "text": ""}, {"line": 6563, "text": ".. caution::"}, {"line": 6564, "text": ""}, {"line": 6565, "text": "   Setting a low limit *can* lead to problems. While rare, code exists that"}, {"line": 6566, "text": "   contains integer constants in decimal in their source that exceed the"}, {"line": 6567, "text": "   minimum threshold. A consequence of setting the limit is that Python source"}, {"line": 6568, "text": "   code containing decimal integer literals longer than the limit will"}, {"line": 6569, "text": "   encounter an error during parsing, usually at startup time or import time or"}, {"line": 6570, "text": "   even at installation time - anytime an up to date ``.pyc`` does not already"}, {"line": 6571, "text": "   exist for the code. A workaround for source that contains such large"}, {"line": 6572, "text": "   constants is to convert them to ``0x`` hexadecimal form as it has no limit."}, {"line": 6573, "text": ""}, {"line": 6574, "text": "   Test your application thoroughly if you use a low limit. Ensure your tests"}, {"line": 6575, "text": "   run with the limit set early via the environment or flag so that it applies"}, {"line": 6576, "text": "   during startup and even during any installation step that may invoke Python"}, {"line": 6577, "text": "   to precompile ``.py`` sources to ``.pyc`` files."}, {"line": 6578, "text": ""}, {"line": 6579, "text": "Recommended configuration"}, {"line": 6580, "text": "-------------------------"}, {"line": 6581, "text": ""}, {"line": 6582, "text": "The default :data:`sys.int_info.default_max_str_digits` is expected to be"}, {"line": 6583, "text": "reasonable for most applications. If your application requires a different"}, {"line": 6584, "text": "limit, set it from your main entry point using Python version agnostic code as"}, {"line": 6585, "text": "these APIs were added in security patch releases in versions before 3.12."}, {"line": 6586, "text": ""}, {"line": 6587, "text": "Example::"}, {"line": 6588, "text": ""}, {"line": 6589, "text": "   >>> import sys"}, {"line": 6590, "text": "   >>> if hasattr(sys, \"set_int_max_str_digits\"):"}, {"line": 6591, "text": "   ...     upper_bound = 68000"}, {"line": 6592, "text": "   ...     lower_bound = 4004"}, {"line": 6593, "text": "   ...     current_limit = sys.get_int_max_str_digits()"}, {"line": 6594, "text": "   ...     if current_limit == 0 or current_limit > upper_bound:"}, {"line": 6595, "text": "   ...         sys.set_int_max_str_digits(upper_bound)"}, {"line": 6596, "text": "   ...     elif current_limit < lower_bound:"}, {"line": 6597, "text": "   ...         sys.set_int_max_str_digits(lower_bound)"}, {"line": 6598, "text": ""}, {"line": 6599, "text": "If you need to disable it entirely, set it to ``0``."}, {"line": 6600, "text": ""}, {"line": 6601, "text": ""}, {"line": 6602, "text": ".. rubric:: Footnotes"}, {"line": 6603, "text": ""}, {"line": 6604, "text": ".. [1] Additional information on these special methods may be found in the Python"}, {"line": 6605, "text": "   Reference Manual (:ref:`customization`)."}, {"line": 6606, "text": ""}, {"line": 6607, "text": ".. [2] As a consequence, the list ``[1, 2]`` is considered equal to ``[1.0, 2.0]``, and"}, {"line": 6608, "text": "   similarly for tuples."}, {"line": 6609, "text": ""}, {"line": 6610, "text": ".. [3] They must have since the parser can't tell the type of the operands."}, {"line": 6611, "text": ""}, {"line": 6612, "text": ".. [4] Cased characters are those with general category property being one of"}, {"line": 6613, "text": "   \"Lu\" (Letter, uppercase), \"Ll\" (Letter, lowercase), or \"Lt\" (Letter, titlecase)."}, {"line": 6614, "text": ""}, {"line": 6615, "text": ".. [5] To format only a tuple you should therefore provide a singleton tuple whose only"}, {"line": 6616, "text": "   element is the tuple to be formatted."}]}
